## Snapshot
Fabless designer of x86 CPUs, GPUs and adaptive/embedded silicon, fabbed at TSMC (2nm/5nm) with some GF, UMC, Samsung capacity. Four segments: **Data Center** (EPYC server CPUs + Instinct AI GPUs — ~56% of revenue and the entire growth story), **Client & Gaming** (Ryzen PCs + Radeon + semi-custom consoles), and **Embedded** (Xilinx FPGA + adaptive SoC, highest-margin business at ~38-40% segment OM). Competes head-to-head with Intel (x86), Nvidia (AI GPU), Arm-based merchant/custom CPUs, and the custom-ASIC complex (Broadcom/Marvell). The thesis is fully an AI-infrastructure story: AMD is the credible #2 merchant GPU and the share-gaining merchant server CPU into an agentic-AI demand pull (10-K FY25, 2026-02-04).

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) EPYC server CPUs (Data Center) · 2) Instinct AI GPUs (MI300/MI350/MI450) · 3) Ryzen client PC CPUs |
| **Main buyer(s)** | Hyperscalers / AI labs for GPUs (OpenAI ~6GW, Meta ~6GW — concentration + warrant dilution risk); PC OEMs (Ryzen) and console makers (semi-custom) |
| **Key suppliers** | TSMC foundry 2nm/5nm + some GF/UMC/Samsung; CoWoS-L advanced packaging; HBM/DRAM (SK Hynix, Micron, Samsung); ASE packaging (FoCoS) |

Those details give us greater confidence that AMD's scale-up roadmap has MOVED BEYOND SIMPLY ADOPTING THE UALINK STANDARD toward building the hardware transport, memory semantics, management and RAS stack required to operate Ethernet as A TRUE SCALE-UP FABRIC."*** ⚠️⚠️ **THIS IS THE DIRECT COUNTER TO THE STANDING BEAR ITEM ON THIS PAGE: the SemiAnalysis blast-radius/RAS work already logged here (and the GB200 NVL72 base-rate note on [[NVDA]] — MTBI 1,000-3,000 GPU-days, XID 149, 8-24h backplane swaps) argued that rack-scale reliability, not FLOPs, is what separates a demo from a deployment. AMD has now published a switch-tray-failure survival story. It is a VENDOR CLAIM at a conference, with no field MTBI data and no third-party validation — but it is the first time AMD has argued on this axis at all.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-26 — NVIDIA PUT A GROWTH RATE ON ITS SERVER-CPU AMBITION FOR THE FIRST TIME, AND IT IS AIMED AT THE SOCKET THIS PAGE'S EPYC THESIS OWNS.** (NVIDIA Q2 FY27 earnings call, 2026-08-26 — company primary. **No AMD rating, PT or estimate; AMD is not mentioned by name.**) Colette Kress: *"Today, we are in **FULL PRODUCTION of our next generation Vera CPU as a standalone product**… Vera completes a task **1.8x faster on the SPEC benchmark and provides FIVE TIMES the bandwidth per watt than any other data center CPU**. We expect Vera to be deployed by **every major hyperscaler, neocloud, AI lab and system OEM**, with shipments already underway to our lead partners including OCI, [[SPCX]] SpaceX, and starting this quarter, [[AMZN]] AWS."* And the sizing: *"We continue to see demand for **approximately $20 BILLION in total server CPUs** and based on our customer demand and improving supply outlook, our preliminary expectation is for **CPU REVENUE TO MORE THAN DOUBLE IN FISCAL 28**, positioning us as **one of the world's leading server CPU suppliers**."* The base it grows from is disclosed: **Grace CPU trailing-twelve-month revenue now exceeds $5BN.**

➜ **WHAT IS ACTUALLY CHECKABLE HERE, AND IT IS THE SMALLER HALF: ">$5bn TTM more than doubling in FY28" is a concrete claim about ~$10bn+ of NVIDIA server-CPU revenue. The "$20bn in total server CPUs" figure is NOT — this wiki already carries UBS/Arcuri cutting that same number to ~$5BN "APPLES-TO-APPLES" CHIP EQUIVALENT, with the balance being systems plus DRAM (logged on [[NVDA]]). Management repeated the $20bn framing on this call WITHOUT the decomposition, so the two remain unreconciled and the larger number should not be treated as a merchant-CPU TAM that AMD is losing.**

⚠️ **THE STRUCTURAL POINT FOR THIS PAGE IS NOT UNIT SHARE, IT IS ATTACH. Vera is being sold as the head-end of a rack that also contains Rubin GPUs and NVIDIA networking — NVIDIA's own revenue-per-GW ladder attributes the step from ~$25bn (Grace Blackwell) to ~$40bn (Vera Rubin) partly to CPU content coming in-house. That is an attach-rate displacement inside NVIDIA racks, which is a different exposure from AMD losing general-purpose EPYC sockets, and Joe Moore's framing already on the [[NVDA]] page — that agentic CPU is *"a new vector, not GPU-correlated"* — argues the same. ⚠️ It is also the exact socket the [[OPENAI]] Jalapeño bill of materials fills with AMD: 2× Turin-class EPYC and 1.5TB of DRAM per host tray, 16 host trays per rack (SemiAnalysis, 08-25, already on this page). So the head-end CPU is being contested from both directions in the same week — NVIDIA moving in with Vera, and the largest non-NVIDIA accelerator programme designing AMD in.**

Our forecast is **broadly consistent with [[NVDA]]'s $200B long-term framework and BELOW AMD's $220B estimate**, which our model can reach under a more aggressive scenario tied to AMD's **~$1.4T accelerator TAM** and higher agent adoption, concurrency and tool usage"* (Raymond James, via @wallstengine, corpus, 2026-08-25). TMTB adds the decomposition: **~$85bn of agentic CPUs and ~$83bn of AI head-end CPUs inside the ~$201bn**, with AMD offering *"the best combination of direct earnings leverage, datacenter positioning and potential share gains."* **AMD +5% on the day.** ⚠️ **A CY30 TAM construction, not a near-term estimate change.** 🔴 **The independent corroboration for the thesis arrived at Hot Chips the same week from [[NVDA]] itself: NVDA designed Vera around the view that "agentic AI makes the CPU a critical part of the inference loop again" — a single agent task chains "20, 50 or more LLM calls" — which is precisely the demand vector RJ is underwriting for EPYC.**

**🆕 2026-08-25 — HELIOS AT HOT CHIPS: THE ARCHITECTURE IS DISCLOSED, AND SO IS THE ADMISSION THAT IT IS THE END OF THE ELECTRICAL ROAD.** (AMD · Steve Scott, David and Krishna; recapped by TMT Breakout, 2026-08-25)
- **Configuration:** **72 MI455 GPUs in 18 nodes, each node 1 EPYC + 4 MI455.** *"The rack delivers almost **1.7 PB/s of aggregate HBM bandwidth**, more than **0.25 PB/s per direction across the scale-up network** and over **40 TB/s of scale-out bandwidth**."* One CPU per four GPUs *"provides the best price/performance and enough CPU throughput to keep the accelerators fed."*
- **Scale-up over Ethernet:** MI455 exposes **direct loads, stores and atomics across the pod**, packaged into Ethernet frames and routed through **[[AVGO]] Tomahawk 6 switches with at most ONE switch hop to any GPU memory in the rack.** AMD distinguishes this from **UALink** — both carry memory transactions, but AMD packetises into Ethernet with end-to-end retransmission while UALink uses hop-by-hop.
- 🔴 **"AMD said Helios is near the practical limit for an ALL-ELECTRICAL scale-up fabric… AMD expects to begin introducing OPTICAL SCALE-UP in the following generation, which should loosen the coupling between compute topology and rack layout."** ➤ **A named vendor putting a generation stamp on the copper→optical scale-up transition — read straight through to `themes/optical-cpo`, [[LITE]], [[COHR]], [[CRDO]].**
- **Cooling/density:** **>85% of Helios heat removed by direct liquid cooling**, and *"asked how rack power evolves, AMD said… **it does not expect a substantial increase in power density from current levels**"* — because optical scale-up removes the need to keep pushing density to preserve signal reach. **Networking: up to ~2.4 Tb/s of scale-out per GPU via multiple 800G Pensando AI NICs**, deliberately programmable rather than fixed-function *"because AI transports, congestion-control methods and telemetry requirements are changing too quickly."*

**🆕 2026-08-25 — ON MEMORY, AMD IS EXPLICITLY THE FLEXIBLE BUYER.** *"I sent out notes on AMD yesterday — **Lisa Su said AMD would be FLEXIBLE ON HBM CONFIGURATIONS for clients based on specific workloads**"* (Jefferies Asia TMT sales · Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-25). ⚠️ **Logged inside the [[NVDA]] de-spec debate: the same behaviour NVDA is being criticised for is framed by AMD as a customer feature. It is a positioning difference, not necessarily an economic one.**

**🆕 2026-08-25 — AND A SECOND SOCKET, FROM AN UNEXPECTED DIRECTION:** [[OPENAI]]'s Jalapeño rack pairs each ASIC tray with a host tray carrying **2× AMD Turin-class EPYC CPUs and 1.5TB of DRAM — 16 host trays per rack** (SemiAnalysis, 2026-08-25). 🔴 **The head-end CPU attach that Raymond James is underwriting shows up in the bill of materials of the most-watched non-NVDA accelerator programme in the industry.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-23 — SEMIANALYSIS RE-RUNS THE INFERENCE BENCHMARK ON *AGENTIC* WORKLOADS AND THE ANSWER FLIPS TWICE IN TWO WEEKS: MI355X HAD CAUGHT B200 vLLM ON PERF/$ — AND THEN LOST IT AGAIN ON 21-AUG.** (SemiAnalysis · Cam Quilici, Bryan Shan, Alec Ibarra, Daniel Nishball, Zane Fong, Kimbo Chen and **Dylan Patel** — "AgentX - InferenceXv3: Does CUDA Moat Hold up in Agentic Inferencing?", 2026-08-23; received via the daniel.grozdea desk forward. **Independent research shop — NOT broker research, no rating, no PT, no estimate.**) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis.html)
- **What changed in the TEST, which is why this supersedes the 08-09 read rather than repeating it:** SemiAnalysis released **AgentX 1.0 — "the world's first fully open source, multi-turn agentic coding inference benchmark at 1 million context, released under Apache 2.0"**, on a **$3mn dataset open-sourced**, with **95%+ KV-cache hit rate**, run across **>1,000 chips** spanning **MI355X, GB300 NVL72, GB200 NVL72, B300, B200, MI325, MI300X, H200 and RTX Pro Servers**. It explicitly replaces *"the previous single-turn traffic of 8k input and 1k output tokens."* ➜ **The 08-09 TileRT number already on this page was batch-size-1 GLM-5.1 decode — the most hostile single operating point. This is the opposite end: realistic long-context multi-turn. Both now sit on the page; neither is the whole picture.**
- 🔴 **THE HEADLINE SEQUENCE, AND THE DATES MATTER BECAUSE THE LEAD CHANGED HANDS INSIDE THE MEASUREMENT WINDOW: _"Before August 21, 2026, AMD's MI355X strong SGLang development team was matching B200 vLLM on performance per dollar on end to end (e2e) performance."_ ⚠️ But even then _"B300 vLLM and B200 SGLang still beat AMD's MI355X."_ Then: _"After August 21, 2026, due to optimizations in vLLM from Inferact and Nvidia, the performance per dollar of Nvidia's B200 has surpassed that of the MI355X."_** SemiAnalysis's own framing is **_"This is a close race and we are excited to see the performance optimizations over the next couple weeks"_**, and it notes **AMD has published a DeepSeekv4 vLLM optimization list "and includes lots of exciting things that they can do to improve their performance."** ➜ **Read it as the strongest AMD software datapoint this page has carried — parity on perf/$ against B200 vLLM was reached — immediately followed by the reason it is not yet bankable: the parity lasted days, and it was never against NVIDIA's best config.**
- 🔴 **THE STRUCTURAL PART, WHICH IS THE ACTUAL ANSWER TO THE TITLE'S QUESTION AND IS THE MOST BEARISH LINE FOR AMD: on decode/prefill context parallelism (DCP/PCP), _"DCP/PCP forms part of CUDA moat as the AMD implementation of DCP/PCP isn't optimized yet. In the vLLM support matrix, EVERY SINGLE AMD BACKEND IS UNSUPPORTED."_** The technique was **"invented partially by Nvidia Research."** ➜ **This is a capability gap, not a tuning gap, and it sits exactly where agentic workloads are hardest (giant-prompt prefill and memory-BW-bound decode over long KV). It is the cleanest statement on this wiki of the CUDA moat being *architecturally* rather than merely *ecosystem*-located.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE OPERATIONAL GAP, WHICH IS WORSE THAN THE PERF/$ GAP: _"MI355X vLLM worked out of the box on day 0 for short context single turn workloads, but for long context multi turn workloads, MI355X AITER and vLLM was completely unusable for MI355X on realistic workloads."_** ➜ **Day-0 support on the benchmark that no longer reflects production traffic; unusable on the one that does. That is the single most important sentence in the note for the MI450/Helios debate on this page.**
- **WHERE MI355X *DOES* WIN, stated fairly:** *"On e2e latency, ATOM MI355X beats B200 vLLM"* — ⚠️ **but not B300 or B200 SGLang, and SemiAnalysis immediately discounts it: _"most AI labs in China or the west do not want to use ATOM in production besides 1 small advertising business unit at Alibaba Corp due to tons of missing features. The main Qwen LLM org at baba does not use ATOM in production."_** Separately, on **Kimi K3**, B200 *"was unable to use speculative decoding"* so its performance *"was horrible and was getting mogged by MI355X"* — a real but model-specific and transient win.
- **THE HARDWARE READ, AND IT POINTS AT AMD'S OWN UNDER-USED ADVANTAGE:** *"Overall, MI355X performs decently well compared to its main competitors B200 and B300. Performance is most comparable at the lower throughput / [interactivity]"* end, and SemiAnalysis wants **better kernels on MI355X "to be more competitive in the high throughput scenarios, ESPECIALLY GIVEN THE 1.5x HBM OVER B200."** ➜ **AMD's memory advantage is real and is not being converted by the software stack — the same conclusion, from a new direction, as the 08-09 entry.**
- **Demand context the note establishes for the whole benchmark, worth carrying on the theme page:** *"Since the Claude Code inflection point in November 2025, long-context, multi-turn agentic workloads have grown rapidly. They now dominate traffic for production inferencing. In April 2026, OpenAI's Enterprise agentic spending overtook ChatGPT spending."* (SemiAnalysis, 2026-08-23)

**🆕🔴 2026-08-19 — TWO SEPARATE AI-ACCELERATOR DATAPOINTS LANDED ON THE SAME DAY, AND ONLY ONE OF THEM IS CONFIRMED.**

**🆕 2026-08-14 — THE SOFTWARE-ECOSYSTEM DATAPOINT THE ROCm DEBATE HAS BEEN MISSING, AND IT COMES FROM THE PLATFORM ITSELF RATHER THAN FROM AMD: AMD AND [[NVDA]] ARE THE TWO ORGANISATIONS PUBLISHING THE MOST NEW OPEN MODELS IN THE WORLD THIS YEAR.**
- **"The two organizations publishing the most new open models this year are also the companies making the hardware: AMD and NVIDIA. Each released more than 200 new model repositories, far ahead of the rest of the field, with LiquidAI ranking third at around 100. Hardware vendors have realized that open models are a way to sell chips"** (Hugging Face · Adina Yakefu / Apolinário / Irene Solaiman, "State of Open Models: Summer 2026 Observations", 2026-08-14). On AMD's contribution specifically: **"AMD contributed many conversions… it enables trillion-parameter models to run efficiently on U.S. hardware."**
- ➤ **WHY THIS MATTERS FOR THE ROCm/ADOPTION DEBATE THIS PAGE TRACKS, AND WHY IT IS NOT A REVENUE DATAPOINT.** The page's ROCm evidence has so far been either AMD's own (rocm.ai, six-week cadence, the 38% MiniMax M3 gain, the multi-year Claude co-development) or a customer's ([[ANTHROPIC]]'s Tom Brown on bootstrapping MI355). **This is third-party, quantitative and comparative: on the single metric of open-model publishing volume, AMD is level with NVDA and an order of magnitude ahead of everyone else** — evidence that the "make the models run on our silicon" motion is being executed at scale, not announced. **The conversions point is the operative one: converting other people's frontier models (including trillion-parameter MoEs) to run on AMD hardware is exactly the CUDA-moat-erosion mechanism the 07-30 StoneX expert call priced at a "15 to 20% overhead in GPU memory" — conversions at volume are how that overhead gets amortised across the ecosystem rather than paid per-customer.**
- ⚠️ **The counterweights, from the same source, both of which cut against reading this as demand:** the Hub's distribution is brutally concentrated — **~85.6% of models have fewer than 200 lifetime downloads and 1.5% of repositories account for 99.2% of all downloads** — so repository COUNT is a supply metric, not an adoption metric; and **only 3% of all 2026 download volume goes to models above 70B**, with **models under 1B taking 83% of all-time downloads.** ➤ **Net: a real, third-party-verified ecosystem-moat datapoint and a legitimate answer to "is ROCm adoption actually happening" — but it must never be carried as a revenue or share signal, and repository count must never be presented as usage.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-14/15 — THREE ITEMS, AND THE ONE THAT MATTERS IS THE DISTRIBUTION PROBLEM: THE ONLY CLOUD WHERE AMD'S ACCELERATORS ARE PUBLICLY AVAILABLE ON DEMAND IS ORACLE.**
- 🔴 **From the one tracker that observes this directly:** *"**Our data set shows that AMD's MI300X/MI355X on-demand instances are ONLY AVAILABLE AT ORACLE.**"* And in the same screen: *"**Trainium and Inferentia appear less competitive on raw compute economics**"* while **NVDA "screens most attractive on a simple $ per hour per PFLOP basis" with TPU the closest alternative — AMD does not appear in the economics ranking at all, because there is not enough public pricing to rank it.** (UBS Global Research · Timothy Arcuri / Natalia Winkler / Seth Cho + **UBS Evidence Lab**, "Cloud Accelerator Study — Aug'26," 2026-08-14.) ➜ **This is a distribution datapoint, not a silicon one, and it is the most concrete version of the "AMD's problem is deployment, not the part" argument this page already carries. Six vendors offer B200 on demand and three offer B300; AMD has one. ⚠️ Caveat that limits it: the tracker measures PUBLIC on-demand and spot listings — large reserved/allocated deals (including the [[OPENAI]] arrangement) would not show up here at all. So this bounds AMD's MERCHANT-CLOUD footprint, not its total deployment.**
- **NET-NEW M&A: AMD has acquired TAALAS** (referenced in UBS's SemiBytes Weekly, via UBS Sales · Ruple, 2026-08-15). ⚠️ **The relay carries only the fact of the acquisition — no terms, no rationale, no size. Flagged to pull the SemiBytes note and confirm before the next pass.**

Logged as a flag to check the 8-K/prospectus, not as a fact set. Relevant because it lands in the same fortnight as [[INTC]]'s $20B+ raise and [[NVDA]]'s $500bn financing platform — the whole complex is raising, and this page's standing question is what AMD funds with it (capacity prepayments? the OpenAI-related build? M&A such as Taalas?).**
- **Positioning, which has moved the wrong way for a long:** *"**AMD fundamentals remain strong, though EXPECTATIONS HAVE BECOME INCREASINGLY ELEVATED, with many already modelling $30 EPS FOR THE OUT YEAR**"* (UBS Sector Sales · Ruple, 2026-08-15 — *not* UBS Research). **AMD sits on UBS's semis LONG-BIASED list.** ➜ **"$30 out-year EPS is already in the buy-side model" is the specific bar to carry into the next print — and it should be read against the MI455/Helios slip to 1Q27 and the 10-15k rack 2027 volume immediately below. A consensus that has already underwritten $30 while the flagship rack slips a quarter is the definition of an asymmetric setup.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-11 — MI455 / HELIOS MASS PRODUCTION PUSHED TO 1Q27 ON RACK-LEVEL TECHNICAL PROBLEMS, AND THE 2027 VOLUME NUMBER IS 10–15K RACKS — a fraction of what one ODM has been marketing (Fubon / Titan Cheng, relayed by Jefferies / Conor O'Mara and Rankie Wong, 2026-08-11).**
- **The push-out, verbatim:** *"Currently **ODMs are facing many technical challenges with the Helios 72-GPU configuration racks** — thus, we now expect **mass production of MI455 to only take place in 1Q27E (vs previous expects of small volumes in 4Q26E)**."* ➜ **A one-quarter slip, and the stated cause is rack integration, not silicon.**
- **🔴 The volume, and the discrepancy that makes it worth logging:** *"We forecast a total of **10–15k MI455 Helios racks to be shipped in 2027E**, with **Hon Hai expected to ship 3k**"* — Hon Hai's share is small because *"Helios is not a primary project for Hon Hai currently."* ⚠️ **"AVC has been talking about 30K racks. AVC reports tomorrow"** (Jefferies, 2026-08-11). **Two supply-chain sources are ~2x apart on 2027 Helios rack volume. Neither is AMD guidance. The AVC print (2026-08-12) is the near-term arbiter.**
- **Context on the comparison that matters: the same Fubon note has Hon Hai shipping 15–20k [[NVDA]] GB300 racks in 2H26 alone, with more in 2027, and a VR200 rack ASP of US$8–9mn.** ➜ **Even at Fubon's high end, 2027 Helios volume is roughly one 2H of GB300 racks at a single ODM. That is the scale gap the MI455 cycle has to close, and this is the cleanest unit-level statement of it on the page.**
- **⚠️ Read against the demand side, which did not deteriorate: JPM/Harlan Sur names AMD among the best-positioned in compute on June WSTS data (industry forecast raised to $1.68T CY26 / $2.25T CY27), and an AlphaSense expert call with a former [[NVDA]] product leader argues enterprises and hyperscalers are moving to a heterogeneous mix "(GPUs, TPUs, AMD, custom chips) based on workload needs, efficiency, and cost"** (both 2026-08-11). **The bull case is unchanged; the delivery date moved.**

**🆕 2026-08-10 — AMD buys Taalas: attacking the memory wall by deleting the memory (UBS / Tim Arcuri, 2026-08-10).** UBS reads the deal, announced late the prior week, as **"another step toward a differentiated AI inference stack,"** following the recent **CBRS partnership for disaggregated inference**:
- **The mechanism:** in a GPU, weights live in external HBM and are repeatedly pulled into compute — bandwidth is the inference constraint. SRAM-centric architectures (CBRS, Groq/NVDA) shrink the movement but the weights are **still data in memory**. **Taalas hard-codes trained model weights directly into the silicon fabric** using fixed transistor structures, **eliminating weight fetches from memory entirely** and freeing SRAM for KV cache and context handling. UBS: it addresses the memory bottleneck **"even more aggressively than SRAM-centric architectures."**
- **The cost:** every new model generally requires a new chip; in exchange, better perf/W and lower cost for **certain high-volume inference workloads**. UBS sees AMD leveraging chip-design R&D, supply chain and customer relationships to scale it, broadening its **custom-compute portfolio**.
- **⚠ The dissenting read on the same deal, same week:** Irrational Analysis — who met Taalas (Ljubisa + one other) months ago under no NDA — says the core IP is **a trick to encode model weights by editing a very small number of lithography masks in the upper metal stack** (14-18 metal layers on ≤7nm-class nodes), so **turnaround from model to chips is only ~2-3 months and NRE is "not that bad"**, with **custom EDA-like wrappers that verify new weight encodings in about a week**. His conclusion is the opposite of the datacenter framing: *"Publicly, AMD is marketing this Taalas acquisition as a datacenter AI play but I think it's more for **FPGA and perhaps some niche embedded AI markets**… This is not a disagg inference solution like Nvidia/Groq."* He had expected Altera, AMD/Xilinx or Lattice to buy them **as an IP company**, having judged Taalas-as-inference-silicon likely to fail absent a way to scale LoRA-style partial weight updates (Irrational Analysis, 2026-08-09). Cross-ref [CEREBRAS.md](CEREBRAS.md).
- **Same source, same week, on the CBRS leg:** the claim that the **Cerebras+MI450 announcement had "not even started" engineering work** — unverified, but it is the direct test of whether AMD's disaggregated-inference story is product or slide.

**Supply-chain read-through:** **AMD's Venice (Zen 6) ramps gradually from 3Q26 into mass volume in 2027** (vs Intel's Diamond Rapids/Oak Stream from 2H27), with the **PCIe 5.0 → 6.0 migration lifting PCB content ~50%** (M6 → M7/8, higher layer count) — the CCL/PCB chain (Delton, ITEQ) is already pricing it (Jefferies / Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-09 and 2026-08-10).

**Q2 FY26 (reported 2026-08-04 AMC — press release + first takes + call notes + ★ the PRIMARY earnings-call transcript, now on file: Bloomberg LIVE TRANSCRIPT, 2026-08-04, folded in below). A beat-and-raise that MISSED the buy-side bar, and the stock fell ~7-9%.** Revenue **$11.54bn** vs Street **$11.31bn** (GS $11.3bn) — **but vs buy-side bogeys of ~$12bn**. Non-GAAP gross margin **56.2%** vs Street 55.8-56.0%. Operating margin **26.8%** (Street 26.7%). Non-GAAP EPS **$1.66** vs Street $1.61-1.62. Segments (actual vs Street): **Data Center $6.72bn vs $6.50-6.60bn** (58% of revenue), **Client $3.06bn vs $3.00bn**, **Gaming $779m vs $791-814m (a miss)**, **Embedded $977m vs $971-978m**. **Q3 FY26 guide: revenue $13.0bn ±$300m at the midpoint** vs Street **$12.5-12.6bn** and GS $12.3bn — **but vs bogeys of ~$13.2bn (Barclays heard $13.25-13.50bn; Jefferies heard $13.4bn)**; **non-GAAP GM ~56%** (Street 55.7-56.25%); **implied EPS ~$1.93** vs Street $1.85. (GS · James Schneider first take, 2026-08-04; Barclays desk · Jeffrey Rand, 2026-08-04; TMTB EOD Wrap, 2026-08-04; Jefferies · Rankie Wong, 2026-08-04.)

⚠️ **This is an expectations miss, not a thesis break — and every desk says so explicitly.** GS: *"We expect the stock to trade lower following a slightly stronger quarter and guidance, set against VERY HIGH EXPECTATIONS heading into the quarter."* Barclays: *"Don't think a lot wrong here on the bull thesis but the stock was up 7% today and these numbers lean a little light for a CROWDED LONG name… I expect this name to remain a favorite long in semis especially as we move closer to the volume GPU ramp in 4Q and into CY27, and I would expect there to be DIP BUYERS if the magnitude of the move down increases."* Jefferies: *"Initial investor feedback is +ve, DESPITE the stock reaction — and most are categorizing this as an EXPECTS MISS RATHER THAN A THESIS BREAK… Sellside #'s clearly going up after this print, but hearing BUYSIDE #'s MOSTLY UNCHANGED OR SLIGHTLY TRIMMED."* TMTB: *"Nothing here changes the bull debate as narrative centered around '27 and beyond."*

**The real content was the 2H26/2027 framing on the call — and it is a guide-up in words rather than numbers, which is why it did not clear the bar (Jefferies · Rankie Wong, 2026-08-04):**
- **2H26:** DC revenue to **accelerate vs 1H** (1H printed +80% y/y — but sell-side was already at +94% for 2H into the print). **Server CPU +80%+ y/y in 2H** and up "substantially" in both Q3 and Q4 (consensus already at +86%/+75%). **DCAI "significant" growth** (consensus already +84%/+150%).
- **2027:** **total DC revenue growth "well over" +100% y/y** — implies upside to consensus at +88%, ⚠️ **but "buyside likely much higher already. Feedback so far indicating people settling in at around ~150% y/y, but hearing some as high as 175-200%."** Server CPU **+70%+ y/y off a higher base** (consensus +52%). DCAI **"well over" +100%** (consensus already +143%).
- **TAM raised again: DCAI accelerator TAM to grow +45%/yr to $1.4trn by 2030; server-CPU TAM +50%/yr to $220bn by 2030**; AMD "confident to outgrow" the overall ~+40% CAGR. Management expects revenue to grow **"substantially above" the prior +35% long-term target** and to **"significantly exceed" the $20 long-term EPS target**.
- **Helios/MI455X:** in production, **initial shipments begin in Q3**, ramping through Q4 and into 2027 — **"coming in AHEAD of volume forecasts"** with strong 2027 demand. ⚠️ **Yield admission: "expect overall yields to improve in the next few quarters — especially for a highly complex product."** Anchor customers named: **[[OPENAI]], [[META]], [[ANTHROPIC]]**, all "in process of ramping," plus interest from smaller non-GW-scale customers.
- **Supply:** Lisa Su sounded confident across **wafers, back-end and substrates**; server capacity was raised through the year (the source of the 2H guide raise) with **"much more capacity coming online in 2027."** Uses chiplets so ramps **fewer leading-edge wafers**. ⚠️ **Supply chain "tight since 1H — a lot of demand was UNFORECASTED"**; into 2027 "demand is better forecasted."
- **🆕 Memory — the most consequential new disclosure, and it cuts both ways: "very good visibility into HBM allocation for 2027"** (i.e. LTAs are signed — see [[MU]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SAMSUNG]]). ⚠️ **But management also opened the door to de-speccing: "every customer is looking at how to optimize memory footprint… we have the OPPORTUNITY TO MODIFY MEMORY FOOTPRINT if TCO is not as significant in certain workloads… for some small/medium-size models, they may not get as much benefit from memory."** That is AMD corroborating the [[NVDA]] Rubin Ultra HBM-despec debate from the demand side, on the record.
- Segment colour: **EPYC +70%+ y/y with both units and ASP up double-digit (units higher; ASP from higher core counts)**, cloud and enterprise both +70%+, **x86 server revenue share gained y/y**; **Venice in production now**, every major OEM on track, leading CSPs deploying later this year, **"customer demand stronger than for any prior generation."** **Client +23% y/y** but planning for a **softer 2H PC market** on component costs. **Gaming −31% y/y** on console cycle + higher industry component costs pushing prices up. **Embedded +19% y/y — strongest growth in 3+ years**, design wins tracking toward **$18bn+** this year.

**Supply-chain corroboration for the 2027 story (Jefferies, 2026-08-04):** *"The +ve commentary for 2027E today is also corroborated by Sherman's recent supply chain checks — he recently revised up AMD's ADVANCED PACKAGING ALLOCATION (TSM's CoWoS + ASE's FoCoS) to 300k for 2027E (+260% y/y), which points to higher output on both their GPUs and CPUs next year."* ⚠️ **Jefferies' own framing of the trade from here is the sharpest question on the page: "bigger question now is what the relative trade is between AMD and [[INTC]] — fundamentally AMD still looks in great shape across CPU and GPU, but IF MOST OF THE NEAR-TERM UPSIDE AND GOOD NEWS IS POTENTIALLY ALL PRICED IN/PLAYED OUT, is there more room for INTC to outperform in the next few months (you still get your CPU exposure, but also upside from foundry customer announcements, and POSITIONING IS LESS CROWDED)?"**

**★ THE PRIMARY TRANSCRIPT IS NOW ON FILE (AMD Q2 2026 earnings call, Bloomberg LIVE TRANSCRIPT, 2026-08-04) — it CONFIRMS every note-derived figure above (nothing revised) and adds the following, which the first takes and desk reconstructions could not carry. The most important item is an explicit on-record guide-up above a NAMED number.**

- **★ THE C.J. MUSE EXCHANGE — management put a FLOOR under 2027 DCAI above ~$30bn, on the record.** Muse (Cantor) took the data-center guide and said it "sort of implies instinct revenues of **30 billion, give or take**" — confirmed in the exchange as **2027 Instinct**. Lisa Su's reply is the single strongest new datapoint from the call: **"what you're hearing from us is that your data center AI number is probably too low"**, and, tying back to Rasgon, **"this notion of, you know, over 100% should consider be well over 100%"** (ASR-garbled sentence, quoted as rendered — the sense is "over 100% should be considered WELL over 100%"). ⚠️ **Read this against the positioning problem that defines the print: consensus 2027 DC growth is +88%, the buy-side had settled at ~150% (some 175-200%). Management has now told the Street on the record that ~$30bn of 2027 Instinct revenue is TOO LOW — without giving a number. That does not reach the 175-200% bogey, but it removes ~$30bn as a ceiling and is a materially harder statement than the "well over 100%" adjective the desks marked the stock down for.** The same mechanism appears in the **Stacy Rasgon (Bernstein)** exchange where the phrase was first pushed: Rasgon asked **"how much work is the 'more' in that statement doing… you seem to be on a trajectory that would take you well above those numbers"**; Su ("Stacy, you bring a smile to my face") answered that server CPU **"more than 70%… we think at this point is a very, very strong statement just given where the business is"** and **"we do expect the overall data center business to be WELL OVER 100%, and the well over 100% comes because the DATA CENTER AI business is going to be well over 100%."** (AMD Q2 2026 earnings call, Bloomberg LIVE TRANSCRIPT, 2026-08-04)
- **Revenue per gigawatt — reaffirmed by management itself, and note WHICH half of the question got answered.** Vivek Arya (BofA) put the premise directly: "the current assumption from everything you've announced is… **about three gigawatts**" of 2027 line-of-sight, and "it seems to be **at least $15 billion per gigawatt**" — then asked Su whether she agreed and where the upside/downside to either variable sits. Su: **"in terms of the revenue per gigawatt, I think we've said double digit billions. We're still in that range… that looks like that's pretty much where it will be."** ⚠️ **The page's $15-20bn/GW marks (UBS 07-15, BofA 07-30, JPM 06-16) are SELL-SIDE estimates; this is AMD itself — but "double digit billions, still in that range" is a floor of $10bn+/GW, i.e. it neither confirms nor refutes $15-20bn.** ⚠️ **And Su never addressed the ~3 GW half of the premise** — the answer walked instead to 2027 DC growth (+70% server CPU, >100% segment), the OpenAI/Meta/Anthropic anchors "all in the process of ramping," and the Helios cadence (Q3 beginning → Q4 step-up → Q1'27 further step-up), plus "we have supply to more than meet the guidance that we've talked about and the upside potential is there." **So the GW count remains a sell-side construction, uncorroborated by management.** (transcript, 08-04)
- **⚠️ A DIRECT CONTRADICTION, now with management as a party to it — "the SAME RACK POWER."** Su's prepared-remarks claim, verbatim: **"Helios delivers up to 15% more throughput at the SAME RACK POWER and up to 30% more tokens per dollar than the competition."** This page already carries **SemiAnalysis's bottoms-up rack build (07-24): MI455X at ~240kW server / ~257kW all-in per rack vs GB300 NVL72 at ~142kW / ~159kW — "MI455X lands even beyond VR NVL72 in power draw"** — and already logs the **opposite top-down read from BofA (05-25), that AMD's MI350X/MI400X platforms are "less power-hungry than Nvidia's."** ⚠️ **"Same rack power" and SemiAnalysis's ~1.6x power delta cannot both be right.** There are now **three data points in one disagreement — AMD management (on record, 08-04) and BofA on one side, SemiAnalysis's bottoms-up build on the other** — and it is deliberately left UNADJUDICATED here, because it decides the "a watt-constrained world deprioritises the watt-loser" argument in Bear. **Resolvable against real Helios deployment data in 2H26**, the first period that generates any. (transcript, 08-04 vs SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-24 vs BofA "Watts to Tokens," 2026-05-25)
- **Q2 SEGMENT OPERATING INCOME (the page carried segment revenue only) — and Client & Gaming is down in BOTH dollars and margin.** **Data Center op income $2.1bn = 31% of segment revenue.** **Client & Gaming $582mn = 15% of revenue, vs $767mn / 21% a year ago** — Jean Hu attributes the decline to "continued investment in go to market activities and expanding product roadmap," i.e. spend, not price. **Embedded $386mn = 40% of revenue, vs $275mn / 33% a year ago**, "driven by higher revenue and favorable product mix" — the highest-margin segment is also the one inflecting, which is what makes Hu's 2027 embedded-GM-tailwind comment below credible. (transcript, 08-04)
- **⚠️ CASH FLOW AND BALANCE SHEET — skipped entirely by the sell-side takes, and this is the mildly negative item of the print.** Q2: **cash from continuing operations $2.4bn**, **free cash flow $1.6bn**, **inventory up sequentially to ~$8.5bn "to support strong data center demand at the end of the quarter"**, **cash + equivalents + short-term investments $13.1bn**. ⚠️ **Set that against this page's own Q1 FY26 mark — a RECORD FCF of $2.6bn (25% of revenue): FCF FELL ~38% q/q to $1.6bn while revenue rose +13% q/q, with inventory building.** Stated as an observation from the two dated figures, not a diagnosis: management's only offered explanation is the inventory build "to support strong data center demand," and **no FCF guide was given**. **A working-capital drag on the ramp — worth watching, because Q3/Q4 double down on exactly the same build (Helios initial shipments + Venice + tight server supply).** (transcript, 08-04 vs Q1 FY26 call, 2026-05-06)
- **Q3 FY26 guide — the line items BELOW revenue and gross margin, none of which were on the page.** **Non-GAAP opex ~$3.65bn** (vs **$3.4bn actual in Q2, itself +40% y/y** on R&D for "AI, silicon systems and software capabilities"), **non-GAAP other income & expense a GAIN of ~$55mn**, **non-GAAP effective tax rate 13%**, **diluted share count ~1.66bn**. Q3 revenue framed as **+41% y/y / +13% q/q**, with "very strong double digit growth" in Data Center, "strong double digit" in Embedded, and Client & Gaming down modestly (slight Client growth more than offset by a strong double-digit Gaming decline). Q2 actuals on the same lines: **op income $3.1bn / 27% OM** and **diluted EPS +82% y/y** — EPS compounding at ~1.6x the revenue growth rate. ⚠️ **The opex line is the one to model: ~$3.65bn is roughly +7% q/q against a +13% q/q revenue step, so operating leverage is intact in the guide even though gross margin is flat q/q.** (transcript, 08-04)
- **2027 gross-margin puts and takes in Jean Hu's OWN words (the page had a compressed version) — and one leg is net-new.** Server-CPU expansion, with the raised TAM: **"from a gross margin perspective, is ACCRETIVE to our overall gross margin."** Data center AI: **"even though the gross margin is SLIGHTLY BELOW CORPORATE AVERAGE"** it delivers "large incremental revenue opportunity… not only it adds a tremendous revenue for the company, but also GROSS PROFIT" — note the framing is gross-profit dollars, not margin. 🆕 **The third leg, absent from the note-derived version: "our embedded business is recovering significantly. We actually think the EMBEDDED BUSINESS WILL GIVE US AN ADDITIONAL TAILWIND ON THE GROSS MARGIN SIDE IN 2027."** And the opex-discipline commitment that underwrites the EPS target (Atif Malik/Citi's question): **"you should expect us to MANAGE OPEX INCREASES LESS THAN THE TOP LINE REVENUE GROWTH. That's what our business model is designed for, so we can drive more operating leverage to deliver earnings per share"** — explicitly tied to **"our EPS will be significantly higher than $20 we outlined at our Financial Analyst Day."** ⚠️ **Hu declined to put a number on 2027 GM ("we'll give you more color when we get there to guide the 2027") and said quarterly mix "could change differently" — so the flat-GM-on-rising-revenue pattern is not committed away, and the mix arithmetic in the Margins row of `## Intra-quarter` stands.** (transcript, 08-04)
- **Server-CPU TAM COMPOSITION — net-new granularity on the $220bn 2030 number.** Answering Aaron Rakers (Wells Fargo) on the three buckets, Su: **the "agentic AI" / "agentic sandbox" bucket is the LARGEST piece of the 2030 TAM, the FASTEST-growing piece, and the SMALLEST piece today** — "in the near term I would say that there is certainly growth in that area, but as we go out over the next three, four, five years, we think that's the largest growth in the server market." Venice is positioned deliberately across all three — **general-purpose, agentic AI, and AI host/head nodes** — with the point being that "it's not just one CPU, it's actually a real family of CPUs that you need," different optimisation points per workload. The share-gain mechanism, stated plainly and worth carrying as the cleanest version on the page: **"there are more workloads that are going to be run on the NEXT generation of EPYC than are run on the PREVIOUS generation"** (Turin → Venice), which is what Su says gives AMD "the confidence to say we can grow substantially ahead of the market." ⚠️ **The composition also sharpens MS's 07-24 objection to the TAM's credibility ("if its forecast was that far off 8 months ago we can't really feel certainty"): the LARGEST 2030 bucket is the one with the LEAST revenue history today.** (transcript, 08-04)
- **Jean Hu clarifies the press-release "data center accelerates in the second half" language — it is a y/y-vs-y/y statement, NOT sequential.** Rasgon flagged the apparent inconsistency directly (DC grew +107% y/y in Q2 vs a Q3 guide "in the 80s" — "which is not an acceleration"). Hu: **"when we talk about this acceleration, we're talking about the SECOND HALF VERSUS THE FIRST HALF. If you look at the first half, our data center business… year over year growth versus the second half, we do think there's an acceleration."** ⚠️ **Useful precisely because the sell-side was already at +94% for 2H against 1H's +80% — so the "acceleration" management is claiming is a bar consensus had already cleared, which is part of the mechanical reason the guide read light.** Related Q4 shape, from the Arcuri (UBS) exchange on the same arithmetic: server CPU grows "substantially" in **both Q3 and Q4** as more supply comes online, and **Q4 Data Center growth "will be HIGHER than" Q3's "very strong double digit"** — because **"the Helios ramp is just starting at the end of Q3, and it'll be much more substantial in Q4."** (transcript, 08-04)
- **Smaller confirmations, each net-new to the page in this exact form.** **ROCm / rocm.ai:** **"more than 3 million models now run out of the box on AMD"**, **open-source contributions to ROCm up more than 10x y/y**, and rocm.ai delivering **">2x the training performance and >3x the inferencing performance of ROCm 7"** across a broad range of models (the page previously carried 2.4x/3.3x on DeepSeek specifically, from MS's event recap — this is the broader company-level claim), with the leading labs (OpenAI, Anthropic, Meta) working directly on ROCm optimisation. **Venice:** the family is **more than 30 processors**, claimed **>2x performance/watt vs leading x86 CPUs** and **up to 3.3x vs leading Arm-based CPUs**, built on **Zen 6 + 2nm**, "in production now," every major OEM on track, leading CSPs deploying "beginning later this year," demand "stronger than for any prior EPYC generation"; **Turin now powers nearly ONE THIRD of the more than 1,600 EPYC public-cloud instance types available globally**, with customers adding "tens of millions of instances in the last quarter." ⚠️ **ASR-ambiguous and therefore NOT adopted as a number: "More than 23[5]th gen Epyc platforms are now in market from HPE, Dell, Lenovo, Supermicro and others" — most plausibly >23 fifth-gen EPYC platforms, but the token is garbled and the figure is not asserted here.** **[[CEREBRAS]]:** the disaggregated fast-inference solution is **"available in Q4 in the Cerebras cloud and extend[ing] into 2027"** — a firmer date than the "later this year" this page carried from the 07-24 event. **[[MSFT]]:** **"Microsoft will deploy Helios AT SCALE ON AZURE FOR FRONTIER MODEL INFERENCING"** across **Microsoft itself, its AI customers, AND Azure AI services** — the three-way scope is new, and it cuts against the "Microsoft PR may not be incremental / mainly OpenAI as the end customer" read carried on this page from BofA (07-20) and SemiAnalysis (07-24). **Streak / context items:** **sixth consecutive quarter of >30% y/y revenue growth**, **fifth consecutive quarter of record server-CPU revenue**, **fourth consecutive quarter of record enterprise sell-through**, Data Center now **58% of revenue vs 42% a year ago**; and the comparability caveat that governs every y/y figure on this print — **all y/y comparisons EXCLUDE the ~$800mn of MI308 China inventory and related charges taken in Q2 2025**. The **"Helios ahead of our initial forecast"** line is clarified on the record as **VOLUMES, not yields** — Su: "it was as it relates to the overall volumes… the amount of demand there is for Helios in 2027" — with the yield answer given separately ("one would expect that the overall yields will improve as we go through the next few quarters… especially on a product like this, which is highly complex"). Client: Su expects **local/on-device AI to become a larger part of how people experience AI** (⚠️ the transcript's "apex" here is garbled — most likely **NPUs**, flagged as uncertain rather than asserted). [Q2 FY26 call transcript](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/AMD_2Q.html) (transcript, 08-04)

**Prior quarter — Q1 FY26 (2026-05-06):** Revenue $10.3B, +38% y/y (beat high end of guide), flat q/q. GAAP-equiv diluted EPS $1.37, +43% y/y. Non-GAAP gross margin 55% (+170bps y/y); op income $2.5B, 25% OM; record FCF $2.6B (25% of revenue). Cash/STI $12.3B.
- **Data Center** record $5.8B, +57% y/y / +7% q/q; segment OM 28%. Server CPU 4th consecutive record, >50% y/y (Turin crossed >50% of server mix). Data Center AI down modestly q/q purely on China step-down ($390M MI308 in Q4 → ~$100M Q1, per Q4 FY25 call 2026-02-03); ex-China not material per Jean Hu.
- **Client & Gaming** $3.6B, +23% y/y (Client +26% to $2.9B; Gaming +11% to $720M); segment OM 16%.
- **Embedded** $873M, +6% y/y; segment OM 39%.

**Guidance Q2 FY26:** Revenue ~$11.2B ±$300M (+46% y/y, +9% q/q), GM ~56%, opex ~$3.3B, tax 13%, ~1.66B shares. **Server CPU guided >70% y/y in Q2**, expected to persist through 2H. **2H gaming guided to decline >20% vs 1H** on memory/component-cost demand drag; 2H PC shipments also planned lower for the same reason, though Client still grows y/y. MI450/Helios ramps Q3 (initial) → significant Q4, into 2027.

**Raised framing (Q1 FY26 call):** Server CPU TAM lifted to **>$120B by 2030 at >35% CAGR** (vs the ~$60B/~18% put out at the Nov-2025 FAD just six months earlier), driven by agentic AI orchestration / CPU-per-GPU ratios moving toward 1:1. The older ~$60B-by-CY30 framing is corroborated externally by UBS, which models server-CPU TAM reaching ~$60B by CY30 at ~+17% CAGR with AMD likely taking ~40% of value share (and ~32% of combined PC+server CPU share by CY28E) (UBS 2026 overview). UBS's dedicated **"CPU TAM" work (2026-05-21)** sizes total server-CPU TAM at **$31B (2025) → $74B (2027E) → $173B (2030E)** bottom-up (or ~$168-170B top-down on 3-4T AI spend + ~1:1 CPU/GPU attach), with AI-server CPU units alone 3M→12M→33M. On **unit share**, AMD is modeled **24% (2025) → 27% (2027E) → 29% (2030E)**, ARM the big winner 16%→32%→42%, Intel collapsing 60%→40%→29% — i.e. AMD holds/gains modest unit share while the structural loser is Intel, not AMD. AMD's leading-edge wafer demand (with ARM) rises to ~122kwpm by 2030E (from 18kwpm 2025); AMD compute-die area keeps climbing Genoa→Turin→**Venice (2026)**. Fubon adds the agentic-CPU mechanism (2026-06-05): under agentic AI the CPU orchestrates multi-step plans (the GPU returns plans, not answers), making CPU perf "the key determinant of agent efficiency" — and AMD's Gen-6 EPYC pushes to **256 cores on TSMC N2 + ASE advanced packaging (FoCoS)**, with AMD's Venice CPU among ASE's three flagship 2027 packaging projects. Long-term targets reiterated from FAD (2025-11-11): >35% revenue CAGR, DC segment >60% CAGR, GM 55-58%, "tens of billions" of AI revenue in 2027, and **>$20 EPS** over the strategic horizon.

**Venice (2026 flagship EPYC, Zen6) architecture detail (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09):** 256-core, built on TSMC N2 (compute) + N6 (I/O); 8 CCDs of 32 Zen6 cores each. AMD finally adopts advanced packaging (EMIB-equivalent) to connect the CCDs to the I/O die — a first for AMD's chiplet approach. 16-channel MRDIMM-12800 memory = 1.64TB/s bandwidth, "2.67x Turin" (channel count up from 12 in 2022's Genoa to 16 in Venice). Mesh design: 32 Zen6c cores arranged in a 4x8 grid, with full 4MB L3 cache/core restored (had been halved on Zen5c) → 128MB cache regions per CCD. New AI-datatype instructions AVX512_FP16, AVX_VNNI_INT8, and new AVX512_BMM (Bit Matrix Multiplication) — SemiAnalysis's analysts believe BMM's binary matrices "do not have sufficient precision for LLMs," so limited adoption is expected (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09). **Vendor claim (AMD's own marketing slide, not independently verified):** AMD's top 256-core Venice variant claims ">1.3x thread density" and ">1.7x performance & efficiency" vs its own top 192-core Turin (SPECrate2017_int_base) (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09).

**AMD's own vendor claim on Turin migration economics** (AMD marketing slide, cited by SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09): migrating 1,000 old 2P Intel Xeon Platinum 8280 servers to 131 modern 2P AMD EPYC 9965 (Turin) servers = 7:1 consolidation, "up to 68% less power," "up to 87% fewer servers," "up to 67% lower 3-yr TCO" (SPECrate2017_int_base benchmark) — labeled here as AMD's own claim, not SemiAnalysis's independent finding.

**New 8-channel Venice SP8 platform** (successor to EPYC 8004 "Siena," up to 128 dense Zen6c cores) launches directly into the gap Intel vacated by cancelling its Diamond Rapids-SP mainstream platform; SemiAnalysis's own conclusion: "AMD will see large share gains in the enterprise markets, a traditional Intel stronghold" (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09).

**Venice unit ramp + CoWoS demand (UBS · Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01):** AMD Venice server-CPU volumes estimated at **4mn units in 2027, up from 1.3mn in 2026**; AMD's total CoWoS demand could grow **232% in 2027E** on the Venice ramp plus MI450/455.

**Server-CPU share + per-GW efficiency (UBS "Asia Semiconductors — Sector Keys", Sunny Lin, 2026-07-02):** the companion sector note reiterates AMD's server-CPU **unit share rising 24% (2025) → 29% (2030E)** (Intel 60%→29%, ARM the big winner 16%→42%) within a global server-CPU market growing **23m→63-70m units 2025→2030 (30% CAGR), ~US$170bn by 2030E** — a driver of TSMC leading-edge demand (server CPUs ~200kwpm of leading-edge capacity by 2030E, from 71kwpm in 2025). On the GPU side, UBS notes **AMD's AI GPU requires the least TSMC capacity per GW — ~1kwpm N2 for the upcoming MI450/455**, on the chiplet design — and confirms AMD's CoWoS demand could **triple in 2027E** on a stronger MI450 ramp plus the Venice server CPU. (UBS "Asia Semiconductors — Sector Keys", 2026-07-02)

**2027 CoWoS allocation ~240k, MI455/MI450 ~1.5mn units (MS · Charlie Chan, "AI Supply Chain", 2026-07-08):** AMD's 2027 CoWoS allocation remains ~240k (for now), with combined MI455/MI450 shipments at ~1.5mn units — a supply-chain checkpoint on the GPU ramp. (Morgan Stanley / Charlie Chan, 2026-07-08)

**BofA raises PO to $620, beat/raise into 'Advancing AI' (BofA "Core Wars Q2 Preview", Vivek Arya, 2026-07-13):** BofA reiterates **Buy, PO $620 (from $550; 47x CY27E PE vs 42x prior)** and expects a **beat/raise on continued EPYC server-CPU share gains + strong cloud demand**, with the Q3 outlook likely incorporating the **first MI455X 'Helios' rack shipment ahead of Q4 volume (~$6-7bn+/qtr exiting Q4)** and 6th-gen EPYC **Venice** launching alongside Helios; the **Jul-23 'Advancing AI 2026' event** (Lisa Su keynote) is the near-term catalyst (Venice/Verano CPUs, MI455X GPU, Helios rack). BofA frames AMD as owning the **broadest CPU portfolio for agentic AI**: Venice tops out at 256 cores (vs Turin 192, NVDA Vera 88, INTC Diamond Rapids ~192), and BofA's modeled rack-level results give the current EPYC 9965 (Turin, 192C) a **2.37x** normalized-geomean advantage over NVDA Vera (88 cores), extending to **3.30x** with Venice — the high-core-count lead that keeps AMD at **~25-27% of server-CPU value share through CY30** even as ARM-based processors ramp. Management's last server-CPU TAM was **$120bn (May)**, which BofA thinks could be raised again. Mizuho (Vijay Rakesh, 2026-07-11) initiates the page at **Outperform $615** ⚠️ **[SUPERSEDED 2026-08-16 → Outperform $580, no stated rationale — see `## Changelog`]** (~42x F27E EPS; bull $800 / bear $450), naming AMD a technology-enabler of the >$1.4T-by-2028E AI-DC-capex ramp (AI accelerator TAM ~$1T by 2030, >40% 24-28E CAGR, AMD ~MSD% share). [BofA source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Vivek_on_CPUs.html)

**AMD in BofA's rack-power demand model (BofA "Watts to Tokens", Vivek Arya, 2026-05-25, read-through):** in BofA's bottoms-up accelerator/rack power model (rack power ~100x to ~1.5MW at Nvidia Feynman CY29/30; ~233GW deployed through CY30), AMD's merchant GPU is one of the non-NVDA platforms, modeled at **~10GW of cumulative deployed capacity CY26-30** (AMD accelerator units 55k CY22 → ~1.4m CY30), i.e. a modest slice of the ~103GW non-NVDA / ~208-233GW total. BofA notes AMD's platforms (MI350X → MI400X, Helios rack) are **less power-hungry than Nvidia's** on the power-budget analysis, and that custom ASICs are generally lower-power still — power/perf-per-watt the competitive axis as the industry moves to 800V. Light read-through: confirms AMD is in the demand model but a minority share behind NVDA and the Google-TPU/AWS-Trainium ASIC ramp; see [ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md) / [custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bofa_Watt_to_tokens.html)

**Financial inflection framing — Barron's "Top CEOs of 2026" (Jack Hough, Barron's, 2026-06-22):** profiling CEO Lisa Su, Barron's notes that "since her first full year as CEO in 2015, sales have grown at a 24% annual rate — and by 38% in the most recent quarter," and that "AMD pulled even with Intel in [data centers] in 2024, and Wall Street analysts expect AMD's data-center sales to surpass Intel's in 2026. They will also become a majority of the company's total revenue" (Barron's, 2026-06-22). Separately, Barron's cover story "Rally in Chips" (Adam Levine, Barron's, 2026-06-01) frames AMD as having "caught up to Intel in CPU chips," with **EPS to jump from $4.17 (2025) to $13.10 (2027), a 77% annualized increase**; AMD claims (**vendor claim**) "40% share" in the data-center CPU market and "doubled its estimate for the data-center CPU market to $120 billion by 2030" (consistent with the >$120B/2030 TAM raised on the Q1 FY26 call above). On the Q1 FY26 earnings call, CEO Lisa Su said: "These results mark a clear inflection in our growth trajectory and a structural shift in our business... Data center is now the primary driver of our revenue and earnings growth" (quoted in Barron's, 2026-06-01). Stock trades 40x 2027 EPS, PEG 0.49x per Barron's calc (Barron's, 2026-06-01).

**UBS raises PT to $700 into AMD's 'Advancing AI' day; GPU-revenue estimates up (UBS · Tim Arcuri, "Previewing AMD's AI Day," 2026-07-15):** UBS reiterates **Buy, PT $700 (from $670)**, on 33x (from 35x) applied to **C28E EPS of $21.11** (from $18.85). Estimate changes: **C2026/27/28 revenue $50.7B/$83.4B/$112.3B** (from $50.4B/$79.2B/$104B); **EPS $7.69/$14.63/$21.11** (from $7.61/$13.47/$18.85). GPU-revenue estimates raised to **$41B in C2027 (from $38B) and $61B in C2028 (from $54B)** — modeling a third large-scale customer ramping just below 1GW in year one, on ~$15-20B GPU revenue per GW of deployed capacity and three ~1GW+ customers by 2027 (path to ~$40-50B GPU revenue in C2027). Data-center segment $32.4B/$65.2B/$91.9B (C26/27/28). Expects the Jul-23 event to focus on the technology roadmap (MI450x Helios later this year, more MI500 detail for 2027, early MI600/2028) more than customers/financials; believes **Amazon a major MI450x customer and Anthropic possibly on the list** (tempers expectations of announcements at the event), and flags a possible **AMD-Cerebras fast-inference partnership + a deeper custom-ASIC push**. On CPU: Venice launches (2nm) with Verano (2027, TSMC 2nm + LPDDR SOCAMM2 memory); last formal server-CPU TAM was **$120B by C2030 (1Q26)**, likely conservative given strengthened data points. This is the current UBS mark; it follows the 06-24 "Revisiting AMD and ARM Estimates" note that took the PT to **$670 (from $455)**, EPS $7.61/$13.47/$18.85, and lifted AMD's C2030 server-CPU revenue to **$50B (from $41B)** in a **$170B C2030 CPU TAM**. (UBS / Tim Arcuri, 2026-07-15 & 2026-06-24) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_on_AMD.html)

**KeyBanc lifts general-purpose server unit-growth outlook to +30% on AMD/INTC wafer supply (KeyBanc via @firstadopter, 2026-07-14):** KeyBanc raised its general-purpose server-CPU **unit-growth outlook to +30% (from +18.5%)** as AMD/Intel squeeze out additional wafer supply and ARM demand rises for agentic-AI workloads. Specific to AMD: it **obtained incremental N4 wafers at TSMC** (freed by MediaTek order cuts on weaker smartphone builds) and estimates it can support **+15-20% AMD server-CPU unit growth this year**; both AMD and Intel now target **>50% server-CPU unit growth in 2027** and are expanding capacity to support it. (Read: these are unit figures — AMD management's >70% Q2 y/y server-CPU number is revenue, i.e. units + ASP/mix — so additive, not contradictory.) (KeyBanc via @firstadopter, 2026-07-14)

**Anthropic becomes AMD's 4th GW-scale customer + Microsoft Helios commitment (2026-07-22 / 07-20):** at/around the **"Advancing AI 2026"** event, **AMD and Anthropic announced up to 2GW of MI450-series (Helios/MI455X) deployment beginning 1H27**, plus a multi-year engineering collaboration (optimize Claude workloads on Instinct + accelerate ROCm). Crucially, the deal is structured as **AMD investing $5B of equity into Anthropic (NOT warrants)** — unlike the OpenAI/Meta deals which carried AMD-share warrants — read by the desk as a **power-shift toward chip companies** in a supply-constrained world (equity "no longer on the table" for LT chip agreements; Barclays/O'Malley OW $665, 2026-07-22). Each GW ≈ **$15-20B** of AMD content (creeping higher on rising CPU intensity), implying **~$15-20B/yr run-rate for Anthropic alone**. Two days earlier (07-20), **Microsoft formalized its Helios commitment** — MI455X GPUs + 6th-gen EPYC "Venice" CPUs + Pensando networking + ROCm in an open rack-scale platform, shipments beginning 2H26 (Lisa Su/@satyanadella; corroborated by Patrick Moorhead). Sell-side read: AMD now has **4 large MI customers — Anthropic, OpenAI (via Microsoft cloud), Oracle, Meta** — with AWS a potential future adopter. **UBS (Arcuri): $5B/no-warrant structure is preferred/lower-dilution; adds conviction to the $40B DC GPU 2027 rev, step-up 2028.** **BofA (Arya, reit Buy $620): the deal could drive $10-15bn upside to the CY27 MI consensus of $35-40bn; DC likely >2x YoY to $60-65bn+ in 2027; ~1.4mn MI450 units CY27 > $40bn at ~$30k ASP.** FinTwit checks (GFHK via @jukan05, 07-21) flag the Anthropic deal could be **larger than the OpenAI and Meta deals**. (AMD/Anthropic + Microsoft announcements, 2026-07-20/22; UBS · Arcuri; BofA · Arya; Barclays · O'Malley)

**"Advancing AI 2026" — the full post-event fold (2026-07-24). The headline was not a customer; it was a TAM re-rate of a magnitude that makes the sell-side uneasy.** AMD raised all three of its CY30 TAM frames at once: **total compute to $2Tn (+45% CAGR), from $1Tn prior**; **AI accelerators to $1.4Tn (+48% CAGR), vs $500bn for CY28 prior**; and **server CPUs to >$220bn (+50% CAGR), from $120bn earlier this year** (BofA · Vivek Arya; MS · Joseph Moore, both 2026-07-24). The server-CPU cadence is the one to keep in view: **~$60bn (18% CAGR) at the Nov-2025 Analyst Day → $120bn (35%) on the Q1 FY26 call in April/May → $220bn (50%) on 2026-07-23** — three raises in eight months. MS's reaction is the honest one and worth quoting because it cuts both ways: *"That pace makes us a bit nervous — after all, if its forecast was that far off 8 months ago we can't really feel certainty — but it certainly points to a very strong 12-month view, and very likely foreshadows very good data center numbers when the company reports."* AMD also framed **agentic AI as ~50% of the TAM with the CPU:GPU ratio tightening toward 1:1** (@sssjeffpu, 2026-07-24), and put usage numbers behind it: **~35 quadrillion monthly tokens, ~160x growth in two years, with inference demand now exceeding training at ~60% of AI compute in 2026** (@PatrickMoorhead, 2026-07-23).
- **Helios launched and in full production — the specs, and the honest scorecard vs Vera Rubin.** Each rack: **72x MI455X + 18x 6th-gen EPYC "Venice" + Pensando NICs/DPUs**, UALink-over-Ethernet scale-up, Ultra Ethernet scale-out, ROCm. Headline specs **~2.9 EF FP4 / 1.4 EF FP8; 31TB HBM4 (~1.7 PB/s); 260/43 TB/s scale-up/scale-out**. Versus **NVDA Vera Rubin NVL72**, AMD claims ~**15% more FP4 compute in training** — but BofA is explicit that it is **~20% *less* in FP4 inference** — plus **~50% more HBM capacity**, **the same scale-up bandwidth**, ~50% more scale-out bandwidth, and **up to ~30% more tokens/$** (with memory bandwidth only ~6% ahead). **Rack pricing could reach ~$5mn+ vs Vera Rubin's ~$6-7mn+** — i.e. a meaningful part of the tokens/$ claim is price, not performance. Versus its own MI355X, MI455X roughly **doubles FP4/FP8 compute, adds ~50% HBM (432GB vs 288GB) and ~2.5x memory bandwidth** (BofA · Arya, 2026-07-24); Guotai's harder claim is **34x higher throughput and 18x lower token cost vs MI355X**, already outperforming NVDA B200 on some LLM inference (@sssjeffpu, 2026-07-24). **Shipments start end-Q3 and ramp through Q4** — consistent with the prior guide. Third-party validation cited by BofA: **SemiAnalysis/InferenceX show MI355X at up to ~40% lower cost-per-token than NVDA B200 on SGLang FP8** — notable given SemiAnalysis is the source of the harshest ROCm criticism on this page (see Bear).
- **Cerebras partnership — the fast-decode answer, and it lands where BofA said to look.** Cerebras CEO Andrew Feldman announced a **joint disaggregated-inference solution pairing Helios racks with Cerebras wafer-scale engines: Helios handles high-throughput prompt processing / long context, Cerebras handles ultra-low-latency token generation, targeting up to 5x throughput (per watt per second) for latency-sensitive real-time agents**; available later this year via **Cerebras Cloud**, with Cerebras deploying Helios in its own data centers (MS · Moore, 2026-07-24; @QQ_Timmy, 2026-07-24). MS frames the competitive logic precisely: this is *"an interesting alternative to Nvidia+Groq"* — NVDA will be able to offer GPU↔SRAM integration via its Groq acquisition, so AMD needed a wafer-scale/SRAM partner. This directly answers **debate #2 of the five BofA flagged pre-event** ("will AMD have options for fast token, fast decode… perhaps Cerebras, d-Matrix"). See [[CEREBRAS]].
- **Venice segmented into three SKUs — the agentic-workload framing made concrete.** 6th-gen EPYC "Venice" ships in **three variants**: **Venice HF** for GPU host nodes; a **256-core version for "agent sandbox" workloads** (density-optimised, up to **512 threads/socket**); and a **128-core version for general enterprise**. AMD cites **up to 1.8x higher performance than Turin, >2x agents per watt vs x86 competition, and up to 3.3x rack-level performance per watt vs competing Arm CPUs** (MS · Moore, 2026-07-24) — the Arm comparison is the newer and more strategically pointed claim, since Arm share gain (not Intel) is the structural bear case on this page. Guotai has Venice at **2.2x/2.0x throughput vs competitors** on AI workloads (@sssjeffpu, 2026-07-24). **Meta appeared on stage** to discuss a **fourth consecutive EPYC generation** and described MI300/350/450 as a **progressively deeper co-design relationship** — the most concrete multi-generation commitment tell of the event, and the answer to the "one-generation customer" bear worry.
- **Roadmap extended to 2028 (previewed to 2030), and the MI500 optical claim is new.** GPUs: **MI450 Series begins 2H26 → MI500 in 2027 (with Helios 500 and the "Verano" CPU) → MI600 in 2028 (Helios 600, Zen 7 "Ferrara")**, with MS citing **">2,000x inference performance improvement over four years"** for the MI500 generation. CPUs: **Zen 7-based "Florence," "Ferrara" and "Fidenza" in 2028; Zen 8 "Ravenna" in 2030.** The net-new technical claim: **MI500 introduces optical interconnect and leads NVDA Rubin Ultra on HBM, 4-die packaging and scale-up domain** (@sssjeffpu, 2026-07-24) — worth tracking against the existing page view that AMD's smaller scale-up domain vs NVL72 is a core constraint. This is now an annual cadence matching NVDA's (which includes Feynman in 2H28) (BofA · Arya, 2026-07-24).
- **rocm.ai — the software answer is to let coding agents do the porting.** AMD introduced **rocm.ai, an agentic layer on top of ROCm that lets existing coding agents (Claude, Codex, Cursor) understand AMD hardware and ROCm natively**, automating kernel selection, tuning and optimisation. Demonstrated: a **38% tokens-per-second improvement optimising MiniMax M3 on MI355**, and cited **2.4x average training / 3.3x inference speedups on DeepSeek models vs ROCm 7**. Cadence improved materially — **ROCm now ships every six weeks rather than every four months** — with default enablement across Hugging Face, PyTorch, JAX, vLLM and SGLang (MS · Moore, 2026-07-24). AMD also emphasised a **multi-year ROCm co-development effort using Claude** (BofA · Arya, 2026-07-24) — the reciprocal side of the [[ANTHROPIC]] deal, in which AMD commits to using Claude in its own R&D. Corroborating datapoint from Anthropic's side: **"We are past the CUDA moat days. Anthropic says Claude can bootstrap AMD Instinct + ROCm"** — Anthropic's Tom Brown on stage describing how the team evaluated MI355 largely on its own after AMD supplied a rack (@austinsemis, 2026-07-23). Read this against the SemiAnalysis ROCm/vLLM critique in Bear: the software gap is real, but the porting *cost* is what rocm.ai is aimed at.
- **Enterprise / client / physical AI (the long tail):** **MI350P**, air-cooled, positioned against NVDA RTX 6000, with an internal AMD LLM query-routing use case between MI350P and EPYC showing a **43% token-cost reduction**; **AT&T's CTO** on stage launching **Otel 2.0**, an open-source telecom model trained on AMD hardware. Client: **Ryzen AI Halo (120GB unified memory, models to 200B params)** and a preview of **"Gorgon Halo" (192GB, to 300B params)**, bundled with a year of Hugging Face Pro. Physical AI: the **Kria AI system-on-module** (on the new Ryzen AI Embedded X100) plus a turnkey **Kria AI Robotics Developer Platform**, claiming **3.4x better real-time results and 2.3x more concurrent agents than Nvidia's Jetson Thor** (MS · Moore, 2026-07-24).
- **The independent engineering layer — SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026" (2026-07-24). Read this as the counterweight to the sell-side fold above: same event, but built from supply-chain checks, a BOM/TCO model and daily first-hand use of ROCm rather than from AMD's slides. The headline is that the harshest ROCm critic on this page has upgraded AMD — conditionally.** SemiAnalysis's own rating arc, stated explicitly: **"we gave AMD a 0% chance of closing the gap with Nvidia in AI accelerators" → (the "AMD 2.0" piece) "a much more meaningful chance at success" → now, "a great chance of success as long as AMD solves the 2 major risks"** (the two risks are Helios rack ramp and internal cluster/CI capacity — both folded into Bear/Risks below). It also stresses this is not zero-sum: *"just because AMD gains market share, that doesn't mean NVIDIA will do poorly. The pie is getting massive."*
  - **Silicon — the most aggressive package shipping, and it has to be.** MI455X is the **first 2nm datacenter silicon** (both the MI455 compute tiles and the Venice CPU are early N2 adopters; **all competing accelerator platforms are on N3**), the **largest CoWoS-L module shipping at 5.5x reticle**, and AMD is **still the only company adopting TSMC SoIC-X hybrid bonding** — together **3,470mm² of logic silicon per package, "by far the most amount of silicon being shipped in a single package."** Layout: **8 N2 XCDs hybrid-bonded atop 2 reticle-sized base dies** (SRAM + HBM controllers + compute fabric) plus **2 new N3P I/O dies**, identical to each other, each carrying **72 flexible lanes** that support 212G UALoE / 64G Infinity Fabric / PCIe Gen6 / 128G UALink / xGMI4. Believed to be the **first chip shipping TSMC active LSI** (active, circuitry-bearing CoWoS-L bridges with mid-channel repeaters that let the top-die PHYs shrink) — the tell being that TSMC's ISSCC-2026 aLSI "test vehicle" *is* the MI455 interposer (2 base dies, 12 HBM4 stacks, 2 IO dies). **The honest scorecard on all that silicon: 20 PF dense FP8 vs Rubin's 17.5 PF — "these performance advantages are relatively mild compared to Rubin given how much more silicon content there is,"** because AMD trails Nvidia on GPU microarchitecture; specifically **MI455X lacks the 3-bit LUT tensor cores Rubin (SM107) has** (which cut relative HBM-bandwidth need). This is the sharpest available framing of the "AMD buys parity with area" concern and sits underneath the sell-side's FP4/FP8 headline specs.
  - **HBM4 — AMD wins capacity, Nvidia bought back the bandwidth, and the second memory tier has been deleted.** MI455X carries **12 HBM4 stacks = 432GB vs 8 stacks = 288GB on Nvidia and Google** (the base dies grew to a ~32mm HBM-facing edge to fit 3 cubes per side, vs 29mm on the MI300X AID) and takes the bandwidth crown at **23.3 TB/s vs Rubin's 22 TB/s** — up from the 19.6 TB/s advertised at Advancing AI 2025. But the margin is thin *despite a 50% wider bus*, because **AMD's HBM4 pin speed is 7.6 Gbps vs Rubin's 10.7 Gbps (40% faster)**: SemiAnalysis's read is that **Nvidia deliberately raised its HBM4 pin-speed target well above the original JEDEC spec precisely to close the bandwidth deficit to MI455X**, which forced memory suppliers to rework HBM4, pushed out Rubin upstream output (now resolved), and means **Nvidia must buy a materially higher-quality bin than AMD**. AMD and Nvidia are the only two shipping HBM4 this year. **Two margin/BOM consequences the sell-side fold does not carry:** (1) **the LPDDR5X second memory tier is gone** — earlier roadmaps had up to **1TB of LPDDR per MI455X attached on the EAM module**; it has "**disappeared**," together with a **significant DDR5 de-spec** (original 2025 BOM estimate: a "staggering 138TB in DRAM across LPDDR5X and DDR5 MRDIMM," now far lower DDR5 RDIMM content), attributed to tight memory supply — i.e. much of the DRAM price increase is being absorbed by de-spec rather than passed through (this **supersedes** the page's prior "off-package LPDDR as a second memory tier" statement — see Bear and Changelog); (2) SemiAnalysis expects a **significant HBM price increase in 2027** as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM, and AMD **cannot pass the full increase through** ("most of AMD's volume goes to large buyers with real negotiating leverage") — it marks HBM up at **a lower incremental margin than corporate**, **dragging gross margin on the MI455X package**. That is a concrete mechanism behind the Q4-FY26 GM step-down already flagged in Bear.
  - **Helios networking — merchant switching is the design constraint, and Broadcom is the only door.** 72 MI455X across **18 compute trays (4 GPUs + 1 Venice CPU each) and 6 scale-up switch trays** placed mid-rack, each tray holding **2 Broadcom Tomahawk 6 102.4T ASICs = 12 switch ASICs**, flat one-tier all-to-all; each GPU runs **72 lanes of 200G UALoE = 1.8 TB/s (14.4 Tbit/s) uni-di scale-up**, 6 lanes to each of the 12 switches. **Only 432 of each switch's 512 200G lanes are used** — pure over-provisioning, because AMD buys merchant TH6 instead of a co-designed switch; Nvidia's **28.8T NVSwitch divides evenly across 72 GPUs with no wastage**. Scale-out: **400G Pollara → 800G Vulcano**, two NICs = 1.6 Tbit/s/GPU (up to a third NIC for 2.4 Tbit/s, each on an x8 UALink128 at 256 GB/s bidirectional), plus a **Pensando Salina 400 DPU** on the front end. The strategic point: AMD ships GPU, CPU, NIC and DPU but **no scale-up switch**, and **"because Broadcom is the only merchant provider shipping a 100T switch with 200G SerDes, Broadcom is AMD's only choice"** → **"AMD cannot reach extreme codesign for the complete rack solution."** Read across to [[AVGO]]. (Also: for Meta's MI455X variant there are **six 51.2T Minipack Ethernet switches in-rack** and the power shelf sits in a separate IT rack.)
  - **Power is the outlier, and it directly contradicts a claim already on this page.** SemiAnalysis models MI455X at **~240kW of server power and ~257kW all-in per rack, versus GB300 NVL72 at ~142kW server / ~159kW all-in** — "MI455X lands even beyond VR NVL72 in power draw." **This contradicts BofA's read-through** (in the Current-state "Watts to Tokens" note above) that **AMD's MI350X/MI400X platforms are "less power-hungry than Nvidia's"** on its rack-power budget analysis. Both are kept on the page deliberately: BofA is a top-down power-budget model, SemiAnalysis a bottoms-up rack build, and **the disagreement is the alpha** — it decides whether AMD wins or loses the "watt-constrained world deprioritizes the watt-loser" argument already in Bear (BG2 panel). Resolvable against real Helios deployment data from 2H26. (BofA "Watts to Tokens," 2026-05-25 vs SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-24)
  - **TCO — competitive on paper, with a stated haircut.** On capital cost and on total cost of ownership **MI455X lands between GB300 NVL72 and Vera Rubin NVL72**. On **marketed dense FP8**, it looks competitive with even VR NVL72 on **TCO per PFLOP and on TFLOPs/W**, and because its TCO per marketed PFLOP is lower it **can be rented cheaper than VR NVL72 at the same ~15.6% minimum project IRR hurdle**. The caveat is explicit and material: **"AMD has historically overstated TFLOPs in their chip specifications relative to what is achievable, and we suspect that AMD's model FLOPs utilization (MFU) is quite low, which likely leads to a lower effective training throughput."** So treat the TCO/PFLOP win as a marketed-spec result, not a delivered-throughput one — the same caution that applies to the "30% more tokens/$" headline above.
  - **The warrant math — the quantified version of MS's "wipes out most of the GPU profits."** AMD issued performance warrants to **OpenAI (Oct-2025)** and **Meta (Feb-2026)**, **each for up to 160M shares (~10% of shares outstanding per deal) at a $0.01 exercise price**, vesting in tranches tied to the purchase and deployment of **6GW** of Instinct, with **price hurdles stepping up to $600**. At $600 (the level that satisfies all tranches) each would record an **effective rebate of ~$16.0B/GW**; since **OpenAI pays ~$15.2B/GW and Meta ~$18.7B/GW**, that is a **partner discount of ~105% and ~85% respectively** — *"OpenAI could earn money simply by buying MI455X servers and exercising the accompanying warrants."* SemiAnalysis also notes the structural asymmetry between the two: **Meta's agreement wraps the co-engineered cut-down custom Instinct variant, while OpenAI buys off-the-shelf MI455X and successor SKUs**, and **the warrants are an incentive, not a commitment — "OpenAI can back out of the first gigawatt at multiple milestones."** This is independent corroboration of the MS warrant-as-marketing-expense argument in Bear, with a number attached.
  - **Customer/financing detail net-new to the page.** **Microsoft:** dropped AMD after MI300X **in 2023 over unreliable Samsung 2023 HBM and poor software quality, skipping both MI325X and MI355X**; the new MI455X Helios commitment is believed to be **"mainly for OpenAI as the end customer for Azure's MI455X racks"** — i.e. closer to BofA's own 07-20 caution that the Microsoft PR "may not be incremental" than to a clean new logo. **Anthropic (07-22):** up to **2GW of MI455X, first GW online 1H27** in Helios racks, plus the ROCm engineering collaboration and **AMD investing up to $5B of its own cash into Anthropic, released as deployment milestones are met**; Anthropic is now set to run **all four major accelerator platforms — Nvidia, TPU, Trainium and AMD**; SemiAnalysis says it called this in April (Accelerator model) and publicly on 07-20. The bring-up anecdote is the software datapoint: **Anthropic Head of Compute Tom Brown "just used Claude over the weekend with '/goal' to bring-up internal Claude inference stack on AMD hardware."** **Financing:** AMD **guaranteed a $300M loan for Crusoe in Feb-2026**, offering to rent its own chips back if no customer was found, which let Crusoe borrow at **6%** — cheap for a neocloud. And per the companion piece, **AMD has been running backstops since 2025**, offering **AWS, OCI, DigitalOcean, Vultr, Tensorwave, Crusoe and other neoclouds** a deal to rent back capacity for internal AMD software development if the neocloud cannot sell it (SemiAnalysis "Nvidia GPU Debt Backstop Unleashes the AI Project Trinity," 2026-07-06) — note the irony against Risk 2 below: that backstopped, borrowed capacity **is** part of what AMD's internal CI has been running on, and pulling it back is what broke vLLM gating. Nvidia's comparable program started at an **$860M lease guarantee (Oct-2025), rising to $3.5B by Jan-2026**.
  - **Cost-structure footnote (light, from the EDA primer):** AMD's **MI300A configuration carries 13 chiplets** (3 CPU compute dies, 6 GPU compute dies, 4 I/O dies + 8 HBM3 stacks) implying **$75-105M of EDA spend** for that program alone, since every die, then every die-to-die interface, then the system, then the package must be verified separately — the design-cost tax on the chiplet strategy that is otherwise AMD's advantage. EDA lock-in is also rising: AMD's fabless-R&D-to-EDA-spend **R² 0.91 (2019) → 0.93 (2024)** (vs NVDA 0.91 → 0.94). (SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer," 2026-05-21; see [[CDNS]] / [[SNPS]])

## Debate / thesis
**🗄️ ARCHIVE BACKFILL — DAMNANG (Substack), 2026-03-22 / 2026-04-17 / 2026-04-25 / 2026-05-16 / 2026-05-22 / 2026-07-11.** ⚠️ **INDEPENDENT KOREAN SEMIS SUBSTACK (damnang2.substack.com; also publishes as "The Optical Edge"), NOT A BROKER — no rating, no price target, no formal estimate.** Launched 2026-03-15, ~9k subscribers, paid. **These are 4-5 months old and are filed as historical context ONLY; nothing here supersedes any current number, rating or thesis on this page.**
- **🗄️ The fullest single archive piece on this name, and its framing is the one the Street eventually adopted** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "Can AMD Beat NVIDIA?", **2026-04-17**). The author's answer is to reject the question: the test is not whether AMD beats NVIDIA head-on but *"whether the AI infrastructure market grows large enough that AMD becomes an indispensable second source."* **Contemporaneous marks, useful because they date the base the current numbers grew from: [[OPENAI]] 6GW supply agreement signed 2025-10-06, first 1GW on MI450 in 2H 2026, warrants for 160mn shares at a $0.01 exercise price with the final tranche struck at $600; [[META]] 6GW five-year deal announced 2026-02-24, CFO Jean Hu framing revenue in *"double-digit billions of dollars per gigawatt"* and WSJ reporting the deal *"could reach up to $100B"*, with a second 160mn-share warrant — 320mn shares total, *"about 20% of the 1.63B shares outstanding"*. BofA's contemporaneous per-GW range was $17.5–20bn.** **Helios as unveiled at CES 2026: 72 GPUs, 31TB HBM4, 43TB/s scale-out, 260TB/s UALink, 3 AI ExaFLOPS per rack, ~7,000 lbs; MI455X at 432GB HBM4 and 19.6TB/s, AMD's own FP4 claim ~40 PFLOPS.
