MI500 previewed on CDNA 6 / TSMC 2nm / HBM4e for 2027.** Market structure at that date: *"The AI accelerator market is still NVIDIA in the 80% range and AMD hovering around 10%."* **His own dilution base case: partial vesting of 50–80mn shares between 2027 and 2028, with EPS drag under 5%.** ⚠️ **He carries the bear quote too, from Hargreaves Lansdown's Matt Britzman: *"AMD handing over 10% of its equity twice tells you how hard it is to generate organic demand at this scale."*** ➜ **Filed because the "indispensable second source, not challenger" frame is now the consensus lens on this page, and this dates when an independent author reached it — before the Street did.**
- **🗄️ The CPU leg, and it is the archive item that has aged best given the Citi SOTP and MS Asia wafer TAM added above** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "Why the CPU Is the Bottleneck in the Agentic AI Era", **2026-03-22**, and "The CPU Bottleneck Trade: Who Actually Gets Paid in the Agentic AI Era?", **2026-04-25**). Marks as printed: **Q4-2025 data-centre revenue $5.4bn, +39% y/y, with *"fifth-generation EPYC Turin … more than half of server CPU revenue"* and EPYC cloud instances *"more than 50% year over year"*; AMD's x86 server-CPU revenue share crossing 40% for the first time (Mercury Research), and 41.3% in Q4-2025 / 39.4% data-centre share in Q1-2025.** Supply-side texture from the same period: **server CPU ASPs rising ~30% in Q4-2025 and AMD lead times extending from eight to ten weeks**, against [[INTC]] CFO Dave Zinsner calling server-CPU supply *"absolutely constrained."* **Zinsner's attach ratio is the number worth keeping: training-era GPU:CPU *"roughly 1 CPU per 7 to 8 GPUs"*, inference *"1 per 3 to 4"*, agentic AI *"to 1-to-1 or higher."*** ⚠️ **That is Intel's number about the industry, not AMD's about itself — but it is the mechanism under both the Citi SOTP and the Venice wafer-TAM line.**
- **🗄️ Two smaller items, filed compactly.** (1) **The author's own scorecard** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "A Mid-Point Review of the Stocks I've Covered", **2026-05-22**) marks AMD at **"+125% from 2/14, +68% from 4/17"** and attributes the move to a FRAME change rather than an estimate change — the market re-rating AMD *"as a full-stack player not GPU second source"*. ⚠️ **A self-graded track record from an interested author over a period when the whole complex rallied; recorded as commentary, not evidence.** (2) **[[SPCX]] exposure, sized honestly by the author himself** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "The Real SpaceX IPO Beneficiaries Are in Semiconductors", **2026-05-16**): AMD is a **confirmed** SpaceX supplier — *"AMD's CEO Lisa Su directly named SpaceX as a key customer during the Q3 2024 earnings call"*, supplying Versal AI Core adaptive SoCs and rad-hard Kintex UltraScale parts from the Xilinx franchise into *"the latest generation of broadband satellites"* — but he immediately caps it: *"the space segment is a tiny fraction of AMD's total revenue. Buying AMD on the basis of SpaceX upside is a weak thesis."* ➜ **Carried precisely so this page does not later pick up a SpaceX-IPO read-across it cannot support.**
- **🆕 Practitioner read-through — AMD's software gap can be an economic opportunity in throughput-oriented inference, not only a moat deficit (Invest Like the Best EP488; Sail Research founder/CEO Neil Movva, 2026-08-25).** Movva calls AMD accelerators "great chips overall" and argues that weaker out-of-the-box kernels than NVIDIA leave more optimization alpha for an operator willing to rebuild around the hardware. His proposed background-agent stack values batch throughput and FLOPS-per-dollar over minimum latency, making AMD and other non-NVIDIA accelerators more usable inside a heterogeneous fleet; he also says large buyers including Meta and OpenAI have absorbed meaningful AMD supply. ⚠️ **Scope:** interested prospective-buyer testimony, not disclosed AMD orders or an independent benchmark. It does not displace the page's documented ROCm/long-context execution risks or change any MI-series volume, rating, PT or estimate.
- **Bull:**
  - **CPU is the underappreciated upside, and it carries the margins.** Server CPU >70% y/y in Q2, sustainable through 2H, mostly unit-driven (~2/3 units, 1/3 ASP) at 60-65%+ gross margins vs lower-margin GPU+HBM (AMD callback, 2026-05-06). Joe Moore (MS, 2026-06-16) "very enthused" on Venice (2nm, ramps 2H), views agentic CPU as incremental net-new demand, not a GPU ratio, and flags an x86 security edge for enterprise agents over Arm. Share-gain trajectory is structural: AMD took record server value share of 36.4% in Q4 (+190bps q/q) on units +25% y/y and ASPs now well above Intel's; UBS models AMD server value share rising past ~37% by CY28E and PC+server unit share toward ~32% (UBS 2026 overview). UBS (Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01) now sizes the Venice ramp explicitly: server-CPU volumes to **4mn units in 2027 from 1.3mn in 2026**, with total AMD CoWoS demand growing **232% in 2027E** on Venice plus MI450/455 — a materially steeper unit ramp than the earlier UBS "CPU TAM" bottom-up share framing (24%→27%→29% unit share) implied on its own.
  - **GPU optionality on top of an under-owned stock.** Each gigawatt deal ≈ $15-20B; OpenAI + Meta ≈ 2GW of the ~3GW in play for 2027; potential additional customer(s) (Anthropic per Citi checks, 2026-05-18) could add big chunks. AI revenue path $14-15B FY26 → $30-40B+ FY27. AMD confirms ~$15B+ revenue capture per GW and "multiple GW+/year customers"; UBS models C2026 DC revenue at ~$29B and flags Street ~$30B C2027 GPU revenue as **likely too conservative** given a rapidly broadening customer base, with ~$0.15 EPS sensitivity per incremental $1B revenue against ~$10.30 C2027 EPS (UBS 2026 overview). Citi (Atif Malik) upgraded to Buy, PT $575, ~+12-13% above Street CY26-28E, SOTP-based (2026-06-13); reaffirmed AMD as **#1 large-cap** in its 2026-06-29 memory-supply ranking (served by both Samsung/Micron). Bernstein (Rasgon) Outperform PT $600 (2026-06-17). Stock owned by only ~1-in-3 large-caps at ~0.41x relative weight — structurally under-owned (BofA, AMD callback 2026-05-06).
  - **Helios rack-scale architecture is the GPU inflection.** MI400-based **Helios** = 72-GPU rack, scaled-up via **UALink-over-Ethernet** (a modified UALink tunneled over Ethernet on Broadcom Tomahawk switches — not pure UALink yet; AMD intends to migrate to native UALink as switches mature, or stay on Ethernet if the UAL timeline slips) (MS Scale-up primer). AMD open-sourced its Infinity Fabric IP into the UALink consortium (ALAB, MRVL, AWS, Google, Meta, MSFT) as the open alternative to NVLink, and UALink specifies latency in-standard to protect AI-workload performance (MS Asia on AI Factories). JPM raised AMD GPU shipment estimates for 2026-28 on rising MI400-series traction and the OpenAI ~6GW long-term commitment (through 2029), and models AMD AI/liquid-cooled-rack volume growing at >90% CAGR (AMD rack count ~1,490→1,921 units 2027→2028E) (JPM AI Datacenters & Power, 2025-11-04). JPM "AI Capex 2.0" (2026-06-16) pegs AMD DC capex at **$35-40B per GW**, of which **AMD revenue is $15-20B/GW** ($14-18B compute, ~$2B networking), and lifts AMD GPU unit shipments to **0.7M (2025) → 0.6M (2026E) → 1.0M (2027E)** as MI450 ramps; OpenAI's AMD deal (6 GW Instinct MI450, multi-generation) sees first ~1 GW deploy 2H26 alongside NVDA Vera Rubin.
  - GS upgraded to Buy $410 (2026-05-06), BofA Buy $450, MS OW $410, UBS Buy $300 (Arcuri) — broad sell-side reset on the agentic-CPU thesis.
  - **BofA EPS trajectory + ownership lever (Vivek Arya, 2026-05-06 recap):** post-Q1, BofA frames consensus moving to **~$7.25 EPS FY26 (from ~$6.80 going into the call), ~$12+ FY27, ~$15 FY28**, with AMD historically trading 35-40x next-year numbers in growth periods → the **$450-500 target** range; data-center GPU full-year ~$14bn (street ~$3bn Q3 / ~$6bn Q4), with 2027 GPU dispersion of $30-50bn as ~3GW comes into play (~2GW OpenAI+Meta, ~1GW a third customer not yet modeled). Crucially AMD remains **under-owned — only ~1-in-3 large-cap funds hold it at ~0.41x relative weight (~36-60% underweight)** vs NVDA's ~78% ownership at ~market weight — the positioning gap is itself the bull catalyst (BofA "AMD recap," 2026-05-06).
  - **CPU debate, Arcuri's pick:** asked AMD-vs-Intel-vs-ARM, UBS's Arcuri would "rather be in AMD" — core count is decisive in an agentic world (agents spawning agents), and AMD is "way ahead of Intel from a core-count perspective"; he is much less optimistic on Intel's product roadmap (UBS "Arcuri call," 2026-06-15).
    - **★ Now quantified at the rack level by a second house, and AMD is named its TOP CPU PICK (BofA · Vivek Arya, 2026-08-12).** BofA's case is **dual leadership — highest frequency (compute/head node) AND highest core/thread count (agentic AI)** — the only vendor with both. **Frequency:** Turin peaks at **5.0GHz** vs INTC Granite Rapids **3.9GHz** (a 4.3GHz mark appears in the summary) and NVDA Grace at a third-party-benchmarked **~3.35GHz**; the lead is expected to **extend with Venice (2H26)**, making AMD the head-node CPU of choice **outside the NVDA ecosystem** (NVDA's offset is NVLink C2C co-integration vs PCIe). **Core count:** **Venice 256C/512T** vs **Turin 192C/384T**, **INTC Diamond Rapids 192C**, **INTC Granite Rapids 128C/256T**, **NVDA Vera 88C/176T**, **ARM AGI 136C**, **AWS Graviton 5 192C** — "within a fixed power envelope of a server/rack, higher core density increases overall aggregate service throughput." 🔴 **The number that makes it tradeable: modeled rack-level results put EPYC 9965 ("Turin," 192C) at a 2.37x normalized geometric-mean advantage over NVDA Vera (88 Olympus cores), extending to 3.30x with "Venice" (256C); INTC's Xeon 6980P ("Granite Rapids-AP," 128C) turns in 1.46x** in high-core-count agentic workloads. ⚠️ **These are BofA-modeled, not measured — but they are the first per-SKU rack-level spread on this page between AMD, INTC and the NVDA CPU, and they say the agentic-rack gap is ~2-3x, not a generation.** BofA also keeps the x86 leg of the argument: **AMD (with INTC) benefits from x86 security and RAS features, "important in agentic AI workloads"** (BofA · Vivek Arya, 2026-08-12).
  - **Structural CPU lead widens through 2026-28, not just holds (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09):** AMD's per-core performance edge over Intel is already large — 96-core Turin matches 128-core Intel Granite Rapids — and SemiAnalysis's independent view is that "the performance gap between AMD Venice and Intel Diamond Rapids will widen even more in the 2026 to 2028 generation" (p.36). Historical basis for the lead: Rome (2019, Zen2) was first to disaggregate into 8 CCDs + a central I/O die on TSMC N7, and "far outstripped Intel who were still stuck at 28 [cores]" (p.30); the current generation, Turin-Dense (EPYC 9965), scales to 12 Zen5c CCDs / 192 cores / 384 threads (p.31). Venice (256-core, N2) extends this with AMD's first adoption of advanced packaging to link CCDs to the I/O die. AMD's new SP8 platform also refills the enterprise/mainstream segment Intel is vacating by cancelling Diamond Rapids-SP — SemiAnalysis: "AMD will see large share gains in the enterprise markets, a traditional Intel stronghold" (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09).
  - **Caveat — Intel still leads on select ISA features:** AMD CTO Mark Papermaster confirmed that Zen7's new matrix-multiplication engine ("ACE") and AVX10/FRED/ChkTag support will all have already debuted earlier on Intel's Diamond Rapids; Diamond Rapids also supports Advanced Performance Extensions (APX), not present on Zen7 — a rare instance where Intel leads AMD on an ISA feature, tempering the "AMD wins everywhere" framing (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09, p.64).
  - **The harshest independent ROCm critic upgraded AMD — and that is the single most under-priced datapoint of the event (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? — AMD Advancing AI 2026," 2026-07-24).** This page has carried SemiAnalysis as the source of the toughest software criticism on AMD (the "DeepSeek V4 Day 0-43" and vLLM-gap bullets in Bear). They have now moved from **0% chance of closing the gap → (AMD 2.0) a meaningful chance → "a great chance of success," conditional only on two named execution risks** (rack ramp and internal CI capacity — both concrete and both fixable with capital, which AMD has). The supporting evidence they cite is not marketing: **AMD's compiler and most of its kernels are open-sourced, so "they are better positioned for the agentic age"** — the porting-cost argument that rocm.ai and Anthropic's Tom Brown bring-up both point at; **MoRI is called "a real win, built by one tiger team"** (a modular RDMA framework with MoRI-EP for expert dispatch/combine and MoRI-IO for KV transfer, built first-principles by a China-based team, now with official ROCm distributed-SGLang docs for MI355X clusters), and on **iso-interactivity DeepSeek-R1 throughput MoRI SGL outperforms the aggregated SGL config by 2-3x**; and AMD's RIXL fork has now been **fully ported into Nvidia's NIXL KV-cache transfer library** (SemiAnalysis brokered the relationship; the follow-up PR hit **341 Gb/s cross-node RDMA on two MI355X nodes over AMD's own AINIC RoCE NICs with no Mellanox in the path**) — i.e. AMD KV transport is landing inside the frameworks customers actually deploy, not only inside an AMD-only island. Cross-check this against the Bear software bullet: the direction of travel is now positive from the toughest possible source, which is a different thing from the gap being closed.
- **Bear:**
  - **GPU is still "show-me," and Helios system-level risk is the gate.** MI450 is AMD's first rack-scale product; Nvidia took ~5-6 months to ramp first Blackwell. Joe Moore (2026-06-16) "still in a show-me" on GPU with the stock at ~45x next year. Supply-chain checks: MI450 chip performance "quite good" but **UALink system-level issues** have ODMs (Inventec, Wistron, Hon Hai, Supermicro, Sanmina) working hard to resolve and could delay mass production — only a few hundred racks in Q4'26, with the major volume in 2027; cold-plate design still early stage (Jefferies / Q425 Techknowledge tech takeaways, 2025-12-17). JPM (Sur) Neutral PT $385 vs stock ~$511 — explicitly cautious on valuation (2026-06-15).
  - **MI450 supply/AI-Factory economics:** AMD revised up 2027 CoWoS bookings (99K→120K wafers; ~413K MI350 + ~580K MI400/450 chip units; upstream production value ~$23-24B) — but the realized sale "depends how successful Helios system performance is" (Jefferies / Q425 Techknowledge, 2025-12-17). Samsung skeptical AMD can fulfill a 1GW OpenAI tranche near-term on DRAM/HBM constraints (same source). MS AI-Factory TCO work flags inference economics profitable for all major accelerator vendors **except AMD** in its modeled config (MS Asia on AI Factories) — software/system maturity, not silicon, is the swing factor.
  - **HBM supplier-concentration track record — MI300X precedent (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall," 2025-08-11):** MI300X was the first mainstream 12-Hi HBM product, and Samsung was the **sole** HBM supplier for it — a single-source setup that led to above-average GPU failure rates and damaged AMD's relationship with Microsoft (p.46). AMD subsequently shifted a significant share of HBM3E supply away from Samsung toward Micron (p.46), a real mitigant, but the MI300X episode is the cautionary precedent behind ongoing single-supplier/system-integration risk on Helios/MI450 (see bear bullets above on ODM/rack-assembly bottlenecks). Memory-capacity trajectory continues to step up: MI350 ~288GB HBM → MI400 ~432GB (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania," 2026-02-06, p.21); MI400 is also — alongside Nvidia Rubin — first to implement a custom HBM4 base die with off-package LPDDR as a second memory tier (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall," 2025-08-11, p.31). AMD (along with Nvidia and Intel) has in-house capability to design its own custom HBM base dies (same source, p.40).
    - **⚠ SUPERSEDED on the LPDDR half (2026-07-24) — the second memory tier has been cut from the shipping MI455X.** The Aug-2025 statement above (custom HBM4 base die **+ off-package LPDDR as a second tier**) is now only half true. Inspecting the shipping compute-tray design, SemiAnalysis finds **"no longer any direct attached LPDDR running off of each accelerator"** — *"Previous roadmaps had up to 1TB of LPDDR as second tier memory for each MI455X attached on the EAM module, however this has now disappeared"* — plus a **significant DDR5 de-spec** (original 2025 BOM estimate **138TB of DRAM across LPDDR5X and DDR5 MRDIMM**; current estimate is far lower **DDR5 RDIMM** content). Cause given: **tight memory supply**, with de-spec absorbing much of the DRAM price increase. The custom HBM4 base die and the 12-stack/432GB configuration stand; **the LPDDR second-tier claim does not.** Prior value retained in the body above and logged in `## Changelog`. Investment read: the up-to-1TB-per-accelerator memory-capacity differentiator that was part of the MI400 pitch is gone, and what remains of the memory story is HBM capacity — where the counter-move (Nvidia's out-of-spec HBM4 pin speed) has already largely neutralised the bandwidth lead. (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?", 2026-07-24)
  - **OpenAI/Meta warrant dilution:** ~26M shares per gigawatt (~160M shares per 6GW deal); ~50M incremental shares modeled into 2027 and again 2028. Final OpenAI tranche only vests at $600/share (AMD callback, 2026-05-06).
    - **Sharpened (MS · Joseph Moore, 2026-07-24) — the warrants are better understood as GPU cost of goods than as dilution.** MS's post-event framing: *"We have to consider the very large warrant issuance as a **marketing expense**, given that the company is up issuing stock to customers, which **wipes out most of the profits from GPU for the next few years**."* This is a stronger claim than the existing share-count arithmetic: it says the GPU business is roughly **profit-neutral through the ramp** once customer-acquisition consideration is expensed, which is why MS stays EW at $410 despite calling the event "very positive" and raising no objection to the technology. MS does judge the spend worthwhile ("ecosystem support is critical and AMD should do whatever it takes"). **Note the contrast the page should preserve:** the [[ANTHROPIC]] deal was structured as **$5B of AMD equity *into* Anthropic, not warrants** — Barclays read that as a power-shift toward chipmakers and the end of financial incentives (07-22), and UBS as lower-dilution. If Anthropic's structure becomes the template, MS's "wipes out most of the GPU profits" objection decays over time; if OpenAI/Meta-style warrants recur on the next GW customer, it compounds. **Watch which structure the *next* GW-scale deal takes** — that is the single cleanest resolution of this debate.
    - **Independently corroborated and quantified the same day (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?", 2026-07-24) — the number behind MS's sentence.** Both warrants are **up to 160M shares (~10% of shares outstanding each) at a $0.01 strike**, vesting in tranches against the purchase and deployment of **6GW** of Instinct, with **hurdles stepping to $600**. At $600 — the level that satisfies every tranche — **each counterparty records an effective rebate of ~$16.0B/GW** against gross payments of **~$15.2B/GW (OpenAI)** and **~$18.7B/GW (Meta)**, i.e. a **~105% and ~85% partner discount**. SemiAnalysis states it plainly: *"OpenAI could earn money simply by buying MI455X servers and exercising the accompanying warrants,"* and that AMD is *"practically giving away a Helios and 5% extra on top of that"* to OpenAI. That is the arithmetic underneath MS's "wipes out most of the profits from GPU," derived independently and from the customer's side of the trade. Two qualifiers that cut for AMD: the warrants are **an incentive, not a commitment** (orders remain contingent on AMD delivering MI455X silicon and the Helios system, and **OpenAI can back out of the first gigawatt at multiple milestones**), and the rebate only crystallises **if AMD stock reaches $600 and the holders keep the shares** — at $554 spot the final tranche is not yet in the money. SemiAnalysis's own framing of *why* AMD does it is the bull rebuttal: with CUDA on one side and TPU/Trainium on the other, **"AMD is fighting a war on two fronts — almost necessitating the undertaking of such deals."** Note the Meta-specific twist: **Meta's warrant agreement wraps the co-engineered cut-down custom variant**, so AMD is paying a ~$16.0B/GW rebate against the *lower*-content half-size part — the worst combination of the two dynamics on this page.
  - **Customer-concentration / allocation risk:** OpenAI is simultaneously tied to Nvidia, Broadcom, Cerebras and Arm; how it splits 2027-28 share is unknown (expert call, 2026-04-22). Supply chain tight (HBM, CoWoS-L, wafers); 2H Client/Gaming demand hit by memory inflation. BG2 panel (2026-06) skeptical AMD actually lands its on-paper ~6 of ~27 openly-committed GW (~30% of GPU GW): in a watt-constrained world the watt-loser silicon gets deprioritized ("you may save money but you'll have less revenue and lower margins" vs NVDA tokens-per-watt) — flagged as an "extremely unlikely" outcome for AMD to hit that share. Epoch AI's H100-equivalent-compute data (via @SouthernValue95, 2026-06-30) shows NVDA share eroding 2024-25 but **stabilizing and actually gaining vs AMZN Trainium and AMD in 1Q26** — a mild share-momentum negative for AMD's GPU ramp narrative. An AlphaSense expert call (consultant, ex-AWS/Cerebras, 2026-06-30) sees NVDA keeping a strong moat in **training**, but expects **rising inference competition from AMD, Trainium, and Cerebras** — i.e. the near-term competitive threat to Nvidia (and AMD's opening) is concentrated in inference, not training.
    - **Meta's cut-down custom MI455 is now a named threat to the Meta revenue line, and SemiAnalysis has made the same call twice in four days.** Confirmed by Lisa Su on the Q1'26 call and specified by SemiAnalysis: **most of Meta's MI455 orders are a customised cut-down of the full MI455X — compute silicon halved from 8 dies to 4, HBM from 12 stacks to 6, and the HBM4 itself stepped down from 12-Hi to 8-Hi stacks** — raising the CPU:GPU ratio for RecSys and **mirroring Nvidia's Meta-specific "Ariel" GB200 variant** (which SemiAnalysis costed at ~14% worse TCO than a standard NVL72). The governance detail matters: **the configuration was decided by Meta's RecSys infrastructure teams before TBD Lab was formed or could have its say**; given the compute and HBM deficiency in the scale-up domain versus Rubin, **"TBD has no interest in this system, nor is it attractive for external customers."** Conclusion, repeated verbatim from the 07-21 piece: this *"is going to nuke AMD's volume at Meta because TBD will vastly prefer Rubin if the half MI455 design is chosen"* — AMD is urged to work directly with TBD to get the standard part, which *"will be competitive with Nvidia's Vera Rubin."* **Why this is a P&L item and not a technology quibble:** Meta is the counterparty paying **~$18.7B/GW** against a **~$16.0B/GW warrant rebate** (see the warrant sub-point above) — so the highest-rebate customer is also the one taking the lowest-content silicon and the one whose frontier-training org would rather buy Nvidia. The optionality flagged on 07-21 stands: **if Meta reverts to the standard MI455, TBD adoption would drive "significantly more traction and business,"** and SemiAnalysis notes Zuckerberg **"has already begun rapidly exploring changes to their infrastructure strategy org culture"** after their prior article. Watch the Meta SKU decision as a discrete, dateable swing factor on 2027 Meta volume. (SemiAnalysis "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset," 2026-07-21; "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?", 2026-07-24)
  - **Software/inference maturity is the real gap (SemiAnalysis "DeepSeek V4 Day 0-43", 2026-06-09):** MI355X was a "Day-0 disappointment" on DeepSeek V4 — FP8-only (native FP4+FP8 checkpoint didn't work on ROCm), usable only at 1-2 tok/s/user, with AMD SGLang/vLLM *distributed* inference still not working. HaiShaw's team then drove **>100x throughput in <26 days** (real AITER/Triton/TileLang/FlyDSL kernels replacing PyTorch fallbacks; FP4 MoE; MTP by week 4), eventually edging H200 at low interactivity. But SemiAnalysis flags AMD re-focusing on its ATOM engine (**"zero production customers"**) over native vLLM that customers actually use, and ROCm vLLM "lagging far behind" CUDA vLLM — software maturity, plus smaller scale-up domain vs NVL72, remains the constraint (full critique deferred to their "State of AMD 2026"). SemiAnalysis reiterated the gap after fresh NVDA vLLM gains: "Great work by NVIDIA on vLLM performance! AMD still has a lot of catching up to do on certain models for vLLM" (@SemiAnalysis_, 2026-07-13) — the ROCm/vLLM inference-software deficit vs NVDA persists into 2H26.
    - **Sharpened — RISK 2 of the two SemiAnalysis names: AMD is starving its own software organisation of GPUs, and it cost them the Advancing-AI software targets (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?", 2026-07-24).** *"The chief complaint from most AMD engineers internally is that there is a persistent lack of stable GPU clusters for internal software development teams & lack of stable GPU clusters for automated testing CI."* The specifics are hard and dated: **Kubernetes-inferencing Pollara NIC CI still sits at 0% parity with Nvidia's ConnectX nightly CI** (Kubernetes being "the layer that most inference deployments across the world use") — **the plan to reach parity by Advancing AI 2026 was missed on cluster issues**. On vLLM, **gating automated-test progress "has massively regressed"** because **AMD leadership pulled clusters away from the internal vLLM team** to deploy elsewhere, since **AMD's internal capacity over-relies on backstopped temporary clusters** — so the **target of 90% CUDA parity on vLLM gating by Advancing AI was missed**. (Gating tests are the ones that matter: a PR cannot merge unless they pass; SemiAnalysis warns that AMD leadership's *non-gating* pass rate is the number shown to non-technical audiences.) Two structural aggravators: **MI455X (gfx1250) is a completely different ISA from MI355X (gfx950)** — unlike Rubin SM107 vs Blackwell SM100 — so **both codepaths need separate kernels, testing and CI**, and **the MI455X open-source vLLM/SGLang nightly CI target slipped from Advancing AI to October 2026** (AMD trying to pull it back into Aug/Sep). And the capacity gap is an order-of-magnitude problem, not a scheduling one: **even with +2,000 MI355X coming online this month and 6,000 more MI325X/MI355X later this year, AMD remains "more than an order of magnitude less than the stable long term clusters NVIDIA has for internal development"** — a gap that *widens* with agentic coding, because every coding agent (and each of its sub-agents) needs GPUs to test against. Concentration footnote for the risk register: **"most of AMD's best 10x engineers are in Shanghai"** — MoRI collective, UMBP KV-cache offloading and the disaggregated forward-deployed team are all mostly Shanghai-based, i.e. "a lot of the ROCm software stack's most important pieces are built in China." **And the moat itself has moved:** the competitive frontier is no longer single-node CUDA but **disaggregated prefill/decode + WideEP**, where the open CUDA ecosystem has been shipping since **early 2024** while **AMD's first public PD-disagg + WideEP recipes only landed in January 2026**; on Helios (gfx1250) specifically, *"arch enablement and the first half of disaggregation, and no WideEP anywhere."* Net: the software critique on this page is not withdrawn — it is now precisely dated, and the binding constraint has been re-identified as **internal cluster capex allocation**, which is a management choice rather than an engineering limit.
  - **Conor's "trim into the print" trade + supply-chain bottlenecks (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23):** loves AMD long-term but flags AMD is **capacity-constrained at TSMC 3nm** plus two substrate/laminate bottlenecks — **ABF**: Kinsus has ~40% share of AMD general-server ABF but is growing only ~10% (vs buy-side hopes of ~30%, Conor's own ~20% read) on capacity; **CCL**: iTeq has ~60% AMD share at ~75% utilization on a **T-glass shortage**, recovering to 90-95% by Q4 as five new glass suppliers (Taiwan Glass, Grace, Kingboard) come online. Trade: if long AMD, **trim ~20% into the print** — backlog of demand is real but AMD may **guide light** on the constraints, then buy weakness. Server CPU pricing +15-20% again in H2 alongside Intel (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23).
  - **MI450 / Helios rack — chip on track, rack assembly the gate (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23):** MI450 chip production on track; **rack forecast steady at ~30,000 units** for next year (mostly **Meta and OpenAI**), per AVC (liquid cooling) / Delta (power) checks. ODM timing split on Helios rack assembly — one ODM sees delay to **Q1 2026**, another only a minor slip from early to **late Q4 2025**; the swing is whether the rack (not the chip) can be built. Context: vs NVDA's ~70-80K racks, AMD's 30K "a pretty decent number for next year" (Redburn, 2026-06-23).
    - **Sharpened — RISK 1 of the two SemiAnalysis names, and it says exactly *why* the rack is the gate (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?", 2026-07-24).** Redburn's ODM checks said "the rack, not the chip"; SemiAnalysis names the mechanism. *"Helios is currently going through slow rack production ramp due to the lack of a cableless design compared to Rubin Oberon rack."* AMD copied the GB200/GB300 Oberon **flyover-cable** architecture — the very design whose manufacturing pain **pushed Nvidia to a cableless design for Vera Rubin NVL72**; by the time Nvidia announced that pivot, *"it was too late for AMD to implement this design philosophy for MI455X Helios."* Then the SerDes problem on top: **AMD's 200G SerDes struggles with copper-path loss between MI455X and the Tomahawk 6, so up to ~85% of the scale-up links/backplane must be retimed — requiring >550 Broadcom Ethernet retimers per rack**, sitting in the switch tray (on Meta's MI455X deployment specifically, ~85% of scale-up links are retimed). Nvidia's Oberon backplane is **completely passive**. The retimers add cost *and* power *and* assembly time — *"it complicates server assembly as it will be a very tedious task tuning all the retimers when bringing up a rack"* — and SemiAnalysis reports AMD **"running into backplane reliability challenges during rack production ramp hell."** The physical scale of the problem: **10,368 differential pairs of copper cable per rack** (144 DPs per GPU, each 200G lane carried on two pairs), **1,728 flyover cables** routed from the backplane connectors to the TH6 switches, and **up to 12 Molex "Genesis" cables per compute tray** carrying the 128G UALink between MI455X and the Vulcano NIC, crammed into 1U alongside liquid-cooling tubes and power cables — *"all the cables become potential points of failure during assembly."* Connector/cable content is quantified at **$68,928 per rack ($44,352 backplane + $24,576 flyover cables)** — a read-through for the connector complex (see [[APH]] / [[AVGO]] on the retimers). Mitigant worth keeping: with only **6 switch trays vs 9 on GB300 NVL72**, UALoE signal distance is shorter, and the 9-up/9-down compute-tray split equalises path length. Net for the thesis: this is the most specific, most falsifiable version of the Helios-ramp risk on the page — and it is **cost, yield and reliability**, not silicon.
  - Q4 FY26 GM steps down ~100-150bps on MI450 mix (below corporate avg), though management sounded notably less worried about it on the Q1 call than historically.
- **Where the sell-side stands:** Skewed bullish post-Q1. Buy/OW: Citi $575, Bernstein $600, **GS Buy $640 (raised from $450; 32x normalized EPS $20.00; 2027 EPS est $14.50, 13% above Street — GS "2Q Preview," 2026-07-05)**, **BofA Buy $620** (Arya, "Core Wars Q2 Preview," 2026-07-13; 47x CY27E PE — raised from $550, vs the page's prior $500 @ 42x from "AI 2030"; beat/raise expected, MI455X 'Helios' first ship Q3 → ~$6-7bn+/qtr exiting Q4), **Mizuho Outperform $580** (Rakesh, "AI Server Call…", 2026-08-16, px $514.39 — ⚠️ **CUT from $615 with NO stated rationale**, the mark appearing in an industry note's PT table rather than an AMD note; prior value in `## Changelog`. The superseded $615 mark: Vijay Rakesh, "DC Capex Ramping...", 2026-07-11; ~42x F27E EPS, bull $800 / bear $450 — first Mizuho mark on the page, AMD a tech-enabler of the >$1.4T-2028E AI-DC-capex ramp), MS OW $410, **UBS Buy $700** (Arcuri, "Previewing AMD's AI Day," 2026-07-15; 33x C28E EPS $21.11 — raised from $670 on 06-24, itself up from a stale $300; see Changelog), **Jefferies Buy $515** (Blayne Curtis, "Advancing AI 2026 Event Preview," 2026-07-16 — raised from $300), **Barclays OW $665** (Tom O'Malley, 2026-07-22 — reads the Anthropic $5B-equity/no-warrant deal as a power-shift to chipmakers + end of financial incentives). Neutral/cautious: JPM $385; MS Equal-weight (Joe Moore, per MS coverage list 2026-07-13). Debate is no longer the CPU (broad consensus AMD has the stronger roadmap vs Intel) — it's GPU execution + valuation (~45x NTM) + warrant dilution. On memory supply Citi (2026-06-29) ranks **AMD #1 among large caps** (dual-sourced Samsung/Micron) — a favorable read in a DRAM-shortage-constrained world.
  - **Post-"Advancing AI" marks (2026-07-24) — the split is now valuation and warrant accounting, not technology.** **BofA (Vivek Arya) Buy, PO $620 reiterated** (price $554.23): AMD has "**successfully transitioned from a merchant GPU vendor to a full-stack, rack-scale accelerator systems supplier**," deepened a "**multi-generational roster of frontier-lab and hyperscale commitments (OpenAI, Meta, Anthropic, Microsoft, Oracle)**," and become a credible **second-source merchant supplier** as ROCm accelerates — "**establishing as the next-best AI vendor in a $2Tn AI TAM**." **MS (Joseph Moore) Equal-weight, PT $410** — a "very positive event," likes the narrative, but two objections. First, valuation: "the stock simply seems **very expensive vs. peers such as NVIDIA and AVGO, which have a much more entrenched position**, albeit one without AMD's optionality." Second — and this is the sharpest new bear argument on the page — **MS reframes the customer warrants as a marketing expense**: *"We have to consider the very large warrant issuance as a marketing expense, given that the company is up issuing stock to customers, which **wipes out most of the profits from GPU for the next few years**."* MS calls it "a worthwhile expense… ecosystem support is critical and AMD should do whatever it takes" — but the economic point stands and is a materially harder version of the "warrant dilution" risk already on the page. MS also still does not put AMD in the lead this generation: "Helios does seem to be a step forward, though it will take time for the ecosystem to mature… we expect AMD to do well, but **still don't see it in the lead in this generation**." Bernstein marked AMD **(+)** in its 07-24 AM Note (Bernstein · Patafio, 2026-07-24). EnerTuition's framing captures the tape: "**Strong AMD Advancing AI Day Runs Into Bad Macro**" (Beyond The Hype, 2026-07-24).
  - **Positioning/flow context (Jefferies Tech · Jeffrey Favuzza, 2026-07-01):** AMD is the **#2 Nasdaq 100 YTD contributor (16% of the NDX gain)** and **#2 S&P 500 contributor (11% of the SPX gain)** for 1H26 — index performance is now meaningfully AMD-dependent alongside the mega-caps. Traded **pre-market -1.4% on July 1 (~$163mn notional)**. Also flagged as a **12-month relative-high name** per the Rothschild desk, grouped with AMAT, LRCX, KLAC, PANW (Jefferies Tech · Jeffrey Favuzza, 2026-07-01).

## Catalysts / what to watch
- **✅ RESOLVED — "Advancing AI 2026" event (Jul 22-23):** delivered the speculated **new GW-scale customer — Anthropic (2GW MI450, $5B AMD equity investment, from 1H27)** + the **Microsoft Helios** formal commitment (07-20); see the Anthropic-deal note under Current state. Net: bull-case customer expansion confirmed (4 large MI customers now), though the stock reaction was muted vs a customer-expansion that "wasn't expected at the AD" (Barclays). Remaining watch: any explicit MI-revenue guide-up (analysts don't expect one), GPU-share allocation at Anthropic (also on TPU/Trainium/NVDA), and a possible fast-token/fast-decode (Cerebras/d-Matrix) tie-up.
  - **Update 2026-07-24 — scoring the pre-event debate list.** BofA had set five debates going in; four now have answers. **(1) How will Anthropic split share across TPU / Trainium / NVDA / AMD? — STILL OPEN**, and now the single most valuable unanswered question on the page (see [[ANTHROPIC]]). **(2) Fast token / fast decode? — RESOLVED, via partnership:** the **Cerebras disaggregated-inference tie-up** (Helios prompt-processing + Cerebras wafer-scale token generation, ~5x throughput, on Cerebras Cloud later this year), which MS frames as the answer to NVDA's Groq acquisition. **(3) HBM costs / memory allocation? — PARTIALLY:** MI455X carries 432GB HBM4 (vs 288GB on MI355X) and 31TB/rack, so the content is confirmed but the cost/allocation question is unresolved — watch it at the Q2 print. **(4) Will future hyperscale deployments keep requiring warrants or strategic incentives? — NOW THE KEY ECONOMIC QUESTION**, sharpened rather than resolved: Anthropic came as **$5B equity, no warrants**, while OpenAI/Meta were warrant deals, and MS now argues the warrants "wipe out most of the profits from GPU for the next few years." **Watch the structure of the next GW-scale deal.** **(5) Sufficient leading-edge TSMC capacity across MI450 / EPYC / Client? — STILL OPEN** (existing page threads: ~240k 2027 CoWoS allocation, TSMC 3nm constraint, ABF/CCL bottlenecks). **Also resolved:** the TAM raise came in **above** BofA's pre-called $1.5tn/$200bn at **$2Tn/$220bn**, and **no MI-revenue guide-up was given**, as expected. Next hard catalyst: **the Q2 FY26 print in ~10 days.**
- **"Advancing AI" event, Jul 22-23 2026** — next-gen Instinct (MI450/Helios detail), Venice/Verano EPYC, customer engagements; possible additional GPU customer announcement (Anthropic speculated). **Citi preview (07-17):** no new financial model expected (investor day was Nov'25) → dev/technical focus; expect the **Helios platform + MI455X** launch (up to **432GB HBM4 = 1.5x Rubin, 320bn transistors**), a **formal new LLM-maker customer** (ramp weighted 2H27/2028 on HBM supply), **Meta product testimonials**, the **Venice 2nm EPYC** (extends CPU lead over Intel Granite Rapids), and AMD's competitive response to Cerebras/NVDA in ultra-fast inference. Citi sees AMD emerging as a legit #2 GPU source poised to win the lion's share at Meta. (Citi · Atif Malik, 2026-07-17)
- **Venice (Zen 6, 2nm) launch later in 2026** + Verano AI-optimized EPYC; sustainability of >70% server CPU growth into 2H/2027.
- **MI450/Helios production ramp** — initial Q3, significant Q4; lead-customer forecasts already above plan; watch rack-scale revenue recognition (recognized on rack-builder shipment). Key gate is UALink/system-level resolution and ODM yield (only a few hundred racks expected Q4'26; major volume 2027) (Jefferies / Q425 Techknowledge, 2025-12-17). Citi's Asia supply-chain checks corroborate MI450 "on track" for first revenue in Q4 — Blayne Curtis saw two of the three MI450 ODMs (end-customers Meta and Microsoft) and flagged **Inventec building MI450 for Microsoft** as a new data point (Citi "Asia recap," 2026-05-22).
- **Memory/DRAM allocation** — Citi's new "DRAM supply is the ranking metric" frame ranks AMD #1 large-cap on dual Samsung/Micron sourcing (Citi, 2026-06-29); watch HBM/DRAM allocation as a gate on the GPU ramp and 2H Client/Gaming.
- ~~**Q2 FY26 print** (early Aug) against the $11.2B guide; gross-margin trajectory Q3→Q4 with MI450 mix-in.~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-08-04**: rev **$11.54bn** (beat the $11.2bn guide and the $11.3bn Street, **missed the ~$12bn buy-side bogey**), GM **56.2%**, EPS **$1.66**; Q3 guided **$13.0bn / ~56% GM** (above Street $12.5-12.6bn, **below the ~$13.2-13.5bn bogey**). **Stock −7% to −9%.** Gross margin guided **flat q/q on a +13% revenue step** — so the MI450 mix-in shows up as *no* GM expansion, exactly as the "DCAI slightly below corp avg" framing implies. See Current state.
- **🆕 Q2 FY26 call TRANSCRIPT (2026-08-04) — not yet on file.** The Q2'26 mgmt-evolution column and the 2027 framing above are reconstructed from desk notes. Priority reads when it lands: the exact wording behind **"well over 100%"** DC growth in 2027 (the whole re-rating argument hangs on whether that means ~110% or ~150%); the **Helios yield** language; and the **"modify memory footprint"** answer in full context.
- **🆕 The MI455X/Helios yield curve — new and dateable.** Management said yields "will improve in the next few quarters" on "a highly complex product" (Q2 FY26 call, 2026-08-04). Watch Q3/Q4 gross margin for whether the first-generation rack ramp is dilutive beyond the "slightly below corporate average" framing — this is where the SemiAnalysis backplane/retimer yield question (gate 1 below) shows up in the P&L.
- **🆕 AMD vs [[INTC]] as the relative trade (Jefferies, 2026-08-04).** With 2027 largely discounted at current bogeys, Jefferies asks whether INTC has more room over the next few months — same CPU exposure, plus foundry-customer-announcement optionality, and **less crowded positioning**. Track against the [[INTC]] **EMIB-T** datapoints landing the same week (50% cheaper than TSMC CoWoS, >90% yields, mass production 2027, a [[MEDIATEK]] AI-ASIC win, [[AVGO]] and [[META]] evaluating).
- **The two SemiAnalysis gates — now the cleanest dated scorecard on the GPU thesis (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-24).** Their upgrade to "a great chance of success" is explicitly conditional on these, so track them as catalysts rather than as opinions. **(1) Helios rack ramp:** watch for evidence the **>550-retimer-per-rack backplane** and the flyover-cable assembly are yielding — rack output vs the ~30K-unit 2027 forecast, any reported backplane reliability incidents, and whether AMD can get to a cableless design on MI500. **(2) Internal CI/cluster capacity:** three named, dated tests — **Kubernetes-inferencing Pollara NIC CI moving off 0% parity with Nvidia's ConnectX nightly CI**; **vLLM gating parity reaching ~90% of CUDA** (missed at Advancing AI after clusters were pulled from the internal vLLM team); and **MI455X (gfx1250) open-source vLLM/SGLang nightly CI landing — currently slipped to October 2026, with AMD trying to pull it into Aug/Sep**. Also watch the internal fleet: **+2,000 MI355X this month and 6,000 more MI325X/MI355X later this year** still leaves AMD >1 order of magnitude below Nvidia's stable internal dev clusters, so any disclosed step-up in dedicated internal capacity is a direct read on how seriously AMD is treating the binding constraint. A clean way to score the whole thesis: **if both gates close, SemiAnalysis's upgrade converts into share; if either stalls, the 2027 GPU numbers on this page are the ones at risk.**
- **Meta SKU decision — standard MI455X vs the cut-down custom part.** Discrete, dateable, and worth real money: Meta's TBD Lab prefers Rubin over the half-size custom MI455 (4 dies / 6 HBM stacks / 8-Hi HBM4) that Meta's RecSys infra team specified before TBD existed. Watch for any sign Meta migrates its frontier-training volume onto the **standard** MI455X — SemiAnalysis's view is that would drive "significantly more traction and business," while sticking with the custom part will "nuke AMD's volume at Meta." Zuckerberg is reported to be "rapidly exploring changes to their infrastructure strategy org culture." (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-21 / 2026-07-24)
- **MI500 (2027) — the optical claim is now specified, and it is bigger than the page assumed.** The existing page entry (from @sssjeffpu, 07-24) says only that "MI500 introduces optical interconnect." SemiAnalysis puts numbers on it: MI500 should pivot to **co-packaged copper (CPC) + near-packaged optics (NPO)**, with **9 optical engines at 6.4 Tbit/s uni-di each = 57.6T of scale-up bandwidth per package**; each engine **32 lanes of 200 Gbit/s**, each lane **8 lambdas (4 Tx / 4 Rx) at 50G NRZ**, light supplied by **ELSFPs also at 6.4T**. Topology stays a **flat, one-tier network — but across 576 GPUs in 8 racks** (one-hop GPU-to-GPU, limiting tail latency), built on **288 switches using a 115.2T Tomahawk 6 SKU** rather than the 102.4T part in Helios. Note the tension to resolve: elsewhere in the same piece MI500 is described as extending the scale-up domain to **256 GPUs across three racks** where copper gives way to co-packaged copper/optics — vs Jefferies' 07-16 read of native UAL at 256 GPUs/rack. Whichever lands, **576-GPU one-tier optical scale-up would go a long way to retiring the standing "smaller scale-up domain than NVL72" bear point** — and is a large read-through for [[optical-cpo]] / [[ALAB]] / [[AVGO]] (ELSFP light sources, 200G lanes, TH6 115.2T SKU). (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-24)
- **BofA buyside EPS bogey poll (Brian Fenske/Annie Liu, 2026-06-30):** AMD included in a Memory/Optical/AI Semi/HDD out-year (2027-2030) buyside EPS bogey poll launched ahead of Q2 earnings season, grouped with SanDisk, Micron, Lumentum, Nvidia, Broadcom — watch for the poll results as a read on buyside out-year EPS expectations vs the Street.
- China MI308 license dynamics (export-control dependent); HPE/Lenovo Helios systems in 2026; MI500 (cDNA6, 2nm, HBM4e) 2027.
- **Verano (2027) + MI500 rack cycle:** MI500 AI racks launch 2027 paired with a new EPYC "Verano" CPU, just one year after Venice. SemiAnalysis's view (2026-02-09) is that the Zen7 core won't be ready in time (AMD's core-design team runs on a ~2-year cadence) — so Verano will likely be a Venice variant, still on Zen6 cores, probably the same 256-core count. The key differentiator is a new I/O die on 3nm adding PCIe Gen7 + 200G Ethernet SerDes, enabling faster Infinity Fabric links to MI500 GPUs and supporting UALoE (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09).
- **Florence (2028) — the "true" next-gen core:** AMD's next real core redesign, "a true next generation Zen 7 EPYC," debuts 2028 on TSMC A16 (backside power); the alternative path is waiting for A14 (2029) if AMD skips backside power at the A16 node. SemiAnalysis's own estimate (explicitly not AMD guidance) is "around 320 or so" cores (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09).
- **SP8 platform filling Intel's vacated enterprise gap:** new 8-channel Venice SP8 platform (successor to EPYC 8004 "Siena," up to 128 dense Zen6c cores) launches directly into the gap left by Intel's cancellation of its Diamond Rapids-SP mainstream platform — watch for AMD enterprise/mainstream share gains through 2026-27 as this lands (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09).

## Risks
- **Intense competition** vs Intel (improving x86 supply / Diamond Rapids, Coral Rapids pulled to mid-2027) and Nvidia (Vera Rubin), plus merchant/custom Arm and ASIC scale-up (10-K FY25).
- **Foundry concentration on TSMC** for leading-edge HPC/AI wafers + advanced packaging (CoWoS-L) — supply tightness in HBM/wafers a gating risk to the ramp; AMD raised 2027 CoWoS bookings to ~120K wafers but realization is Helios-execution dependent (Jefferies / Q425 Techknowledge, 2025-12-17).
- **Customer concentration** in Data Center AI (OpenAI/Meta) and uncertain hyperscaler allocation of 2027-28 GPU share.
- **Export-control / China** revenue volatility (MI308/MI325 licensing "dynamic").
- **Memory/component cost inflation** dragging 2H Client + Gaming demand; console cycle in year 7 (semi-custom down significant double-digits in 2026).
- Warrant-related share dilution; GPU gross margin dilutive during ramp.
- **Rack power is an unresolved contradiction in the file — and it is the axis the bear case runs on.** SemiAnalysis's bottoms-up build puts **MI455X Helios at ~240kW server / ~257kW all-in per rack vs GB300 NVL72 at ~142kW / ~159kW**, i.e. "even beyond VR NVL72 in power draw" (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?", 2026-07-24) — the opposite sign to **BofA's power-budget read that AMD's MI350X/MI400X platforms are "less power-hungry than Nvidia's"** (BofA "Watts to Tokens," 2026-05-25, already on this page). Both views are retained; neither is discarded. If SemiAnalysis is right, the **BG2 "in a watt-constrained world the watt-loser silicon gets deprioritized"** objection in Bear gets materially stronger and the ~6-of-27-GW share ambition looks harder; if BofA is right, that objection largely dissolves. Note SemiAnalysis's own offsetting point — on *marketed* dense FP8, MI455X still screens competitive on **TFLOPs/W** — so the disagreement may partly be absolute rack power vs power per unit of (marketed) work. **Resolvable with real Helios deployment data from 2H26 and with any customer disclosure of racks-per-MW.**
- **Scale-up switch single-sourcing on Broadcom.** AMD ships the GPU, CPU, NIC and DPU but **no scale-up switch of its own**; because **Broadcom is the only merchant vendor shipping a 100T switch with 200G SerDes, it is AMD's only option**, which puts the Helios roadmap on a partner's execution and, per SemiAnalysis, means **"AMD cannot reach extreme codesign for the complete rack solution."** The over-provisioning cost is visible today (**only 432 of each TH6's 512 200G lanes used**, vs Nvidia's 28.8T NVSwitch dividing evenly across 72 GPUs with zero waste). UALink switches at 115.2T and 57.6T are "on the horizon, but not close enough to anchor this architecture." (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-24; see [[AVGO]] / [[ALAB]])
- **HBM cost pass-through is a 2027 gross-margin risk, not just a supply risk.** SemiAnalysis expects a **significant HBM price increase in 2027** as memory suppliers move to match commodity-DRAM margins; AMD passes it through **at a lower incremental margin than corporate**, because most of its volume goes to large buyers with real negotiating leverage and credible alternatives → **gross-margin drag on the MI455X package**. This is the mechanism behind the existing "Q4 FY26 GM steps down ~100-150bps on MI450 mix" bullet, extended into 2027, and it is partly being pre-absorbed by de-spec (LPDDR5X removed entirely, DDR5 de-specced) rather than by price. (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-24)
- **Internal-CI capacity as an execution risk, and the key-person/geography concentration behind it.** AMD's software quality gates are bottlenecked on GPU clusters it keeps reallocating (see Bear, Risk 2); separately, **"most of AMD's best 10x engineers are in Shanghai"** — the MoRI collective, the UMBP KV-cache offloading team and the disaggregated forward-deployed engineering team are mostly China-based, meaning a large share of the most differentiated parts of the ROCm stack sits in one geography with obvious export-control/geopolitical tail risk. (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-24)
- **Memory-supply lock-up dynamic — flagged as a reader/community comment, NOT SemiAnalysis analyst body content:** a comment from Drew Van Pelt on SemiAnalysis's "Memory Mania" piece (comment section, p.35, 2026-02-06) speculates that AMD (and Meta) could be incentivized to lock up disproportionate long-term memory supply if they perceive a risk of being squeezed out as laggards in the memory-shortage environment — i.e. a fear-driven over-ordering dynamic that could tighten HBM/DRAM allocation further or strand AMD with excess commitments if demand undershoots. This is explicitly a third-party comment, not part of SemiAnalysis's own analysis, and should be weighted accordingly.

| **09-01** | 🔴🔴 **HOT CHIPS — MI400/HELIOS WAS THE MOST COMPREHENSIVE RACK DISCLOSURE AMD HAS MADE, AND JPM SAYS IT DE-RISKS THE INFERENCE POSITION** (JPM · Harlan Sur, *"Hot Chips 2026 takeaway"*, 2026-09-01) | **produto / competicao** | **bull** | ★★ **THE SPECS, AS DISCLOSED: *"**AMD's MI400/Helios rack detail was the MOST COMPREHENSIVE DISCLOSURE TO DATE — 2.9 EXAFLOPS, 31TB HBM4 across 72 GPUs, UALink 128 as an OPEN NVLINK ALTERNATIVE — DE-RISKING its competitive positioning for INFERENCE workloads where Harlan sees AMD GAINING SHARE OFF A LOW BASE.**"*** 🔴 **AND THE FRAMING AROUND IT IS THE REAL BULL CASE, because it says AMD does not need to win a FLOPS race: Harlan's headline finding is that the workload has shifted *"from compute-FLOPS-bound / prefill-dominated NOW TO **MEMORY-BANDWIDTH-BOUND / DECODE-DOMINATED** — a bifurcation that **BROADENS the AI beneficiary set beyond the training-heavy prior cycle**"*, and that *"custom ASICs and merchant GPUs ([[NVDA]], AMD) are seen as **COMPLEMENTARY, NOT SUBSTITUTIVE**, with custom XPU share expected to trend toward **PARITY** with merchant GPU over several years."* ➤ **31TB of HBM4 across 72 GPUs (~430GB/GPU) is a memory-capacity-led design, which is exactly the axis the workload moved to. The independent floor read agrees: *"MI455X uses an advanced process, large-capacity HBM4 and high bandwidth to target MEMORY-INTENSIVE INFERENCE. **Helios makes CPU, GPU and AI NIC into a liquid-cooled full-stack system, emphasising an ETHERNET OPEN ARCHITECTURE to reduce hyperscalers' fear of NVLink lock-in.** Hardware specs are already close [to NVDA's]"* (@QQ_Timmy, corpus, 2026-09-01).** ⚠️ **The unstated gap in both reads is software — neither JPM nor the corpus claims ROCm parity, and "hardware specs already close" is precisely the framing that has been true before without translating into share.** |

| **08-31** | 🔴 **[[AVGO]]'s VMWARE PICKED AMD SILICON FOR ITS FLAGSHIP "AI FACTORY" — AN ENTERPRISE-SOFTWARE VALIDATION THIS PAGE HAS NOT HAD BEFORE** (The Register via MS desk · Tom Wigg; @StockMKTNewz, VMware Explore 2026) | **produto / demanda** | **bull** | ★ *"**AVGO VMware announced [its] own version of an 'AI factory' à la [[NVDA]] that uses AMD INSTINCT GPUs and the OPEN AMD ROCm SOFTWARE ECOSYSTEM.** Per management, **VMware AI Factory is an 'EVOLUTION' of VMware Private AI Foundation with NVDA**"* — announced alongside *"VMware Cloud Foundation Brings Leading AI Models to the Private AI Cloud"*. ➤ 🔴 **THE SIGNIFICANCE IS THE SOFTWARE STACK, NOT THE SILICON: VMware is the default private-cloud control plane for large enterprises, and its flagship AI reference architecture now runs ROCm. That is an ENTERPRISE/on-prem inference channel — the segment where AMD's memory-capacity-per-dollar advantage matters most and where NVDA's CUDA moat is least reinforced by researcher habit. ⚠️ Do not overread: a reference architecture is not a volume commitment, Broadcom explicitly frames it as an "evolution" of the NVDA-based product rather than a replacement, and no customers or units were named.** **A second, smaller datapoint in the same direction: *"**Wafer, an inference provider that uses AMD chips**, has received acquisition offers and **$40 million in new funding**"* at a $200m+ valuation (The Information, 09-01) — the AMD-based inference-service layer is being bid for.** |

Both halves belong on this page. Do NOT carry this as a TPU win. Watch for what it actually is — the plausible readings are general-purpose accelerator capacity or a networking/CPU engagement.** |

Especially with the [[NVDA]] risk event cleared this week and some very punchy FY28 growth numbers from the company. **Rankie flagged in his feedback email that AMD was coming up A LOT as SOUNDING BEST at the conference.** Does this suggest there is **growing appetite to own more core compute names**?"* (Jefferies, 2026-08-31).** ➜ **Two years of this cycle have been traded as a hunt for the next bottleneck (HBM, CoWoS, substrate, power, optics). Jefferies' observation is that the hunt has run out of undiscovered bottlenecks — and the default rotation from there is back into core compute, where AMD is the highest-beta non-NVIDIA name. **A sentiment/positioning datapoint, not a fundamental one, but it is the kind that front-runs multiple expansion.**

COMPLETE forwarded-email edition; the separately-slugged PDF is a truncated copy of the same article, not a second source.**) ⚠️ **The AMD host-CPU socket from this article is ALREADY logged under `## Current state` (08-25) — this row carries the COMPETITIVE half, which is a different and much less comfortable read.** | competicao / produto | **bear** | 🔴🔴 **THE HARSHEST PARAGRAPH WRITTEN ABOUT AMD's ACCELERATOR PROGRAMME THIS YEAR, AND IT COMES FROM A SHOP THAT HAS BEEN GENEROUS TO AMD ON SOFTWARE: *"Meanwhile at AMD, alarm bells should be going off. [[OPENAI]] could potentially own more than 10% of AMD if OpenAI and its partners buy 6GW of AMD GPUs. However, a custom silicon program has delivered in 9 months something that AMD has not been able to figure out in the 4 years since the ChatGPT moment. Namely, outperforming NVIDIA GPUs. Time for the AMD kernel engineering team to start burning more tokens in pursuit of performance."*** ⚠️⚠️ **THE NORMALISATION MUST TRAVEL WITH THE CLAIM: "outperforming" here means *"token throughput per All-in utility MW"* — tokens per megawatt, i.e. tokens per joule. NOT tokens/$, NOT per-GPU, NOT absolute throughput. On tokens/$ Jalapeño is only *"head-to-head"* with Vera Rubin, and no AMD perf/$ comparison is published in this article at all.** **What was actually tested: SemiAnalysis was invited into OpenAI's lab and ran its InferenceX suite on A0 silicon, concluding Jalapeño beat *"every Nvidia, AMD, and Google chip we have been able to test on multiple top open source models."* ⚠️ SemiAnalysis's own three caveats apply in full and are quoted on the [[NVDA]] page: OpenAI-supplied numbers with no full InferenceX suite and no AgentX runs; Blackwell being the wrong comparator; and non-frontier test models — *"NVIDIA and AMD have published results on larger models such as DeepSeek V4 Pro and Kimi K3, using AgentX."*** 🔴 **THE DIVERSIFICATION SEQUENCE, WHICH IS THE STRATEGIC POINT FOR THIS PAGE: *"OpenAI, which is one of the single largest end users of Nvidia GPUs, is now following [[ANTHROPIC]]'s footsteps in diversifying its compute stack. First with the AMD GPU deal, next with Trainium, and now its own ASICs."*** ➜ **AMD was step ONE of a three-step diversification, not the destination. The 6GW deal and the warrants are not evidence of a durable second-source position if the customer's own silicon is the endpoint.** ✅ **THE HONEST OFFSET, RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THE DEMAND CASE AND THE SAME ARTICLE MAKES IT: *"demand for OpenAI's Codex product is so strong that it is making every silicon partner look good. If growth continues at the current trajectory, OpenAI will need all the NVIDIA, AMD, TPU, Trainium, Cerebras, and Jalapeño chips they can get their hands on."*** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_25_ENC_OpenAI_Jalape%C3%B1o_Better_Than_Nvidia_Blackwell_email.html) · [archived (truncated) PDF copy](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_on_Jalapeno.html) |
| 08-24 | 🔴🔴 **Morgan Stanley Asia-Pacific Technology · Charlie Chan / Daniel Yen / Daisy Dai / Tiffany Yeh (w/ Lucas Wang, Ethan Jia, Henry Zhao) — "AI Supply Chain: Google ASIC, who gets what for design services?"** (**no AMD rating or PT in this note — AMD is Joe Moore's coverage in the US**) | supply / produto | **bull** | 🔴🔴 **THE SINGLE MOST BULLISH SUPPLY DATAPOINT ON THIS PAGE, AND IT IS A CAPACITY BOOKING RATHER THAN A FORECAST: AMD's 2027e CoWoS allocation goes to 530k wafers / 20% of a 2,694k industry total, from 130k / 9% in 2026e — +308% y/y, the fastest growth of any customer in the table, against [[NVDA]] +57% (1,222k, 45%) and [[AVGO]] +61% (484k, 18%). [[MRVL]] +276% (64k) is the only comparable rate and off a far smaller base.** ⚠️ **This is CoWoS ALLOCATION — booked packaging capacity, not shipped units and not revenue. It is the strongest available leading indicator of the MI450/Helios ramp, and it is exactly the kind of number that goes stale if allocation is later handed back.** **The 2027e programme detail (Exhibits 11–12), which is net-new to this page at this granularity: MI455 — 157k CoWoS wafers at 7 chips/wafer ⇒ 1,099k units; 432GB of HBM4 12-hi (Samsung/Micron); 850mm² 2nm compute die ×2; ~116k wafers consumed; US$3.49bn of wafer TAM at a $30,000 2nm wafer price. MI450 (for [[META]]) — 35k wafers ⇒ 490k units; 216GB HBM4 12-hi; 2nm; US$0.78bn. MI500 (Arcadia) — 24k wafers ⇒ 96k units; 768GB HBM4e 12-hi; 2nm; US$0.31bn. Venice CPU — 270k wafers at 21 chips/wafer ⇒ 5,670k units; 155mm² 2nm die ×8; ~227k wafers; US$6.80bn — the largest single AMD wafer-TAM line in the table.** **For 2026e by contrast: MI455 65k wafers ⇒ 455k units; MI350/375 7k ⇒ 84k; MI300 3k ⇒ 36k; Venice 50k ⇒ 1,050k.** ➜ **Read the Venice line: on MS Asia's own numbers AMD's 2027 CPU wafer TAM is roughly TWICE its MI455 GPU wafer TAM. That is a quantified version of the "CPU is the underappreciated upside" bull bullet already on this page.** **Separately, and worth a flag rather than a thesis: MS Asia relays that *"AMD may also be involved in TPUv10"*, sourced to Joe Moore's 08-19 [[MRVL]]/Google note.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/GLOBAL_20260824_1435.html) |

Risks as printed: *"AMD remains a distant second supplier to [[NVDA]] in the AI GPU race, with key software stack ROCm trailing CUDA."* 🔴 **The read-across that matters more than the PT: memory de-spec is framed as a VOLUME UNLOCK — SA argues OEMs *"could cap future HBM content at 4-8Hi"* and the HBM *"trade ratio 3:1 to 5:1 DRAM"* constraint is diminishing, so *"the lower DRAM/GPU content could allow ~20-30% more GPU/ASIC shipments"* and better GPU/ASIC margins. ⚠️ This lands directly on the "AMD is FLEXIBLE ON HBM CONFIGURATIONS" bullet already dated 08-25 in `## Current state`: a third party is now arguing that flexibility is worth incremental UNITS, not just goodwill.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/SemiAnalysis__Memory_De-Spec_WFE_800V_Uncertainty_with_Cop.html) |

| **08-05** | **🔴 UBS · Timothy Arcuri — POST-EARNINGS FOLLOW-UP CALL (recorded debrief, the single densest source in this window)** | **demanda / margem** | **mixed** | **★ THE MOST DECISION-RELEVANT SOURCE OF THE DAY-2 FLOW, because it is a Q&A debrief rather than a note and it puts numbers on four things the published research left vague.** **(1) DC GPU trajectory, quantified: "AMD reported Data Center GPU revenue of $2.75 BILLION for the June quarter, up from $2.45 BILLION in March. The 2026 full-year target is 'BETTER THAN $15 BILLION,' implying a Q4 2026 RUN RATE OF AT LEAST $6.5 BILLION, well above prior street expectations." [BULL]** — that is the first clean bridge on this page from the reported quarter to the implied exit rate. **(2) The buy-side already knew: "While AMD's Q4 2026 guidance implies revenue in the $17 BILLION RANGE (vs street at $15.3–15.7 billion), the analyst notes the BUY-SIDE LARGELY ALREADY ANTICIPATED THIS UPSIDE, drawing parallels to past Nvidia dynamics where the SELL-SIDE LAGGED." [NEUTRAL]** — the cleanest explanation on the page for a beat-and-raise that traded −7/−9%. **(3) 🔴 SERVER CPU GUIDANCE CALLED TOO CONSERVATIVE, with a supply mechanism: "AMD guided Server CPU growth at 70% for 2027, but the analyst believes this is OVERLY CONSERVATIVE, with SUPPLY-CHAIN CHECKS INDICATING POTENTIAL FOR 2X+ GROWTH, DRIVEN BY AN INFLECTION IN [[TSM]] N2 ALLOCATION." [BULL]** — this is the most actionable upside claim in the window and it is checkable via N2 allocation. **(4) ⚠️ THE MARGIN COST OF THE MIX, stated plainly: "Despite robust growth, AMD's gross margin is expected to remain AROUND 55% THROUGH 2027. This is due to higher growth dollars coming from LOWER-MARGIN GPU ($30 BILLION INCREMENTAL) vs. HIGHER-MARGIN CPU ($20 BILLION INCREMENTAL)." [BEAR]** — the revenue mix that drives the bull case is the same mix that caps the margin, quantified for the first time. **(5) ⚠️ WARRANT DILUTION TIMING: "Any significant warrant dilution is NOT EXPECTED UNTIL THE BACK HALF OF 2027, contingent on shipping full gigawatt chunks and meeting commercial conditions. This remains a 'NON-EXACT SCIENCE' for modeling." [NEUTRAL]** — relevant because MS's sharpest bear line on this page is that the warrants are "a marketing expense… which wipes out most of the profits from GPU for the next few years"; Arcuri's timing puts that beyond the 2027 numbers everyone is now debating. **Bonus, non-AMD but same call — [[AEIS]]/Integrus read: the Colorado facility "will be a gross-margin headwind even THROUGH 2027, NOT BREAKING EVEN UNTIL LATE 2027 / H1 2028. A POTENTIAL WRITE-DOWN IS BEING CONSIDERED" [BEAR], against strong market tailwinds (mid-to-high-teens growth, possibly ~20%, on 75% exposure to a low-to-mid-teens MSI market and 25% to a 30–35%-growth WFE capex market) [BULL]. Also [[LRCX]]-related: UCT undergrew Lam systems shipments in Q3 after outgrowing in Q2 — a timing lag, not a demand signal.** (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, post-earnings follow-up call, 2026-08-05) |
| **08-05** | **🔴 Bernstein · Stacy Rasgon — "AMD (defend)" · OUTPERFORM, PT $600 → $650** | **valuation** | **bull** | ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT: Bernstein PT $600 (06-17) → $650, Outperform maintained.** **The argument is a framing attack on the market's reaction, and it is the sharpest version of it: "Stacy believes investors are increasingly focused on THE WRONG DEBATE, with another strong quarter highlighting that EXECUTION CONTINUES TO IMPROVE even as expectations around AI remain exceptionally high. The company also raised its outlook into the back half of the year, with Q3 guidance ahead of consensus and continued strength expected across Datacenter and Embedded. While investors remain focused on AI GPU trajectories and elevated spending levels, Stacy's view is that THE CORE BUSINESS CONTINUES TO STRENGTHEN through server share gains, commercial PC adoption, and improving Datacenter economics. THE STOCK REACTION IS INCREASINGLY LESS ABOUT FUNDAMENTALS AND MORE ABOUT WHETHER AI CAN CONTINUE TO OUTPACE ALREADY ELEVATED EXPECTATIONS."** (Bernstein AM Note via Christopher Brady, 2026-08-05) |
| **08-05** | **BofA · Vivek Arya — "Accelerating growth in expanding AI TAM, ignore quarterly noise" · BUY, PO $620 REITERATED (valuation basis CHANGED)** | **valuation** | **bull** | **Large estimate raise, PO held, and — the item worth logging — the multiple was RE-BASED onto a different metric.** **Estimates: "we raise CY26/27/28E EPS by +2%/+20%/+34% to $7.62/$15.88/$23.88 with CY30E likely IN EXCESS OF $30/sh or ~4x current levels."** **The path: "AMD DC sales could reach ~$170bn BY CY30E from just ~$32bn IN CY26E, growing at >50% CAGR," at "just 7–8% AI GPU share and 25–30% CPU share," outgrowing management's raised $2Tn total AI-compute TAM (incl. $1.4Tn+ accelerators, $220bn CPUs) growing ~+40% CAGR.** ⚠️ **THE VALUATION-BASIS CHANGE IS MATERIAL AND SHOULD NOT BE READ AS "PO UNCHANGED": "Reiterate $620 PO, NOW ON 27x CY28E INCLUDING SBC (vs 47x CY27E EXCLUDING SBC prior), in-line with key AI compute peers now including SBC in their EPS forecasts, and as more of the growth upside is in the out years. Our 27x target multiple is still in-line with AMD's historical 5-yr fwd median of 27–28x."** So the same $620 now rests on a year-later, SBC-burdened base at roughly half the headline multiple — a genuine de-rating of the valuation framework dressed as continuity. **On the reaction: "While the magnitude of near-term beat/raise is modest, and likely to pressure the stock near-term, we think it's IMMATERIAL as flagship Helios full-scale rack system ramp begins in earnest ONLY FROM Q4."** **Risks named: "rising exposure to UNPROFITABLE FRONTIER LABS," competition at concentrated customers, rack-scale execution — with the mitigation that "AMD's deployment at PUBLIC HYPERSCALERS can help generate sustainable traction."** (BofA · Vivek Arya, 2026-08-04/05) |

Muse exchange floor of ~$30bn DCAI; IR is now sanctioning ~$70bn+ total DC for 2027, i.e. +130%. Logged as an IR-sourced desk relay, NOT as guidance — but it is the number the buy-side will hold management to.** **(2) ⚠️ THE POSITIONING RISK, and it is new language on this page: "But also seeing an INCREASING CHORUS ABOUT AMD NOW POTENTIALLY BEING THE NEXT FUNDING SHORT — and not just paired vs [[INTC]], but also vs [[NVDA]] AND ALL SEMIS."** After a +132–145% YTD move, that is a mechanical de-rating risk independent of fundamentals — the mirror image of the crowded-short unwind that just happened at [[MCHP]]. **Jefferies' own numbers: Blayne Curtis TP $640 → $650, 2026E rev/EPS +3%/+3%, 2027E +29%/+42%, 2027E/28E EPS $18.18/$24.63; "he sees mgmt's initial framework for 2027E as an ENCOURAGING BASELINE, with potential for AI GPU business to hit $40BN+ NEXT YEAR (vs our prior estimates of $30bn) alongside server revs of $30BN+."** (Jefferies desk · Rankie Wong, "Reading the Tech Leaves," 2026-08-05) |

Logged as an unaffiliated specialist view, not a bank rating.** **(2) THE GUIDANCE-CRAFT CRITIQUE: EnerTuition/"Beyond The Hype" — "AMD delivered a TRIPLE BEAT (revenues, EPS, and guidance)… While the magnitude of the beats was smaller than some expectations, AMD's story has been and is about MI450 and Venice ramps and in context the smaller beats were LARGELY IRRELEVANT. | 08-05 | **MS · Joseph Moore — "AMD: 2H a little better, 2027 significantly better" · EQUAL-WEIGHT, PT $410 → $465** | guidance | **mixed** | ⚠️ **PT RAISED to $465 from $410 with EQUAL-WEIGHT MAINTAINED. AlphaSignals: thesis "UNCHANGED / Modest upside / Modest revision higher." CY26/CY27 EPS +3%/+9%.** *"Near term numbers not surprising but commentary about 2027 data center revenue 'SIGNIFICANTLY MORE THAN DOUBLING' is certainly positive commentary vs. OUR 80% ESTIMATES. #s higher; we like the revenue growth BUT THE WARRANT ISSUANCE TEMPERS OUR ENTHUSIASM."* ⚠️ **★ The most useful number MS publishes, because it puts a run-rate on the un-disclosed AI GPU line: "AMD does not report the AI GPU segment, but we think it was up to about a ~$10.4BN RUN RATE IN Q2 and model it EXITING 2026 AT ABOVE $25BN, SURPASSING CPU as the largest piece of the datacenter business. Management expects well north of 100% growth in 2027, with participation from each of the three large committed customers; WE MODEL $38BN BUT WOULD THINK EXPECTATIONS ARE AROUND $45BN (~1 GW WITH EACH CUSTOMER AT $15BN PER GW)."*** ⚠️ **That $45bn "expectations" figure is the cleanest statement yet of the buy-side 2027 DCAI bar on this page — and note it is derived from the very ~3GW × $15bn/GW construction that Lisa Su declined to confirm on the call (she answered only the $/GW half). So the bogey rests on a premise management has not endorsed.** On server CPU: *"Server continues to be exceptionally strong, +70% y/y, with AMD now having LINE OF SIGHT TO SIMILAR OR HIGHER LEVELS OF GROWTH OVER THE NEXT 6 QUARTERS… But it's clear SUPPLY IS PUTTING A CEILING ON NEAR-TERM NUMBERS at least to some degree."* ⚠️ **The durability caveat, and it is a genuine bear argument rather than a valuation one: "with the sudden spike in demand and SEVERAL PARTICIPANTS ALL LOOKING TO USE THAT AS AN OPPORTUNITY TO GAIN SHARE, we are NOT YET CONVINCED THIS IS DURABLE FOR MULTIPLE YEARS. Server CPUs were historically a slow growth TAM, but as agentic needs have accelerated, EACH OF [[INTC]], AMD, [[ARM]], AWS, Axion and [[NVDA]] are ramping products aggressively with the intent to gain share. UNITS AT INTEL HAVE GONE FROM −5% Y/Y IN Q1 TO +9% Y/Y IN Q2, so the trajectory of supply is improving quickly."** Why it stays EW: *"we like the server position and inroads in GPU but THE PREMIUM TO PEERS EVEN LOOKING THROUGH THE WARRANTS limits upside potential."* Also flags **"GPU upside more limited in front of a Helios launch that is mostly in Q4"** and that **"given the warrant arrangements, it will be CHALLENGING TO ASSESS THE TRUE LEVEL OF CUSTOMER BUY-IN even as revenue ramps more materially next year."** (MS · Joseph Moore / Mason Wayne / Shane Brett / Ella Tulchinsky / Nicole Kozhukhov, 2026-08-05) |

| 08-05 | **UBS · Timothy Arcuri (hosted by Robert Ruple) — the "ALAB_AMD_ENTG Recap Call" flagged in the row above, PRIMARY TRANSCRIPT** | guidance | **bull, with two margin/ASP caveats** | **★ The most model-specific post-print source on this page, and it is triangulated rather than inferred: Arcuri says he took the numbers off the group callback AND a separate conversation with Matt (Ramsay, IR) "last night post call," with Jean Hu confirming the segment split. "I have triangulated all this stuff in my model."** **THE UNDISCLOSED GPU LINE, quantified: "the DATA CENTER GPU NUMBER FOR THE JUNE QUARTER WAS $2.75BN, up from $2.45BN [March]." For the full 2026 "he said they hope to do better than $15BN. So $15bn is sort of THE BAR, WHICH WOULD IMPLY THAT $6.5BN IS A GOOD BASELINE FOR THE DECEMBER QUARTER."** ⚠️ **Note the basis clash with MS the same day: MS puts the AI GPU line at a "~$10.4bn RUN RATE in Q2" (annualised) — Arcuri's $2.75bn is the actual quarter, i.e. an ~$11bn run rate. The two are consistent once annualised; do not read $2.75bn against $10.4bn.** **Q3 split, company-confirmed: "is it about equal? So they're BOTH GROWING ABOUT $750 MILLION [q/q]. And Jean said, yeah, that's about right."** 🔴 **ON THE 2027 NUMBER — this is the other side of the C.J. Muse exchange logged in the 08-04 transcript row. "Someone threw a number out of like $30 billion next year… that is NOT EVEN LIKE A REALISTIC NUMBER… that's like WAY, WAY TOO LOW. I think WE'RE ALL THINKING SOMEWHERE IN THE MID-40s. And they wouldn't confirm or deny that number."** ➤ **So the $30bn Muse put to Lisa Su (answered "your data center AI number is probably too low") is independently called "way too low" by UBS, who sits at mid-$40s — versus MS/Barclays at ~$38bn, Jefferies $40bn+, Street $33.2bn and the ~$45bn buy-side bogey. UBS is at the buy-side bogey, not the sell-side centre of gravity.** **THE 2027 DC BRIDGE: "there are MORE GROWTH DOLLARS coming next year in GPU than in CPU. It's not 2x… it's going to be $30BN OF INCREMENTAL GPU DOLLARS in data center and… MORE LIKE $20[BN] COMING IN CPU."** ⚠️ **He separately states a "NORTH STAR" of "close to $70 BILLION" for server/data-centre revenue, verbally attached to "this year" — the arithmetic only works for 2027 ($15bn+$30bn GPU ≈ mid-40s, plus ~$20-25bn CPU ≈ $70bn), and it lands exactly on the $70bn 2027 DC target Jefferies re-affirms on 08-07. Carried as a 2027 figure with the misstatement flagged, NOT as a 2026 number.** 🔴 **THE MARGIN CAVEAT, and it is BELOW where the mix optimists sit: because the incremental GPU dollars (below corporate average) outrun the incremental CPU dollars (way above corporate average) by 30 to 20, "he said you should KEEP GROSS MARGIN SORT OF IN THAT 55% RANGE. So gross margin in December is going to go FROM 56 IN SEPTEMBER DOWN TO 55 IN DECEMBER and… just sort of keep it in that 55 range THROUGH NEXT YEAR."** ⚠️ **A flat ~55% GM through 2027 on a revenue base that roughly doubles is the arithmetic version of the "fastest-growing line is the least margin-accretive" nuance in the Signal table above — now with a number attached. Opex: "up 35% this year year over year. IT WILL GROW A SIMILAR NUMBER NEXT YEAR."** 🔴 **THE ASP CAVEAT — a direct challenge to the Venice-ASP leg of the Jefferies bull case: "MOST OF THE GROWTH CONTINUES TO BE IN UNITS, NOT IN ASP… two-thirds of the year-over-year growth is in units and only one-third is coming in ASP. So ASP is probably UP 25% Y/Y… KEEP YOUR PRICING EXPECTATIONS IN CHECK because ASP is being driven mostly by CORE COUNT… they're shipping mostly into cloud and the hyperscalers, and IT'S VERY DIFFICULT FOR THEM TO RAISE PRICES ON THOSE CUSTOMERS."** ✅ **THE SUPPLY OFFSET, and it is the reason he is above the company on server: on the +70% 2027 server-CPU number — "that's NOT THAT GREAT OF A NUMBER. I'm already at UP 2X, MORE THAN UP 2X, just looking in the supply chain and looking at the wafers and looking at the capacity allocation." Management's own explanation, verbatim: "we like to give a number THAT WE CAN WALK UP." ➤ Log that against the "management chose vagueness" reading in the Signal table — UBS says the 2027 marks are deliberately set to be raised, not conservative by accident.** **And the supply mechanism is named: "ALMOST ALL OF THE RECENT TSMC CAPEX RAISE IS N2-RELATED AND LARGELY TO SUPPORT AMD. LISA WENT THERE THREE TIMES IN THE LAST FOUR MONTHS. I think they're finally getting a HUGE INFLECTION IN N2 ALLOCATION."** (cross-file [[TSM]]) **ON THE WARRANTS — the item MS said "tempers our enthusiasm": "if there is any warrant vesting IT WOULD BE IN THE BACK HALF OF '27, because YOU HAVE TO SHIP THE FULL GIGAWATT CHUNK and there are commercial conditions that have to be met… they'll give more color on it" then.** ⚠️ **NEW RISK, and it is specific: like [[NVDA]], AMD ships compute boards ahead of racks — "there is some risk that maybe they CAN'T SHIP AS MANY COMPUTE BOARDS IN… a question of how many compute boards they can ship and HOW MUCH THE ODMs OR THE CUSTOMERS ARE PREPARED TO FINANCE. Will they wait for the racks to be shipped?" That is the execution risk sitting under the $6.5bn Dec-Q DC GPU baseline.** **Client: "yes, you're going to have a SUB-SEASONAL DECEMBER this year," market down in 2027 but AMD outperforms — model client+gaming down y/y next year. Capex up "to invest in BACK END to support the growth in server."** **Positioning, a fourth independent desk on the expects-miss: "the gap between the SELL SIDE AND THE BUY SIDE on this stock is VERY LARGE… that's the way it used to be for Nvidia. Most people I talk to were already thinking Q4 is going to be somewhere in the $17 BILLION range" — versus a Street at "$15.7 or $15.3." So the breadcrumbs imply a Q4 "way, way above the street" and merely in line with the buy side.** ⚠️ Machine transcript; the host addresses Arcuri as "Dan"/"Sam" in places (ASR), UBS sales is Robert Ruple. OUTPERFORM, PT CUT $615 → $580** (px $514.39) | **valuation / supply** | **mixed** | ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT, WITH THE CAVEAT ATTACHED: the PT moves $615 → $580 and the rating stays Outperform, but the note gives NO AMD-specific rationale — the mark appears in the PT/rating table of an AI-server INDUSTRY note, with no revised multiple or EPS.
