| **08-14** | **Hugging Face · Adina Yakefu / Apolinário / Irene Solaiman — "State of Open Models: Summer 2026 Observations"** (platform-operator data; **no rating, no PT, not a bank**) | **produto / competicao** | **bull (ecosystem only)** | 🔴 **THE FIRST THIRD-PARTY, COMPARATIVE, QUANTITATIVE DATAPOINT ON THE ROCm-ADOPTION DEBATE: "the two organizations publishing the most new open models this year are also the companies making the hardware: AMD and NVIDIA. Each released more than 200 new model repositories, far ahead of the rest of the field, with LiquidAI ranking third at around 100. Hardware vendors have realized that open models are a way to sell chips."** On AMD specifically: **"AMD contributed many conversions… it enables trillion-parameter models to run efficiently on U.S. hardware."** ➤ **The conversions line is the operative one — converting third-party frontier models (incl. trillion-parameter MoEs) to run on AMD silicon is precisely how the "15 to 20% CUDA→ROCm memory overhead" the 07-30 StoneX expert quantified gets amortised across an ecosystem instead of paid per customer.** Until now the page's ROCm evidence was AMD's own (rocm.ai, six-week cadence, 38% MiniMax M3, the Claude co-development) or a single customer's ([[ANTHROPIC]]/Tom Brown on MI355); this is the platform operator's own count. ⚠️ **Two counterweights from the same source, and they matter: the Hub is brutally concentrated — ~85.6% of models have <200 lifetime downloads and 1.5% of repos take 99.2% of downloads — so repository COUNT is a supply metric, not adoption; and only 3% of 2026 download volume goes above 70B (models under 1B take 83% of all-time downloads).** ➤ **Carry as an ecosystem/moat datapoint only. Never as revenue, share, or usage.** (Hugging Face, 2026-08-14) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/State_of_Open_Models__Summer_2026_Observations.html) |

Those pages have now been patched by hand.** | **produto / supply** | **mixed** | ★ **THE HELIOS CONTENT IS THE REASON THIS FILE MATTERS, AND THE HONEST HEADLINE IS THAT THE ASIA CHANNEL HAS LITTLE TO SAY YET: *"To be fair we didn't hear much feedback on this project."*** What the analyst WILL commit to: **① SUPPLY CHAIN — AMD "is trying to leverage a new US supplier, but it looks like the original Taiwanese supplier Wistron needs to step up and try to help smooth the production cycle."** **② CUSTOMERS — "the two major projects are [[ORCL]] and [[META]]," both in progress.** **③ TIMING — "mass production could be around Q1 next year" (1Q27), "slightly after Nvidia"; MODULE-level volume possible in 2H26; RACK-level shipment "will be more like first half next year."** The reason for the gap between module and rack is stated: *"remember this is a new system, a new design for AMD, so at the downstream level it will take more time to complete the rack-level verification and do more customization."* 🔴 **④ THE REFUSED CONCLUSION, which is the most useful part because it is the question the Street keeps answering without evidence. Asked directly whether Sanmina/ZT is losing share to Wistron: *"Taiwanese supply chain has been a good partner for AMD… if AMD's existing supply chain has some issue, then Taiwan supply chain can just step up and help them solve the issue. Morgan Asia hardware/supply-chain call, 2026-08-20) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_20_nw_jpm_20_ago_26.html) |

**Quarter synthesis (Q2 FY26 window, opening):** AMD beat the Street on every line and guided above it, **and the stock fell 7-9% — because the Street stopped being the bar some time before the print.** Three independent desks put the buy-side bogeys at **~$12bn for the quarter (printed $11.54bn)** and **~$13.2-13.5bn for the Q3 guide (guided $13.0bn)**, into a tape where SOX had run **+15% in four sessions** and AMD itself was **+7% on the day of the print**. The unanimous read — GS, Barclays, Jefferies, TMTB — is that this is an **expectations reset, not a thesis break**, and the evidence for that is that **sell-side numbers go up while buy-side numbers stay flat or trim slightly**: the two sides were never using the same model. ⚠️ **What genuinely disappointed was communication, not substance.** Management's 2027 framing — DC **"well over +100%"**, server CPU **+70%+**, DCAI **"well over +100%"** — is above consensus (+88%) but **below the ~150% (some 175-200%) the buy-side had already settled on**, and it was delivered in adjectives rather than numbers, which Jefferies calls **"a lot of guesswork and mental gymnastics."** Meanwhile the *physical* evidence points to the higher number: **advanced-packaging allocation revised up to 300k for 2027E (+260% y/y)**, Helios demand **"ahead of volume forecasts,"** and Venice with **"customer demand stronger than for any prior generation."** ⚠️ **Two new negatives worth carrying rather than netting.** (1) **Helios yields**: management volunteered that yields "will improve in the next few quarters" on a "highly complex product" — the first on-record admission of a first-generation ramp cost, and it sits against SemiAnalysis's 07-24 finding that the software CI dates had already slipped ~1-2 quarters. (2) **The memory de-spec door is now open from the demand side**: AMD has 2027 HBM allocation locked *and* said customers can "modify memory footprint if TCO is not as significant" — read that as a negative for HBM bit demand ([[MU]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SAMSUNG]]) rather than for AMD. The forward question the desks are actually asking is no longer about execution: **with 2027 largely discounted at these bogeys, is [[INTC]] the better-positioned CPU expression on positioning and foundry optionality?** **08-14/08-16 add one ecosystem positive and one unexplained PT cut, and they do not cancel.** ⚠️ **Mizuho cut AMD to Outperform $580 from $615 with no AMD-specific rationale** — the mark sits in the PT table of an AI-server industry note, the second time this quarter this house has moved a PT inside an industry piece rather than a covered-name note (it did the same to [[INTC]] on 08-09). **Logged as a mark, not as a view**, and it leaves the ladder still clustered high (BofA $620 / GS $640 / Bernstein $600 / Mizuho $580 / Citi $575). Against it, the same note is **constructive on AMD's packaging supply**: ASX/AMKR doubling CoWoS gives a name that queues behind [[NVDA]] on CoWoS-L a real second source, into CoWoS **+>75% y/y in 2027E**. And **Hugging Face supplies the first third-party, comparative evidence on ROCm adoption: AMD and NVDA are the two biggest open-model publishers on earth this year (>200 repos each, ~2x the third-place player), with AMD's contribution explicitly the CONVERSIONS that let trillion-parameter models run on US hardware** — which is how the 15–20% CUDA→ROCm overhead the 07-30 expert quantified gets amortised across an ecosystem rather than paid per customer. ⚠️ **Held with its counterweight: repository count is a supply metric on a Hub where 1.5% of repos take 99.2% of downloads and only 3% of 2026 volume goes above 70B — ecosystem evidence, not revenue evidence.** **08-20 adds the first Asia SUPPLY-CHAIN read on Helios, and its contribution is a distinction rather than a direction.** The JPM Taiwan analyst separates **module-level volume (possible 2H26)** from **rack-level mass production (~1Q27, "slightly after Nvidia") and rack-level shipment (1H27)** — which is compatible with management's "initial shipments Q3, step-up Q4, further step-up 1Q27" only if "shipments" means modules, and which matches this page's own 2025-12-17 mark of "a few hundred racks in Q4'26, major volume in 2027." He also **names [[ORCL]] and [[META]] as the two major Helios projects**, says **Wistron "needs to step up"** to smooth the production cycle while AMD leverages a new US supplier, and — asked directly — **declines to call a Sanmina/ZT-to-Wistron share shift**, offering instead *"this is not an easy product."* ⚠️ **Weight it accordingly: the analyst opens with "we didn't hear much feedback on this project," and the file itself is a severe ASR defect (13,071 `[silence]` lines around ~20KB of content, heavy homophone garbling), so the DATES and the module-vs-rack distinction are the usable output — not the names, and not a share call. ⚠️ Housekeeping worth recording once: the ingest router sent that ENTIRE file to AMD alone; [[AMZN]], [[MU]], [[DELL]], [[TSM]], [[ORCL]] and [[META]] content in it was invisible to a keyword router and has been patched by hand.**

"Expecting a healthy BEAT & RAISE from AMD with June revenue of $12.2B+ (+20% Q/Q) and September GUIDED TO $13.5B+ (vs. St. $12.5B)."** ⚠️ **Compare with the UBS row directly below: UBS models FQ2 $11.33B and an FQ3 guide of $12.71B. Jefferies is ~$0.9bn above UBS on the quarter and ~$0.8bn above on the guide — a very wide gap between two bulls on a name printing in 48 hours, and it is not a Data-Center-vs-Client mix difference; Jefferies is simply carrying a much larger server-CPU number.** **The server-CPU argument is specific and checkable: "we see potential for SERVER VOLUME GROWTH OF ~20% Q/Q+ after capacity constrained [[INTC]] printed 9% in 2Q26 with PRICING UP 10%+ on mix dynamics" — i.e. Intel's own constrained print is read as the read-across that AMD took the incremental units.** **VENICE is the pricing lever and the number is large: "potential for a MATERIAL STEP-UP IN PRICING with ASPs in the range of $3-5K vs. 1Q26 average CPU price of ~$1.5K" as 2H26 progresses.** ⚠️ **THE HEDGE, and it is a genuine near-term negative that UBS's 9/10-sentiment framing does not carry: "near-term we see LESS BENEFIT FROM THE MI3xx SERIES AS CUSTOMERS PREPARE FOR THE LAUNCH OF HELIOS. Timing on Helios likely varies by customer but we are expecting a MODEST 3Q26 BENEFIT before a more significant ramp in 4Q26 and 1Q27." So Jefferies is underwriting a big CPU quarter with an accelerator air-pocket inside it — a product-transition pause, not a demand problem, but it is the most likely source of a soft data-point on the call.** **CUSTOMER LIST EXPANDED, and this is net-new to the page: "the customer base continues to broaden out with AMD NOW LISTING BOTH [[MSFT]] AND [[ANTHROPIC]] AS HELIOS CUSTOMERS in addition [[META]] AND [[OPENAI]]."** Independent supply-chain corroboration the same day: **Morgan Stanley's Greater China hardware team names Wiwynn as "a key supplier of Trainium server racks AND AMD HELIOS RACKS FOR META," and Wistron as "a key L6 partner to [[NVDA]] AND AMD"** (MS · Howard Kao, 2026-08-02) — two independent routes to the same conclusion that Helios has real rack-level programmes behind it. Desk colour: **"Still feels more longed than not to us, and the debate is more around UPSIDE TO THE GUIDE and IF THEY NEED TO UPTICK ON GROSS MARGINS"** (Jefferies · Favuzza, Tech desk, 2026-08-02). |

| 07-28 | **Fubon Research · Sherman Shang / Daniel Yang — "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates"** (dist. Jefferies; sector Overweight) | supply | **bull** | **The primary PDF behind the Jefferies relay above. Every figure in that row checks out, and the note adds the WHY plus the split between TSMC and ASE — which is the part that changes how the 300k should be read.** **Fubon verbatim: "Thanks to agentic AI and strong server CPU demand, we see AMD is able to get more capacity from TSMC plus ASE. We estimate AMD's total allocation to significantly increase to 300k in 2027, with the majority of the increase coming from ASE."** So the driver is named — **agentic AI + server CPU**, not the MI-series alone — which lines up with the >$120B/2030 server-CPU TAM raise already on this page and with Venice rather than only MI455X. **The split matters: Fubon's Figure 5 has AMD at just 7.2% (2026) → 7.0% (2027) of TSMC CoWoS = 84,300 → 132,300 wafers.** Against a **300k total allocation**, that means **roughly 170k wafers — well over half of AMD's 2027 packaging — sits OUTSIDE TSMC CoWoS, at ASE.** ⚠️ **Read the two bases carefully before comparing to anything else on this page: 132,300 is TSMC-only CoWoS; 300k is TSMC + ASE. They are not the same number and should not be netted against NVDA's 945,000 TSMC-CoWoS wafers (whose own total allocation is 1.03mn once SPIL is counted).** **Corroborating the ASE leg: Fubon raises ASE's FoCoS capacity forecast to 50kwpm for 2027 "to support CPU and networking customers' demand"** — up from its own 45k (06-05) and 30k before that. AMD's Venice CPU packaging has been at ASE FoCoS on this page since the 06-05 Fubon outlook, so the FoCoS raise and the AMD allocation raise are the same event seen from two sides. **Context for how favourable this is relative to the field: in the same revision [[NVDA]] holds flat at 50.0%, [[AVGO]] is trimmed to 22.3% (from Fubon's own 24–25%), and only AMD and [[MEDIATEK]] (to 7.3% / 137,700) take incremental 2027 allocation.** One cost item AMD carries from the same note: **HBM4 runs US$31-32/GB for NVDA but US$35-36/GB for "other GPU and custom-ASIC projects," i.e. HBM cost roughly doubling in 2027** — AMD sits on the expensive side of that spread, which compounds the ASE-quote and BT-substrate inflation already logged on 07-27. Fubon's overall stance is **more confident in the CPU supply chain, the SPE supply chain and TPU momentum than in the [[NVDA]] GPU chain** (Rubin 2026 cut to ~1mn), with **ASE its top pick on Vera + Venice exposure** — i.e. the house preference is explicitly for the CPU leg AMD sits in. (Fubon "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates", 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Our_advanced_packaging_CoWoS_forecast_adjustments_other_up.html) |

| 07-29 | Aurelion Research/PhotonCap · Lumentum mgmt meeting (read-through) | produto | mixed | **Read-through only — subject company is Lumentum, and this is PhotonCap's independent technical commentary, not AMD or Lumentum management. Source is a joint independent publication (management meeting + investment analysis by Aurelion Research, technical comments by PhotonCap).** **Two marks, both about network intensity rather than accelerator share. (1) The AMD–Cerebras split-inference architecture is used as the live proof that inference is becoming network-heavy: "The joint inference solution AMD and Cerebras announced in July is the live example. Helios handles the front half, reading the prompt, and Cerebras handles the back half, generating the answer, so work in progress crosses a network between two machines."** ⚠️ **PhotonCap immediately bounds the claim, and the bound is the point: "What that link is made of has not been disclosed. So this is evidence that inference moves a lot of data, not evidence of any vendor's revenue."** It is offered against the standing view that a training→inference mix shift is bearish for optics ("inference is less network intensive, or at least has been historically, so a mix shift toward inference does not translate to a 1:1 demand for optics" — Aurelion), with the gap closing via longer context windows, heavier routing, **disaggregated prefill/decode** and agentic usage. **(2) On the customer side: "Every camp pursuing CPO needs high power light. AMD's rack scale expansion creates EML, CW, and pluggable demand. Broadcom is a potential customer and at the same time a platform competitor with its own laser sources… Absent a disclosed design win, though, these are demand paths, not confirmed customers."** So AMD's rack-scale roadmap is framed as an *incremental* optical-demand source for the LITE/COHR light-source complex — ⚠️ **no design win, no supplier named, no dated program.** Consistent with the MI500 NPO + ELSFP architecture already on this page from SemiAnalysis (07-24) and on [themes/optical-cpo.md](themes/optical-cpo.md). See [LITE.md](LITE.md), [COHR.md](COHR.md). |

| 08-18 | 🔴 **Meritz (via @jukan05) + DIGITIMES — AMD is the SMALLEST of the four named 2028 HBM buyers, and it is a distant fourth** | supply / competicao | **bear** | ★ **The most uncomfortable third-party datapoint of the day for the MI-series volume case.** Meritz's planned **2028 HBM demand by company: [[AVGO]] 35–40bn Gb · [[NVDA]] 30bn Gb · [[GOOG]] 20bn Gb · **AMD 10bn Gb****. ⚠️ **Broker estimate relayed via the FinTwit corpus; no methodology, no bridge from Gb to accelerator units, and "planned" demand is not shipped demand.** 🔴 **If directionally right, AMD's 2028 HBM pull is ~1/3 of NVDA's and ~1/4 of Broadcom's — i.e. the memory supply chain is provisioning AMD as a fourth-tier buyer behind two merchant vendors and one internal ASIC program, which is a harder read than the datacenter-GPU share narrative implies.** **Separately, and negative for the strategic-partner optionality: DIGITIMES reports "Elon Musk has made [[SPCX]]'s AI hardware strategy unusually clear: future infrastructure will rely EXCLUSIVELY on Nvidia chips and technology. Yet AMD, Nvidia's largest rival in AI accelerators, still [owns] US$565M of SpaceX shares"** ⚠️ **(headline + lede only, body not read)** — **a $565m equity stake in a customer that has publicly gone single-sourced to the competitor.** **Tape: AMD RSI and pair action both soft — MRVL/AMD was a +19% August MTD pair spread** (Jefferies desk, 08-18).

**Quarter synthesis:** the CPU is no longer a debate (broad consensus that AMD has a superior roadmap to Intel) and the focus migrated entirely to GPU execution + valuation (~45x NTM) — bulls (Citi $575, Bernstein $600) raise PTs on the view of $30-50bn of GPU revenue in '27, while the supply-chain flow (Redburn) repositions the risk from the chip (on track) to the Helios rack assembly and substrate bottlenecks, suggesting a tactical trim into the print. Newest datapoints (06-29/06-30) cut both ways: **Citi** ranks AMD #1 large-cap on **memory supply security** (dual Samsung/Micron) as DRAM becomes the binding constraint on AI compute — a supply-side positive; **Epoch/SouthernValue** shows NVDA stabilizing/gaining compute share vs AMD & Trainium in 1Q26 — a mild demand-share negative. Two further threads added: **Barron's** (06-01/06-22) frames a financial-inflection narrative — DC sales overtaking Intel's in 2026 and becoming a majority of revenue, EPS $4.17→$13.10 (2025→27E) — squarely consistent with the >$120B/2030 CPU TAM management raised on the Q1 call. **SemiAnalysis's "CPUs are Back" (02-09)** independently corroborates a widening AMD-vs-Intel performance-per-core gap through the Venice (2026) → Verano (2027) → Florence (2028) roadmap, with the caveat that Intel still leads on the APX ISA extension; its vendor-marketing-labeled slides (Turin TCO claim, Venice-vs-Turin claim) are kept distinct from SemiAnalysis's own independent view. The memory-history pieces ("Scaling the Memory Wall," "Memory Mania") add the MI300X single-source-HBM cautionary precedent (Samsung sole-source → failure rates → Microsoft relationship damage, later diversified to Micron) and flag — via an explicitly-labeled reader comment, not SemiAnalysis's own analysis — a speculative memory-supply lock-up risk if AMD/Meta over-hedge against shortage. **Newest (07-01):** **UBS/Sunny Lin** sharply raises the Venice unit ramp (1.3mn units 2026 → 4mn 2027) and models total AMD CoWoS demand +232% in 2027E on Venice + MI450/455 — the most explicit bullish unit/CoWoS quantification yet of the CPU-led capacity story, layering on top of (not superseding) the existing UBS "CPU TAM" bottom-up share framing. Positioning context from **Jefferies/Favuzza**: AMD is now the #2 NDX and #2 SPX YTD contributor for 1H26 (16%/11% of the respective index gains) and a 12-month relative-high name — the stock's index-level importance has grown alongside the fundamental re-rating, even as it traded -1.4% pre-market on July 1. On competition, an **AlphaSense expert call** (ex-AWS/Cerebras) nuances the GPU debate: NVDA's moat holds in training, but inference competition is rising from AMD, Trainium, and Cerebras alike — consistent with the "show-me" framing already on the page but locating the near-term threat specifically in inference. Separately, AMD is one of the names in **BofA's new 2027-2030 buyside EPS bogey poll** (Fenske/Liu) ahead of Q2 earnings season, alongside SanDisk, Micron, Lumentum, Nvidia, Broadcom — a read on buyside out-year expectations to watch as results come in. **07-11/12/13 (the CPU-preview cluster):** three notes reinforce the CPU-led bull into the print. **JPM's proprietary server-CPU model (Hung/Hariharan, 07-12)** sizes the tailwind — total server CPU **26mn (2025) → 68mn units (2028, 38% CAGR)** with **x86 on an accelerated ~25% CAGR** (vs flattish prior cycle), a direct EPYC/Venice volume driver even as ARM rises to ~43% of the blended mix; the Hon Precision handler read-through (Jimmy Huang, 07-12) confirms Venice test-demand "strengthened materially" into 2H26. **Mizuho (Rakesh, 07-11) initiates the page at Outperform $615** (~42x F27E EPS), a technology-enabler of the >$1.4T-2028E capex ramp. And **BofA (Arya, 07-13) raises its PO to $620 (from $550, 47x CY27E PE)** on an expected beat/raise, the first MI455X 'Helios' shipment (~$6-7bn+/qtr exiting Q4) into the Jul-23 'Advancing AI' event, and AMD's high-core-count lead (Venice 256c = 3.30x rack-geomean vs NVDA Vera). Net: the CPU story stays a near-unanimous bull (BofA $620 / GS $640 / Bernstein $600 / Citi $575 / Mizuho $615) with GPU execution + valuation the residual debate; the AMD PT ladder is now clustered $575-640 across the top houses. **07-13/14 (Twitter/X + subject-flow additions):** the CPU-supply-driven demand read strengthens further — **KeyBanc (via @firstadopter, 07-14) lifts general-purpose server-CPU unit growth to +30% (from +18.5%)**, with AMD securing incremental TSMC N4 wafers (freed by MediaTek smartphone cuts) to support +15-20% AMD server-CPU unit growth this year and both AMD/INTC targeting >50% unit growth in 2027 — a wafer-supply confirmation of the EPYC/Venice ramp, and a supportive read alongside the same-day UBS "SemiBytes" (Arcuri) and BofA "Core Wars" server-strength notes. On the bear side, **SemiAnalysis (@SemiAnalysis_, 07-13)** flags that AMD "still has a lot of catching up to do" on vLLM after fresh NVDA gains — the ROCm/vLLM inference-software gap persists, consistent with the existing "DeepSeek V4" critique. The SemiAnalysis×Supermicro (Malyala) interview (07-14) adds qualitative Helios rack-scale color (double-wide, liquid-cooled) vs NVDA Vera Rubin, and @QQ_Timmy's scale-up networking note (>$70bn market by 2030, 4x prior) frames the growing interconnect TAM behind Helios/UALink. Net: no thesis change — the CPU-led bull is reinforced on supply/units, while the software/inference gap remains the standing GPU-execution caveat. **07-16/17 (into the Jul-23 'Advancing AI' event):** the previews turn to TAM-expansion + the MI500 roadmap. **BofA (Arya, 07-17)** frames AMD broadening the narrative to a **$1.5T+ CY30 infrastructure TAM (from >$1T at the Dec-25 Analyst Day)** and a path to **~100%+ CY27 DC growth (vs cons +82%)**, keeping BUY $620. **Jefferies (Blayne Curtis, 07-16) raises its PT to $515 (from a stale $300)** and adds the sharpest MI500 detail on the page — **CDNA6/2nm/HBM4E in 2027, native UAL 256-GPU/rack, likely requiring optical CPO** (AMD working with [[ALAB]] on UAL + [[AVGO]] on ESUN, Ayar Labs for the optical engine) — a direct [[optical-cpo]] read-through if confirmed. On customers, both temper expectations (no major new logo beyond OpenAI+Meta likely) but flag **Anthropic as the swing factor** (hiring ROCm engineers) and **MSFT a likely MI400 customer**; BofA's open questions (HBM cost/allocation, hyperscale warrants, TSMC allocation) frame the event's debates. The PT ladder now spans $410 (MS) → $700 (UBS), with the top cluster $600-700 and only JPM ($385) implying downside. **07-20 (into 'Advancing AI'):** BofA's "Big Tech setups lightning round" (Arya) keeps the event framing bullish — MI450 on track, Venice "best out there," an expected **TAM raise ~$1tn→$1.5tn** and **server-CPU TAM $120bn→$200bn**, with data-center "could double" — while flagging the setup risk (**no new customer logos**; the Microsoft PR possibly non-incremental). Backfilled: the actual **Bernstein "CPU Renaissance? $223bn TAM"** report (06-17, OP $600 already logged) adds EPS 2026E/27E/28E $6.98/$14.61/$19.53 and AMD's CPU-TAM doubling ($60bn→$120bn). **07-23/24 — the event delivered, and the debate moved to price:** the recap notes land three things the previews had asked for and one they hadn't. (1) **All three CY30 TAMs raised at once** — total compute $1Tn→**$2Tn**, AI accelerators to **$1.4Tn**, server CPU $120bn→**$220bn** — overshooting BofA's pre-called $1.5tn/$200bn. (2) **The fast-decode gap is closed by partnership, not silicon**: the **Cerebras disaggregated-inference tie-up** (Helios prompt-processing + wafer-scale token generation, 5x throughput, Cerebras Cloud later this year) answers BofA's pre-event debate #2 and, per MS, is the counter to **NVDA's Groq acquisition** — see [[CEREBRAS]]. (3) **Multi-generation commitment evidence**, which is what the bear case actually needed: **Meta on stage on a fourth consecutive EPYC generation** and MI300/350/450 as progressively deeper co-design; BofA's structural argument that "no customer undertakes the design-in effort for just a single generation" makes Anthropic likely multi-gen through MI500/600. And the thing nobody asked for: **rocm.ai**, which reframes the software moat question from "is ROCm as good as CUDA" to "how cheaply can a coding agent port to it" — with Anthropic's Tom Brown on stage saying Claude largely bootstrapped the MI355 bring-up itself ("we are past the CUDA moat days," @austinsemis). **But the bull case did not get cheaper.** Two honest debits from the recaps: BofA's own spec table shows Helios **~20% BEHIND Vera Rubin on FP4 *inference*** (the ahead-on-training/behind-on-inference split is easy to lose in the headline "30% more tokens/$", part of which is a **~$5mn vs ~$6-7mn rack price** gap rather than performance); and MS's **warrant-as-marketing-expense reframing** — "wipes out most of the profits from GPU for the next few years" — is the hardest version of the dilution argument yet put on this page, and is why MS stays EW $410 while conceding the event was "very positive." The clean forward test on that last point is **the structure of the next GW-scale deal**: Anthropic came as **$5B of AMD equity into Anthropic, no warrants** (Barclays: a power-shift to chipmakers), whereas OpenAI/Meta came as warrants. Which template recurs decides whether MS's objection decays or compounds. Immediate next checkpoint: **the Q2 FY26 print in ~10 days** against the $11.2B guide — MS explicitly reads the TAM raise as "very likely foreshadow[ing] very good data center numbers." **07-24, the independent layer — and the most important single line to add to this quarter: the harshest independent critic of AMD's software has upgraded the name, while naming two concrete execution gates.** SemiAnalysis — the source of every hard ROCm/vLLM criticism on this page — moved its standing call from **"0% chance of closing the gap with Nvidia" → (AMD 2.0) "a meaningful chance" → "a great chance of success as long as AMD solves the 2 major risks."** That is a genuine change of view from the one house with no incentive to flatter AMD, and it is not what the sell-side notes captured. The two gates are specific, dateable and both fixable with capital rather than with invention: **(1) the Helios rack** — no cableless design (Nvidia already pivoted to one for Vera Rubin, too late for AMD to copy), weak 200G SerDes forcing **up to ~85% of the backplane to be retimed with >550 Broadcom retimers per rack**, backplane reliability problems in ramp, and **10,368 copper differential pairs + 1,728 flyover cables + $68,928 of backplane/cable content per rack**; and **(2) internal cluster/CI capacity** — **Pollara/Kubernetes CI still at 0% parity with Nvidia's ConnectX nightly**, the **90%-CUDA vLLM-gating target for the event MISSED** after leadership pulled clusters from its own vLLM team, **MI455X (gfx1250) nightly CI slipped to October 2026**, and an internal dev fleet still **>1 order of magnitude below Nvidia's** even after +2,000 MI355X this month and +6,000 later this year. Read together with Redburn's ODM checks, gate (1) is now corroborated from two independent directions, which raises confidence in a slower rack ramp than the guide implies; gate (2) reframes the software debate from "can AMD's engineers do it" to **"will AMD's leadership give them clusters"** — a management-allocation question, and the reason the ROCm bear bullet on this page is dated rather than withdrawn. **Three things the independent read genuinely changes.** First, **the warrant argument now has a number**: OpenAI and Meta each hold up to **160M shares (~10% of s/o) at $0.01** vesting against 6GW to a **$600** hurdle, which at $600 is an effective **~$16.0B/GW rebate** against **~$15.2B/GW paid by OpenAI and ~$18.7B/GW by Meta** — **~105% and ~85% discounts**, i.e. *"OpenAI could earn money simply by buying MI455X servers and exercising the accompanying warrants."* That is MS's "wipes out most of the profits from GPU" derived independently from the customer's side, and it makes the "what structure does the *next* GW deal take" test the sharpest open question on the page. Second, **the memory story inverts on inspection**: AMD does win capacity (**432GB vs 288GB**) and nominal bandwidth (**23.3 vs 22 TB/s**), but **Nvidia lifted HBM4 pin speed 40% above AMD's (10.7 vs 7.6 Gbps) and above JEDEC spec specifically to erase that gap**, and on AMD's own BOM the **LPDDR5X second memory tier has been deleted entirely** (from an up-to-1TB-per-accelerator plan) alongside a significant DDR5 de-spec — with a **2027 HBM price step that AMD can only partly pass through**, a gross-margin item and not just a supply item. The Citi "AMD #1 on memory supply" read moves from ✓ to ⚠. Third, **power is now an open contradiction in the file and should stay open**: SemiAnalysis builds MI455X Helios at **~240kW server / ~257kW all-in per rack vs GB300 NVL72's ~142kW / ~159kW**, the opposite sign to BofA's "AMD's platforms are less power-hungry than Nvidia's" (Watts to Tokens, 05-25). Both are retained deliberately — the resolution decides whether the BG2 "watt-constrained world deprioritizes the watt-loser" objection strengthens or dissolves, and it is testable against real 2H26 Helios deployments. Net for the quarter: **the technology and the customer roster are no longer the debate; the debate is (a) price, (b) the warrant economics, and now (c) two named, dated execution gates on rack manufacturing and internal CI** — with the notable and bullish asymmetry that the most credible bear on AMD software has just moved to the other side of the trade. **08-03 (run-inbox) adds one datapoint to each of those three legs and changes none of them. On capacity: the Fubon PRIMARY transcript reconfirms the 300k 2027 allocation with no revision and names AMD's Venice as one of exactly three programs funding ASE's expansion (with NVDA's Vera and AVGO's Tomahawk 6) — the strongest version yet of AMD's claim on that capacity — while warning that TSMC N2/N3 stays "very, very tight" in 2027, so the front end, not the back end, is the remaining gate on Venice. On software: SemiAnalysis's Kimi K3 note records AMD with a Day-0 vLLM recipe alongside NVDA (DRAM offload + DSpark spec-decoding) — the first time the publisher that wrote the harshest ROCm criticism has AMD arriving same-day on a frontier open model, though with no published K3 performance or cost result on Instinct to go with it. Net: the execution gates are unchanged, the evidence on the software gate has begun to run in AMD's favour, and the binding capacity question has migrated from packaging to N2 wafers.

_Source: AMD earnings calls (dates above); management commentary, paraphrased. Q2'26 column reconstructed from post-call desk notes (Jefferies · Rankie Wong 2026-08-04; GS · Schneider 2026-08-04) — **the transcript is not yet on file**, so treat quoted phrasing as desk paraphrase rather than verbatim._

**Inter-quarter management update — "Advancing AI 2026" (2026-07-23), not an earnings call.** The Q1'26 column's **"TAM >$120B/2030"** was superseded ~11 weeks later at the event: **server-CPU TAM raised to >$220bn by 2030 (+50% CAGR)**, with **total compute to $2Tn (from $1Tn)** and **AI accelerators to $1.4Tn (+48% CAGR, vs $500bn for CY28 prior)**. The full cadence of the server-CPU number — **~$60bn (18% CAGR) at the Nov-2025 FAD → $120bn (35%) on the Q1'26 call → $220bn (50%) on 2026-07-23** — is the single most important management-guidance drift on this page, and MS explicitly flags the revision *velocity* as a reason to treat it as a strong 12-month signal rather than a reliable 2030 estimate. Also new from management at the event: **agentic AI framed as ~50% of the TAM**, **CPU:GPU ratio tightening toward 1:1** (from 4:1 today per the 07-23 roundtable), **Helios in full production with first shipment in September**, and a roadmap extended to **MI600 / Zen 7 "Ferrara" in 2028** (previewed to Zen 8 "Ravenna" 2030). The prior $120bn/$1Tn figures are retained above and in the Changelog. ✅ **Re-anchored on the Q2 FY26 call (2026-08-04) and they held: server-CPU TAM $220bn by 2030 (+50%/yr) and DCAI accelerator TAM $1.4trn by 2030 (+45%/yr) were both repeated, with management adding that revenue should grow "substantially above" the prior >35% long-term target and "significantly exceed" the $20 long-term EPS target.** ⚠️ **The EPS target is the one number the buy-side has already moved past — Jefferies reports "expects are likely still for $25+ EPS in 2028E" (2026-08-04), so ">$20" now functions as a floor, not a target.**

AMD Advancing AI 2026" (Patel et al., 2026-07-24)](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html) — **the primary independent engineering read of Advancing AI 2026 and the source of the ratings upgrade (0% → "a great chance of success")**, conditional on two named gates: the Helios rack ramp (no cableless design, ~85% of backplane retimed, >550 Broadcom retimers/rack, 10,368 copper differential pairs + 1,728 flyover cables, $68,928/rack of backplane+cable content, backplane reliability in ramp) and internal cluster/CI famine (Pollara/Kubernetes CI at 0% parity, 90%-CUDA vLLM-gating target missed, gfx1250 nightly CI slipped to Oct-2026, dev fleet >1 order of magnitude below NVDA, best engineers in Shanghai). Also: first 2nm datacenter silicon / 5.5x-reticle CoWoS-L / SoIC-X / 3,470mm² logic / first active LSI, 20 PF FP8 vs Rubin 17.5 PF but no 3-bit LUT tensor cores; HBM4 432GB & 23.3 TB/s vs Rubin 288GB & 22 TB/s at 7.6 vs 10.7 Gbps pin speed (Nvidia raised HBM4 above JEDEC spec to close the gap); **LPDDR5X removed from the BOM + significant DDR5 de-spec**; 2027 HBM price rise → MI455X GM drag; 12 Broadcom TH6 102.4T (432/512 lanes used; Broadcom the only option → "cannot reach extreme codesign"); **~240kW/~257kW per rack vs GB300 NVL72 ~142kW/~159kW**; TCO between GB300 and VR NVL72, competitive on marketed dense FP8 at a ~15.6% IRR hurdle with an explicit overstated-TFLOPs/low-MFU caveat; **warrants — 160M shares each at $0.01 to $600, ~$16.0B/GW rebate vs ~$15.2B (OpenAI) / ~$18.7B (Meta) paid = ~105%/~85% discounts**; MSFT history (dropped after MI300X on Samsung 2023 HBM + software, skipped MI325X/MI355X; Helios believed mainly for OpenAI on Azure); Anthropic 2GW / 1H27 / up to $5B AMD cash / all four accelerator platforms / Tom Brown's weekend "/goal" bring-up; Meta custom MI455 (4 dies, 6 stacks, 8-Hi) "going to nuke AMD's volume at Meta"; $300M Crusoe loan guarantee → 6%; MoRI "a real win, built by one tiger team," RIXL fully ported into NVDA's NIXL; **MI500 CPC+NPO — 9 optical engines at 6.4 Tbit/s = 57.6T, 8 lambdas/lane at 50G NRZ, ELSFP light sources, flat one-tier across 576 GPUs / 8 racks, 288 switches on a 115.2T TH6 SKU**.
  - [SemiAnalysis — "Nvidia GPU Debt Backstop Unleashes the AI Project Trinity" (2026-07-06)](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_06_ENC_Nvidia_GPU_Debt_Backstop_Unleashes_the_AI_Project_Trinity_Capital_Offtake_and_Datacenters_email.html) — light AMD read-through: **AMD has been running its own vendor backstops since 2025**, offering AWS, OCI, DigitalOcean, Vultr, Tensorwave, Crusoe and other neoclouds a deal to rent back a significant chunk of capacity on long-term contracts for internal AMD software development if the neocloud cannot sell it — the same borrowed/temporary capacity whose reallocation broke AMD's internal vLLM gating CI (see Bear, Risk 2). Nvidia's comparable program: $860M lease guarantee (Oct-2025) → $3.5B (Jan-2026).
  - [SemiAnalysis — "EDA Market Primer" (2026-05-21)](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_21_ENC_EDA_Market_Primer_email.html) — light cost-structure/lock-in read-through: **AMD's MI300A carries 13 chiplets** (3 CPU compute dies, 6 GPU compute dies, 4 I/O dies + 8 HBM3 stacks) implying **$75-105M of EDA spend** for that program, since each die, then each die-to-die interface, then the system, then the package must be verified separately — the design-cost tax on the chiplet strategy. AMD's fabless-R&D-to-EDA-spend **R² 0.91 (2019) → 0.93 (2024)** (NVDA 0.91 → 0.94), i.e. deepening EDA lock-in. See [[CDNS]] / [[SNPS]].
  - [SemiAnalysis — "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset" (2026-07-21) · canonical SemiAnalysis email copy](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_21_ENC_Metas_Infrastructure_Team_Needs_A_Culture_Reset_email.html) — same piece already linked on this page under the `Meta_-_semianalysis.html` slug (07-21 full-log row); added here as the canonical newsletter-email HTML. Adds the detail not previously on the page: Meta's custom MI455 steps the HBM4 down to **8-Hi stacks vs the standard SKU's 12-Hi**, the configuration was **decided by Meta's RecSys infra team before TBD Lab was formed**, TBD "has no interest in this system, nor is it attractive for external customers," and the conclusion — this **"is going to nuke AMD's volume at Meta because TBD will vastly prefer Rubin."**

- **2026-08-11 (/run-inbox, scheduled night run) — READ-THROUGH ONLY from a [[TSM]] note. 1 intra-quarter row, this record. NOTHING SUPERSEDED — no AMD rating, PT or estimate in the source.** **Source: Bernstein · Mark Li / Edward Hou / Yipin Cai, "TSMC: Higher revenue & capex on CPU; Outperform with 40% upside" (2026-08-10, first published 10 Aug 12:33 UTC), 19pp primary PDF — a TSMC note whose thesis is that agentic-AI workloads have made the CPU TSMC's third growth wave (CPU revenue US$6-7bn 2023 → US$15-16bn 2025 → US$high-30s bn 2027E, 50-60% CAGR, "about as big as XPU in terms of WAFER revenue contribution in 2027"). The full note is folded into [[TSM]]; only the AMD-specific content is here.** 🔴 **THE HEADLINE READ-THROUGH: AMD PULLED TSMC's N2 FORWARD. "It's AMD Venice CPU, followed by its GPU MI450-series, made N2 earlier than recent prior nodes" — N2 posted first revenue contribution in 2Q26 (3% of TSMC total wafer revenue) versus 3Q23 for N3, 3Q20 for N5 and 3Q18 for N7, a full quarter earlier than the last three generations. Two independent sources now date AMD's N2 lead (SemiAnalysis 07-24 on [[TSM]] has MI455X as "the first 2nm datacenter silicon to ship" while "all of the competing accelerator platforms are on N3"), and Bernstein adds that it moved the foundry's node economics, not just AMD's product calendar.** **ALSO NET-NEW: AMD sized as TSMC's largest CPU customer in both years — ~50% of a US$6-7bn CPU book in 2023, 35-40% of a US$15-16bn book in 2025, so spend roughly doubled to ~US$5.5-6.5bn while share fell as [[INTC]] came in at mid-30s%.** ⚠️ **AND ONE THAT CORRECTS A COMMON MODELLING SHORTCUT: "most of CPUs are not packaged with advanced packaging technology. Some CPUs are packaged with CoWoS-equivalent technology, BUT NOT BY TSMC, and AMD's Venice packaged by ASE… [is an] example" — Venice volume does not consume TSMC CoWoS at all, which is consistent with the Fubon mark on [[TSM]] that the majority of AMD's 2027 CoWoS-equivalent increase comes from ASE. Any model scaling AMD's TSMC packaging allocation off total AMD unit growth is wrong by construction.** **Chiplet composition from Bernstein's teardown table: Venice = N2 compute + N6 IO; Ryzen 7 9700X = N4 compute + N6 IO — the mechanism behind the note's point that CPU demand lifts N3/N5/N7 too.** Cross-filed [[TSM]], [[INTC]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md).

  ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT — one PT raised: Bernstein · Stacy Rasgon, Outperform, PT $600 (06-17) → $650.** **BofA · Vivek Arya reiterated Buy PO $620 but CHANGED THE VALUATION BASIS, which must not be read as "unchanged": from 47x CY27E EXCLUDING SBC to 27x CY28E INCLUDING SBC — the same $620 now rests on a year-later, SBC-burdened earnings base at roughly half the headline multiple. Estimates raised sharply: CY26/27/28E EPS +2%/+20%/+34% to $7.62/$15.88/$23.88, with CY30E "in excess of $30/sh (~4x current)" and DC sales ~$32bn CY26E → ~$170bn CY30E at just 7–8% AI-GPU and 25–30% CPU share.** Also captured: Jefferies (Curtis) TP $640 → $650 with 2027E rev/EPS +29%/+42% and 2027E/28E EPS $18.18/$24.63 (already logged 08-05, now with the AI-GPU $40bn+/server $30bn+ decomposition).
  🔴 **THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT NET-NEW ITEM: AMD IR PUT A NUMBER ON LISA SU'S "WELL ABOVE 100% GROWTH." Per Jefferies desk callbacks, "AMD IR ENDORSED A $70BN+ # FOR 2027E DC REVS (WHICH IMPLIES +130% Y-Y), in response to all the consternation about what Lisa meant." Logged as an IR-sourced DESK RELAY, not guidance — but it resolves the largest ambiguity from the print and is the number the buy-side will now hold management to. It sits alongside, and far above, the ~$30bn DCAI floor management conceded to C.J. Muse on the call.**
  🔴 **NEW POSITIONING RISK, in new language for this page: "an INCREASING CHORUS ABOUT AMD NOW POTENTIALLY BEING THE NEXT FUNDING SHORT — and not just paired vs [[INTC]], but also vs [[NVDA]] and all semis" (Jefferies desk, 08-05), after a +132–145% YTD move. This is a mechanical de-rating risk independent of fundamentals and the mirror image of the crowded-short unwind at [[MCHP]] the same week.**
  **NET-NEW QUANTIFICATION from the UBS follow-up call (Arcuri), four items the published notes left vague: (1) DC GPU revenue $2.75bn in the June quarter, up from $2.45bn in March, and the FY26 ">$15bn" target implies a Q4 run-rate of AT LEAST $6.5bn; (2) Q4'26 guidance implies ~$17bn total revenue vs street $15.3–15.7bn, but "the BUY-SIDE LARGELY ALREADY ANTICIPATED THIS UPSIDE, drawing parallels to past Nvidia dynamics where the sell-side lagged" — the cleanest explanation on this page for why a beat-and-raise traded −7/−9%; (3) 🔴 the 70% 2027 server-CPU guide is "OVERLY CONSERVATIVE, with SUPPLY-CHAIN CHECKS INDICATING POTENTIAL FOR 2X+ GROWTH, driven by AN INFLECTION IN [[TSM]] N2 ALLOCATION" — the most actionable upside claim in the window and independently checkable; (4) ⚠️ GROSS MARGIN "expected to remain AROUND 55% THROUGH 2027… due to higher growth dollars coming from LOWER-MARGIN GPU ($30bn incremental) vs HIGHER-MARGIN CPU ($20bn incremental)" — the mix that drives the bull case is the same mix that caps the margin, quantified here for the first time.**
  ✅ **WARRANT-DILUTION TIMING NOW BOUNDED: "any significant warrant dilution is NOT EXPECTED UNTIL THE BACK HALF OF 2027, contingent on shipping full gigawatt chunks and meeting commercial conditions… a NON-EXACT SCIENCE for modeling" (UBS · Arcuri). This bears directly on the sharpest bear line already on this page — MS's "the customer warrants are a marketing expense… which wipes out most of the profits from GPU for the next few years" — by placing the effect OUTSIDE the 2027 numbers the Street is currently debating. Not a refutation; a timing bound.**
  **TWO BEAR VOICES ADDED, both non-bank and both flagged as such: (1) BEP Research's Benito Zaragozi (via The Information) — AMD is "STUCK IN THE MIDDLE… COMPRESSED ON BOTH SIDES from a margin perspective… they're NOT GOING TO WIN ON TRAINING and they're trying to be the best, lowest-cost inference player, but then you have all these other guys trying to do it as well" — the qualitative twin of Arcuri's ~55% GM point, with the added claim that the low-cost-inference position is itself contested. (2) EnerTuition/"Beyond The Hype" — a triple beat whose after-hours decline "seems like EARNINGS POSITIONING," but "CEO Lisa Su's GUIDANCE SKILLS NEED A LARGE IMPROVEMENT… the company's IR seems to be sleeping at the wheel." ⚠️ Note these two converge with the Jefferies item: the "$70bn+ / well above 100%" confusion had to be cleaned up by IR AFTER the call. That is a communication failure, not a fundamental one — but it is the second consecutive event (after "Advancing AI") where the number the market needed was not given clearly on stage.**
  **Bernstein's framing retained in full because it is the sharpest statement of the reaction problem: "investors are increasingly focused on THE WRONG DEBATE… the stock reaction is increasingly LESS ABOUT FUNDAMENTALS and more about whether AI can continue to OUTPACE ALREADY ELEVATED EXPECTATIONS."**
  **Cross-name items carried off the same UBS call: [[AEIS]]/Integrus Colorado a GM headwind THROUGH 2027, breakeven only late-2027/1H28, with A POTENTIAL WRITE-DOWN UNDER CONSIDERATION (bear), against mid-to-high-teens/~20% growth on 75% MSI + 25% WFE-capex exposure (bull); and UCT undergrowing [[LRCX]] systems shipments in Q3 after outgrowing in Q2 — a revenue-recognition lag, not a demand signal.**

- **2026-08-05** — **/wiki-ingest — the DAY-2 published research on the 08-04 print (desk first takes + the primary transcript were ingested 08-04). 5 new Full-log rows; TWO PT MOVES logged per the thesis-drift rule; no house downgraded and no estimate on this page was reduced.** ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT — superseded and moved here: MS (Joseph Moore) EQUAL-WEIGHT PT $410 → $465; Jefferies (Blayne Curtis) BUY PT $640 → $650. Barclays (Tom O'Malley) OVERWEIGHT PT $665 UNCHANGED. All other marks stand: GS Buy $640, UBS Buy $730, Citi Buy $575, Bernstein OP $600, JPM Neutral $385.** **★ THE MOST VALUABLE ADDITION IS A NUMBER THE COMPANY DOES NOT REPORT — the AI GPU run-rate, and the 2027 bar in dollars rather than adjectives. MS: the AI GPU line "was up to about a ~$10.4BN RUN RATE IN Q2" and is modelled "EXITING 2026 AT ABOVE $25BN, SURPASSING CPU as the largest piece of the datacenter business." For 2027, three houses now publish a DCAI/Instinct figure against a Street at $33.2bn: MS $38bn, Barclays $38bn (raised ~$7bn), Jefferies $40bn+ (raised from $30bn). ⚠️ And MS states the buy-side bar explicitly: "we model $38bn but would think EXPECTATIONS ARE AROUND $45BN (~1 GW WITH EACH CUSTOMER AT $15BN PER GW)."** ⚠️ **That $45bn bogey is built on the very ~3GW × $15bn/GW construction Lisa Su declined to endorse on the call — she answered only the $/GW half ("double digit billions… we're still in that range") and never addressed the ~3 GW premise, as the 08-04 transcript ingest recorded. The buy-side bar therefore rests on a sell-side premise management has not confirmed, which is the cleanest framing yet of the positioning problem that defined this print. Set it against the C.J. Muse exchange from the transcript, where management said ~$30bn of 2027 Instinct "is probably too low": THE LIVE 2027 RANGE IS ~$30BN (MANAGEMENT FLOOR) → $38-40BN (PUBLISHED SELL-SIDE) → ~$45BN (BUY-SIDE BOGEY).** ⚠️ **A GENUINELY NEW BEAR ARGUMENT, about durability rather than valuation — MS on the server-CPU leg: "with the sudden spike in demand and SEVERAL PARTICIPANTS ALL LOOKING TO USE THAT AS AN OPPORTUNITY TO GAIN SHARE, we are NOT YET CONVINCED THIS IS DURABLE FOR MULTIPLE YEARS. Server CPUs were historically a slow growth TAM, but as agentic needs have accelerated, EACH OF [[INTC]], AMD, [[ARM]], AWS, Axion and [[NVDA]] are ramping products aggressively with the intent to gain share. UNITS AT INTEL HAVE GONE FROM −5% Y/Y IN Q1 TO +9% Y/Y IN Q2, so the trajectory of supply is improving quickly." ★ The Intel unit datapoint is net-new to this page and lands squarely on the AMD-vs-[[INTC]] relative-trade question Jefferies raised on 08-04 — it is the first quantitative evidence that Intel's supply is recovering.** ⚠️ **AND IT IS DIRECTLY CONTRADICTED BY JEFFERIES ON THE SAME FACT: Jefferies reads supply-constrained server demand as suppressed upside — "this framework appears PARTICULARLY CONSERVATIVE FOR THE SERVER CPU BUSINESS given improving supply availability and ASP tailwinds from the 2H26 ramp of Venice" — while MS reads the same constraint plus six competitors ramping into it as grounds to doubt multi-year durability. Logged as an open disagreement, not adjudicated.** **On the reaction, Barclays is blunt and did not move its PT: "STOCK REACTION AFTERMARKET SEEMS EXTREME after slightly better revenue and EPS with a 20bps margin miss in the guide underwhelming high expectations… commentary suggests ALL MAJOR ITEMS ARE PROGRESSING SMOOTHLY."** **MS's stated reason for staying EW is now the warrants specifically, not just the multiple: "we like the revenue growth BUT THE WARRANT ISSUANCE TEMPERS OUR ENTHUSIASM," and "given the warrant arrangements, it will be CHALLENGING TO ASSESS THE TRUE LEVEL OF CUSTOMER BUY-IN even as revenue ramps more materially next year" — a disclosure-quality objection this page had not carried in that form.** **Also logged: JPM Asia published a PCs-and-Servers read-across from the AMD takeaways into Taiwan ODM estimates (Hung/Hariharan, 08-05), pairing with the [[ANET]]→Accton read-across the same morning; and a UBS-hosted "ALAB_AMD_ENTG Recap Call" ran at 8am ET alongside MS's 2Q group callback — no notes from either captured at time of ingest.** Cross-filed [[INTC]], [[NVDA]], [[ARM]], [[AMZN]], [[ALAB]].

The 07-28 numbers stay as the page's marks; nothing moved to Changelog because nothing was replaced.** **Net-new from the transcript:** ASE's expansion is funded by **exactly three named programs — NVDA's Vera CPU, AMD's Venice, AVGO's Tomahawk 6** (the page previously had Venice as "one of ASE's three flagship projects" without the other two named); **ASE "again raising their capex"** and named by its own domestic tool/materials suppliers as their fastest-growing customer; and the front-end counterweight — **TSMC 2nm/3nm "very, very tight" in 2027 even with capex rising to $80-85bn from $60-64bn**, which matters because Venice is an N2 part, so packaging relief ≠ ramp relief. **Net-new from the K3 note (kept deliberately small):** **both NVDA and AMD had Day-0 vLLM recipes for Kimi K3, with DRAM offload and DSpark speculative decoding** — logged as time-to-enablement evidence against this page's standing ROCm/vLLM bear argument, and explicitly flagged as NOT a performance result: SemiAnalysis publishes no AMD throughput, cost-per-token or interactivity figure for K3 (its K3 InferenceX work runs on B300/B200; MI355X appears only in the earlier DeepSeek-V4 Day-0-to-43 study). **Judged unsupported and therefore NOT written:** any AMD read-through from the K3 TCO section ($0.171/M input tokens on B300, $0.74/M blended on Moonshot's platform, $3/$15 per M OpenRouter floor) — those are NVDA-platform figures and were left to [[NVDA]] / [themes/tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md). **Cross-linked rather than duplicated:** TSMC/ASE capacity → [[TSM]] + outros-asia; MediaTek's competing 2027 allocation → [[MEDIATEK]]. **Unresolvable, left flagged:** "Seasam" (ASR-garbled Taiwanese advanced-packaging supplier) written as-is rather than guessed.
- **2026-07-31 (wiki-ingest) — the bogey structure into the 08-04 print, a washed-out semis positioning read, and a 2-3x test-time datapoint from the supply chain.** No rating or PT superseded. **Bogeys canvassed by JPM (and separately by Barclays the prior day — two desks surveying the same name signals how unsettled the bar is): F2Q26 revenue $11.2b guide / $11.3b Bberg; DATA CENTER revenue vs $6.55b Bberg; GM 56% guide / 56.05% Bberg; EPS vs $1.63 Bberg; F3Q26 revenue guide vs $12.48b Bberg; F3Q26 GM 56.2% Bberg; F27/F28 EPS vs $13.66 / $18.96 Bberg** (Meyers, 2026-07-31; Rand, 2026-07-30). ⚠️ **The positioning read is the more actionable half and it has now triggered all three of JPM's markers: after three consecutive 2-3z de-gross days, "the 3d amount of gross reduced [is] one of the biggest since 2020, only behind late Jan '21 and mid-Nov '22"; 6m z-score of HF positioning + factor performance at −2z; Momentum selling −2.2z on 20d; HF crowding performance in tech down hard (longs −30%, shorts −11%). US SEMIS: −2.6z net selling over 5d, −1.1z over 20d, mostly longs — taking semis positioning to the 60TH %-ILE ON 12M but STILL THE 95TH SINCE '18, against Software at +1.6z/+3.3z net buying and the 27th/3rd %-ile.** The rotation out of semis into software is the setup risk: washed out on a 12-month view, not on a multi-year one. **Supply is the differentiator and BofA presents AMD as having already solved it: "it has ALREADY SECURED SUPPLIES to ship its next-gen HELIOS full-stack rack, UP TO 3GW+ THROUGH 2027 AT $15-20BN/GW CONTENT EACH" — a very large 2027 revenue envelope, and a contrast with INTC (server ASP +48% y/y but units −6% q/q) and ARM (upper bound on AGI CPU through CY26/27), both "bounded by wafers, substrates"** (Arya, 2026-07-30). [[MSFT]] confirmed it is among the first clouds deploying **Helios**. ⚠️ **Net-new and genuinely two-sided, from the test supply chain rather than from AMD: "the TESTING TIME of AMD's GPU could be 2-3X THAT OF NVIDIA'S GPU, with TDP largely on par or slightly higher" (JPM · Tsai, Chroma ATE, 2026-07-30). Unambiguously good for test vendors — but 2-3x test time is a real cost, cycle-time and constrained-tester-capacity burden on AMD's own back end. Worth watching whether it surfaces as a gross-margin or lead-time issue.** Also: **Hon Precision says SLC handler competition for AMD's next-gen server CPU "could be a swing factor for sentiment in the next 6 months,"** and **ASE's Full-Process CoWoS ramp starts with AMD VENICE this year, scaling in 2027, yield "progressing well"** (see [[TSM]]). **AMD reports 08-04 alongside ALAB and LSCC; MS AMD 2Q callback with Joe Moore 08-05, 8am ET.** Cross-filed [[NVDA]], [[INTC]], [[TSM]], [[TER]], [[MSFT]], [[ALAB]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md).
- **2026-07-30 — Aurelion Research / PhotonCap "A Conversation with Lumentum" (2026-07-29). 1 intra-quarter row (07-29, produto/mixed) + 1 Sources entry. NOTHING SUPERSEDED — no rating, PT, estimate or existing AMD number changed.** ⚠️ **Logged as a READ-THROUGH, and specifically as PhotonCap's independent technical commentary — not AMD management, not Lumentum management. The subject company is Lumentum; the source is a joint independent publication (management meeting + investment analysis by Aurelion Research, technical comments by PhotonCap), not a bank.** **Two marks, both about network intensity rather than accelerator share. (1) The AMD–Cerebras July split-inference architecture is cited as the live proof that inference is becoming network-heavy: "Helios handles the front half, reading the prompt, and Cerebras handles the back half, generating the answer, so work in progress crosses a network between two machines." ⚠️ PhotonCap bounds it immediately and the bound is retained verbatim because it is the honest part: "What that link is made of has not been disclosed. So this is evidence that inference moves a lot of data, not evidence of any vendor's revenue." It is offered against Aurelion's own caution that "inference is less network intensive… so a mix shift toward inference does not translate to a 1:1 demand for optics," with the gap closing via longer context windows, heavier routing, disaggregated prefill/decode and agentic usage. (2) "Every camp pursuing CPO needs high power light. AMD's rack scale expansion creates EML, CW, and pluggable demand… Absent a disclosed design win, though, these are demand paths, not confirmed customers" — i.e. AMD's rack-scale roadmap as an incremental optical-demand source, with no supplier, design win or dated program attached.** Consistent with the MI500 NPO + ELSFP architecture already on this page from SemiAnalysis (07-24). **Deliberately kept to one row rather than expanded into a thesis.** Cross-filed [LITE.md](LITE.md), [COHR.md](COHR.md), [themes/optical-cpo.md](themes/optical-cpo.md).
- **2026-07-28 (/run-inbox — the Fubon PRIMARY PDF)** — **1 intra-quarter row added, the 07-28 Jefferies relay row annotated as confirmed, 1 Sources entry. NO rating, PT or estimate superseded** (the Fubon note carries no AMD rating), **and nothing double-logged: the `/wiki-ingest` entry below already captured this note via the Jefferies sales relay, so only net-new content was entered and the relay row was marked confirmed rather than rewritten.** **Every figure in the relay checks out against the primary — the 300k total allocation, "majority of the increase coming from ASE," and ASE FoCoS to 50k. What the primary adds is the driver and the split, and the split is the part that changes the read.** **Driver, verbatim: "Thanks to agentic AI and strong server CPU demand, we see AMD is able to get more capacity from TSMC plus ASE." So this is not primarily an MI-series allocation win — it is the server-CPU/Venice leg, which lines up with the >$120B-by-2030 server-CPU TAM raise already on this page rather than with the accelerator story.** **Split, net-new: Fubon's Figure 5 has AMD at only 7.2% (2026) → 7.0% (2027) of TSMC CoWoS = 84,300 → 132,300 wafers. Against a 300k total allocation, roughly 170k wafers — well over half of AMD's 2027 packaging — sits at ASE, outside TSMC CoWoS entirely.** ⚠️ **Unit-basis guardrail recorded: 132,300 is TSMC-only CoWoS and 300k is TSMC + ASE; the two must not be netted, and neither is comparable to NVDA's 945,000 TSMC-CoWoS wafers without also noting NVDA's own 1.03mn total (TSMC + SPIL). This is the same class of basis error the UBS package-vs-die note caused across the wiki on 07-27.** **Field context that frames how good this is: in the same revision [[NVDA]] holds flat at 50.0%, [[AVGO]] is trimmed to 22.3% (from Fubon's own 24–25%), and AMD and [[MEDIATEK]] are the only two customers taking incremental 2027 allocation. Fubon also names ASE its top pick "due to its higher exposure in Vera and Venice" and is explicitly more confident in the CPU / SPE / TPU chains than in the [[NVDA]] GPU chain — i.e. the house preference sits squarely on the leg AMD is gaining in.** **One cost item logged against the ramp: HBM4 runs US$31-32/GB for NVDA but US$35-36/GB for "other GPU and custom-ASIC projects," roughly doubling HBM cost in 2027 — AMD is on the expensive side of a ~US$4/GB spread, compounding the ASE-quote (+20%) and BT-substrate (+20-30%) inflation logged on 07-27.** Cross-filed [[TSM]], [[AVGO]], [[NVDA]], [[MEDIATEK]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md).
- **2026-07-28** — **/wiki-ingest (Outlook + Twitter). 3 intra-quarter rows. No rating/PT superseded** — no AMD-specific broker action this ingest; **all three inputs are third-party evidence about AMD, which is the useful thing about them.** **(1) The rack ramp got its cleanest external confirmation yet, from the contract manufacturer's P&L rather than from AMD.** GS raised Celestica's 2027E revenue to **$39.0bn (+90% y/y)** and 2027E EPS to **$22.47 vs consensus $15.03**, explicitly on **“[[OPENAI]], AMD programs and supply availability”** — with the supply half being the non-obvious part: **component availability improved “broad-based and not linked to a single vendor or component,”** i.e. the constraint that has gated accelerator-rack ramps is easing generally. **(2) Packaging allocation moved in AMD's favour: Jefferies raises AMD's total CoWoS allocation (TSM + ASE) “significantly to 300k wafers,” majority from ASE**, and lifts ASE's end-2027E FoCoS to 50k (from 45k) for CPU/networking. In the same revision **[[NVDA]] held 50% and [[AVGO]] was TRIMMED to 22.3% (from 24.3%)** — so AMD and [[MEDIATEK]] are the only two customers taking incremental 2027 allocation. Capacity has been the binding constraint on the MI-series story; it is now loosening. ⚠️ Note the cross-current on the same page: [[SANM]]'s F3Q26 print disclosed **zero AMD Helios rack revenue in F4Q26** on revenue-recognition timing, with Helios starting F1Q27 and ZT weighted to F2H27 — **so the capacity is arriving before the recognised revenue does.** **(3) A bear challenge worth tracking rather than dismissing:** @SemiAnalysis_ argues the **OpenAI and [[META]] warrant deals are better modelled as a REBATE matching the price of the compute — “up to a 105% discount for OpenAI”** — rather than as equity sweeteners. ⚠️ **Unverified thread teaser, flagged not adopted**, but if the arithmetic holds it converts the marquee OpenAI win from evidence of pricing power into a negative-effective-price customer-acquisition cost — the same circular-financing critique currently weighing on [[NVDA]]. Tape context: AMD **-6% on 07-27**, named by Goldman's desk among the most-crowded semis longs hit hardest in the unwind. Cross-filed [[SANM]], [[OPENAI]], [[NVDA]], [[MEDIATEK]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md).
